  - **🔴 §C18 (NEW, P1) + §C14 ② — the terminology model, RE-READ WITH THE LEAD 2026-08-04. The bulk Íðorðabankinn import was FINE; what it broke is a selector.** `status` is not a provenance stamp (that is `source`) — it is the **chooser** between competing translations of one headword, and **7,601 of 20,272 headwords have more than one**. With all of them `approved`, `buildGlossaryMap`'s `Map` silently last-write-wins, so **database row order decides which Icelandic word readers see** for `atom` (*frumeind* vs *atóm*) — and `formatGlossary` sends both, contradicting itself, to the MT API. ✅ **The fix is small and per-book:** `approved` = "the term THIS book uses"; **chemistry is 124 decisions**, flipped as an editor meets them. ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-07 — the count stands, "decisions" does not.** All 124 are an Íðorðabankinn head form plus its own listed synonym, and preferring the head form (`idordabanki_id IS NOT NULL`) resolves **all 124, leaving 0**; the lead ruled it the default with a per-term override, so they are a pre-answered queue, not blockers. Measurement, derivation and the population it does *not* dissolve → **§C14 ②**. Do **§C18's determinism guard first** — it is independent of all editorial work. Also open: the export's non-zero exit (two books legitimately refuse) makes `/api/health` read `glossary_export: ok=false` **even across the run that wrote and pushed** — it cannot tell "never ran" from "ran and wrote".

Three efni tasks remain; see §C9. ⏰ **lead deadline: before fall semester.**

The **published tarball** has no install script and BUNDLES 8 prebuilt binaries incl. `linux-x64.node`; measured install is ~2s and the loaded binding is `prebuilds/linux-x64.node`, not `build/Release`. **v13 is more robust than v12 here**, not less: v12's `prebuild-install` *downloaded* the binary at install time, which is the very path a wrong-ABI binary arrives by (the 2026-05-10 incident). What remains true is that npm auto-runs node-gyp (the package ships a `binding.gyp`), so the box needs python3 + make + g++ — **confirmed present** (Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS, build-essential installed). `deploy.sh`'s two comments describing the `prebuild-install` fetch were corrected in the same pass. **Durable lesson: `npm view <pkg> scripts` is the source repo's manifest, NOT the shipped artifact — inspect the tarball (`npm pack`) before reasoning about install-time behaviour.**
- **▶ [LEAD] queue:** **① publish the C13 fix — ⏸️ HELD by lead decision 2026-07-30, do not sync.** `liffraedi-2e` ch03+ch05 are corrected and re-rendered on disk, but the whole book is now queued for re-extraction + re-MT (see [`2026-07-30-target-architecture-assessment.md`](2026-07-30-target-architecture-assessment.md)), and syncing now would publish a page the assessment records as known-bad and then immediately re-render it. **Deliver it with the post-re-MT sync instead.** *(Original scope, retained: `node scripts/sync-content.js --source ../namsbokasafn-efni` → build → deploy in vefur, 6 section pages, no filename changes; ⚠️ a clean sync exit is not proof — the duplicate warning does not fail the run.)* · **② C14 dry-run — ✅ RAN 2026-08-03; the `--force` decision is now per-book and is NOT yet made.** Numbers and analysis in **§C14 ②**. Standing position until each is settled: **`liffraedi-2e` — do not `--force`** (approved `0 → 13561`, unguarded growth, reader-visible render path; check `approved_by`/`approved_at` first) · **`lifraen-efnafraedi` — do not `--force`** (its committed glossary is a byte-identical copy of chemistry's; decide what organic's glossary should *be* first) · **`efnafraedi-2e` — write-eligible**, but it drops 408 total terms under the 0.5 threshold, so make that a decision rather than a default ⚠️ **— REVERSED 2026-08-07; do NOT adopt on this line. The 408 is a net (522 out, 0 in) and 112 of the losses are correct chemistry terms the strict subject filter discards for want of a fallback, `pH` among them → §C14 ②** · **`edlisfraedi-2e` is silently absent from the export entirely** · C11(a) sync-content deploy key *(deferred — manual sync works)* · C12 branch protection *(decided: force-push + deletion blocking only; apply when ready)* · the 2 nginx redirects · **✅ A2 off-box DB backup — DONE and restore-tested 2026-08-04** (§A2 in the LEAD operational lane has the measurements); **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-05 14:47Z — the unattended firing is CONFIRMED.** `checks.offbox_backup` = `age_hours: 2, stale: false, ok: true`, and the deploy that day explicitly **skipped** the off-box leg (`BACKUP_REMOTE not set — skipping off-box upload`), so nothing hand-triggered it. ⚠️ **Correction while closing it: the schedule is `30 */6 * * *` (00:30/06:30/12:30/18:30 UTC), not a single 00:30 daily run** — the last firing was 12:30Z, which is why the age is 2 h and not 14. ---

## Purpose

The pre-semester campaign shipped 21 items and, per the standing "log every out-of-scope find" rule, accumulated a large register of deferred/emergent findings plus a Phase-5 hygiene backlog. This campaign organizes **all outstanding work** into one prioritized picture so it can be worked down in an orderly fashion.

**Ordering principle (as requested):** each item is ranked **primarily by what it blocks** (delivery to editors/readers → biology onboarding → another finding → nothing), and **secondarily by severity** (correctness → robustness/hardening → polish). Tiers P0→P3 encode that ordering.

**A structural truth worth stating up front:** the highest-blocking items are **operational**, not code — nothing merged in items 12–21 is live to editors until the server is deployed, and no content fix reaches readers until vefur is synced. Those are **[LEAD]**-executed. The efni **[CODE]** backlog that I execute is a set of medium-severity themed batches (P1–P2) plus a long low/polish tail (P3).

## How to run

- **Per item:** brainstorm → writing-plans → subagent-driven-development, **one PR per item**. `npm test` from the repo root is the **authoritative gate** (no branch protection — a red PR can still merge, so local green is the real proof). Whole-branch adversarial review before each PR (the item-17/-21 pattern).
- **Legend:** **[CODE]** = I execute. **[LEAD]** = you execute (deploy / data-op / product decision). **[decision-gated]** = a **[CODE]** item blocked on an L7-style lead decision — resolve the decision first.
- **Register provenance:** codes like `I14-R2`, `MTA-R8`, `osd-1`, `P1-R1` are defined in the pre-semester campaign's register sections (`docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md`) and the SDD ledger. This doc consolidates and re-prioritizes them; it does not restate their full history.
- **Excluded false-positives:** the inventory sweep surfaced two register lines that are **already resolved** and are NOT in this campaign: **B4-D11** (fixed #279 + data #280 — biology term round-trip is live) and **I18-R1** (closed by item-19 #306 — tag-at-approval). Re-verify any code against its register line before starting; a line *describing* a fixed problem is not open work.
- **⚠️ This register was built from *summaries of itself*** (see Provenance below) and so **never carried the 2026-07-11 review's own findings**. The [closure audit](../audit/2026-07-26-closure-audit.md) recovered 8 of them — now **§C10**. That ledger is **frozen evidence**, not a second register. **Closing a C10 item = edit §C10 here. Do not edit the ledger.** One fact, one live home — see CLAUDE.md § *One source of truth*.

Per the official `nodejs/Release` schedule: **v22 entered MAINTENANCE 2025-10-21, EOL 2027-04-30**; **v24 has been Active LTS since 2025-10-28** and goes to maintenance **2026-10-20**; **v26 becomes Active LTS 2026-10-28** (EOL 2029-04-30). v20 died 2026-04-30.
      - **Do NOT take Node 24 as part of this fix.** A 24 move is really an **npm 10 → npm 11 lockfile migration** — the exact breakage CLAUDE.md documents (npm 11 drops optional `@emnapi/*` peer-dep entries → prod `npm ci` fails) — plus a prod-runtime bump and a native ABI rebuild (cf. the 2026-05-10 ABI incident). Node 22 is supported through the whole 2026–27 school year, and 24's Active-LTS window has <3 months left. **When a quiet post-semester window opens, go 22 → 26 and skip 24**: same npm-11 work either way, but 26 is Active LTS to Oct 2027 and supported to 2029. Note `better-sqlite3@12.11.1` already declares `20.x || 22.x || … || 26.x`, so the DB driver is not the blocker in either direction.

  - **✅ THE PATHSPEC WORKS IN PRODUCTION — CONFIRMED ON THE 22:00 TICK, 2026-08-06.** `258c32d8 auto-backup: 2026-08-06 22:00` committed **exactly the two files C3 was filed for**: `efnafraedi-2e-2026-06-16.tmx` (+2799) and `efnafraedi-2e-2026-06-23.tmx` (+2887), and **nothing else**. The ignored-match guard also held — `pipeline-output/backup.log` carries **no** `ERROR: git add failed for pathspec`. Both halves of the fix are demonstrated on prod's own deployed code, on the first unattended run.
  - **🔴 BUT THE PUSH WAS REJECTED, SO THE FILES ARE STILL NOT OFF-BOX. C3 IS NOT CLOSED.** `[2026-08-06T22:00:05Z] ERROR: git push failed (local ahead 1, behind 3)`. Prod is **ahead 1 / behind 3**: the commit exists only on the box, which is the exact exposure C3 exists to end. **This is §C11(b)'s stranded-backup mode firing again** — the cron commits, cannot push, and by design never fetches first (a rebase there would wedge prod mid-`merge.ours`; → CLAUDE.md).
    - **⚠️ Cause, recorded because it is a process lesson and not a code defect: three `docs(…)` commits were pushed to `main` from dev AFTER the deploy** (`027e338e`, `6fefbe73`, `f33934af`), which put prod 3 behind. Prod's cron then had a real commit to push for the first time in hours and was rejected. **Pushing docs to `main` while prod holds unpushed content is enough to strand a content backup** — the two are the same branch.
    - **🔴 CORRECTED 2026-08-06 22:2xZ — "one deploy releases them" WAS WRONG, and it was wrong in this file, in the dashboard, and in what I told the lead.** The deploy ran and did its half: prod is **behind 0**, service active, HEAD `de3de47e` (the auto-backup commit, rebased). **But the two TMX files are still NOT on `origin/main`** — verified by `git ls-tree -r origin/main -- books/efnafraedi-2e/tm/`, which lists only the pre-existing `2026-06-13` file. Prod remains **ahead 1**.
      - **Why: `scripts/git-backup.sh` NEVER PUSHES ON A QUIET RUN.** Its quiet-run branch checks `unpushed_backlog`, and on a non-zero backlog it logs `ERROR: unpushed backlog: N commit(s)` and **`exit 1` — before the `git push`, which is only reachable after a fresh `git commit`**. So a deploy makes the push *possible* (behind 0 ⇒ it would fast-forward) but **nothing performs it**. The commit waits for the next tick that has new content of its own.
      - **This is §C11(b)'s documented mode, firing for the FIFTH time** — *"a rejected push stays stranded until some later run happens to have content."* The register recorded four. The alarm side is correct (`write_status error`, no heartbeat ⇒ health flips not-ok after the threshold — modulo §C28's delay), but **detection is not delivery.**
      - **▶ SINGLE NEXT ACTION [LEAD], one command on the box:** `git -C ~/repos/namsbokasafn-efni push origin main`. Safe as a fast-forward now that prod is behind 0. **Then verify on GitHub, not on the box:** `git ls-tree -r --name-only origin/main -- books/efnafraedi-2e/tm/` must list the `2026-06-16` and `2026-06-23` files.
      - **⚠️ Worth fixing properly (candidate follow-up): the quiet-run branch could push its backlog instead of only reporting it.** It is behind 0 by then in the deploy case, so the push is a fast-forward and the "never fetch" prohibition is untouched — the script would be *sending what it already committed*, not reconciling. Left unlogged as its own item pending a lead call, because it changes an unattended write path.
    - *(Former text, retained as the record of the wrong call: `./scripts/deploy.sh` does the pull as part of its normal run and is the right vehicle rather than a bare `git pull` — **true, and insufficient**.)* Verify after: `git log --oneline -- 'books/*/tm/*'` **on GitHub's `main`**, not on the box. ⚠️ **Use the `books/*/tm/*` pathspec with the trailing `*`** — `git ls-files "books/*/tm/"` returns **empty even when the files are tracked**, a false negative that cost one wrong reading during this very check.
  - **⏰ SUPERSEDED — kept because the reasoning was right and the check it prescribes is what caught the push failure.** *(Former text: the proof is the next cron tick, not the deploy.)* Deploying only puts the pathspec on prod's checkout; the two June TMX files (`efnafraedi-2e-2026-06-{16,23}.tmx`) reach git when `git-backup.sh` next runs (2-hourly). **Verify with `git log --oneline -- 'books/*/tm/'` on `main`, or `git -C ~/repos/namsbokasafn-efni status --porcelain books/*/tm/` on the box** — an `auto-backup:` commit carrying those two files is the closure. ⚠️ **Do not mark C3 closed on the deploy alone: §C11(b) is the precedent** — a shipped detector that had never actually run went unnoticed for 13 days. **Watch for one specific non-event too:** the run must NOT log `ERROR: git add failed for pathspec` (that would mean the ignored-match guard did not do its job on prod's tree).
  - **🔴 FOUND WHILE FIXING IT, and it is the larger half: `git add` treats an EXPLICITLY NAMED ignored path as a hard error, and the loop was already hitting it.** `git add -- <ignored-dir>` exits **1** even though it stages every other path in the same command — so one ignored match makes `git-backup.sh` log `ERROR: git add failed`, abort with status `error`, and **skip the C11(b) heartbeat**, i.e. a test artifact manufactures a false "content backup is broken" alarm. **Not theoretical and NOT introduced by C3:** the E2E fixture book is *committed* while its four test-written output dirs are *gitignored* (`.gitignore`, "E2E fixture book" block), and **measured on this dev box `git add -- books/*/03-faithful-translation/` already exits 1** because `books/__e2e-fixture__/03-faithful-translation/` exists there. Dormant on prod only because prod never runs E2E. The loop now drops ignored matches, logging each one at `INFO`. ⚠️ **The regression test deliberately ignores a dir matching a PRE-EXISTING pathspec (`05-publication`), not the new `tm` one** — keyed to `tm` it would have passed vacuously whenever the tm pathspec was absent, pinning nothing.
  - **⚠️ A one-character trap worth keeping: `books/*/tm/` cannot be written literally in a block comment** — it ends with the two characters that close one. Another instance of CLAUDE.md's "`npm run lint` ≠ the Lint job", one level sharper: *nothing you can run under that name lints the server at all.*

Vefur deliberately never picks a winner between two `mt-preview` files: they are different human-visible translations, and `mtime`/git order are not content properties. A conflict does **not** change the sync's exit code (it is a content defect here, and failing the sync would block a deploy over something vefur cannot fix).

Our prune-on-rename is therefore the *only* place that information still exists — a prune that merely unlinks destroys it permanently and 404s every inbound link to the old URL. **Task 1 must EMIT the map alongside the rendered output, not just delete.** (Same underlying need as the 2 nginx redirects already in the [LEAD] queue, and as the two `liffraedi-2e` ch03 renames already queued on the vefur side.)

  - **Mechanism (read in vefur, confirmed — not inferred):**
    1. `scripts/sync-content.js:12,139-141` — `mt-preview` is mirrored **with `--delete`**, then `faithful` is copied on top **WITHOUT `--delete`** (deliberate: a partial overlay must never wipe baseline chapters).
    2. Renaming means the two tracks emit *different filenames*, so the overlay **adds** rather than **replaces**: the destination ends up with both `3-3-fitusyrur.html` (baseline) and `3-3-lipid.html` (overlay).
    3. `scripts/generate-toc.js:499` enumerates `readdirSync(chapterPath)` over the **synced destination** (`static/content/<book>/chapters/<NN>/`), so **both files become TOC entries**.
    4. `generate-toc.js:266-279` `isReviewedModule` tests `existsSync(faithful/chapters/<ch>/<contentFile>)` — again **by filename** — so the new file gets `reviewed: true` (no MT banner) and the stale one keeps `reviewed: false` (MT banner).
  - **Reader-visible result:** the chapter TOC shows **two entries for the same section** — one under the corrected title with no banner, one under the old mistranslated title still flagged machine-translated. The stale page stays reachable and indexable.

One renamed title makes a fully-reviewed chapter read as incomplete, so its rollups (summary / key-terms / key-equations / exercises / answer-key) **never switch to faithful and the MT banner never clears**. No duplicate, no error, no warning — a permanently stuck banner. Easier to miss than the duplicate.

- **C10 · Closure-audit resurrections** — **[CODE]** — findings from the **2026-07-11 review** that this register never carried. Source: [`docs/audit/2026-07-26-closure-audit.md`](../audit/2026-07-26-closure-audit.md) (ledger) + [`-evidence.md`](../audit/2026-07-26-closure-audit-evidence.md) (quoted code for every claim).

So the registers track findings discovered *during* items, never the findings the campaign was created to fix. Severity annotations did not survive summarization. **Nobody deprioritised these; they evaporated.**
  - **Audit result overall: on target.** All four High/Medium authz findings closed; code batches A–E and G at 100%. Most of the tail is open-but-tracked; the untracked remainder is R1–R8 below (R3 withdrawn on blind re-verification). **Disposition counts are not repeated here — they live in the frozen ledger's §1 table, which is authoritative.**
  - **⚠️ The pattern worth acting on:** every dimension whose gap was **code** closed or improved. **Joint batches 3 (docs authority → item 23) and 9 (dependency/dead-code → item 22) are both 0%** — not one of the four named docs edited since 2026-07-11. *The campaign shipped code and did not ship docs.* Both live in P3 today; the review ranked docs-authority **rec #1 of 6, "cheapest fix, widest blast radius."*** Consider promoting.
  - **⚠️ C10 was BLIND-REPLICATED and ADJUDICATED 2026-07-26** — a second run of the identical audit under **Fable 5**, then 3 blind adjudicators per disputed claim. Score **3–3**; **7 corrections applied to the ledger, including one C10 item withdrawn as a false positive.** Study: [`docs/audit/2026-07-26-closure-audit-model-comparison.md`](../audit/2026-07-26-closure-audit-model-comparison.md). **Items below are the corrected set.**
  - **✅ R1 (P1) · `code-ref-3` — FIXED 2026-07-27** (branch `fix/c10-closure-audit-resurrections`). Fix is **ordering, not compensation**: every DB mutation now runs *above* the file write, so `saveModuleSegments` is the last mutation before COMMIT (`segmentEditorService.js`, steps renumbered 5 = mark applied/superseded, 6 = acceptance lifecycle, 7 = write, 7b = write-verification). The step-4b `content_versions` snapshot therefore commits or rolls back **together with** the file's advance, which closes the *rollback* route to the register's named harm (pre-apply content invisible to "Saga útgáfa"). **It does not close the OTHER route to the same symptom:** `snapshotModule` is wrapped in a deliberate best-effort try/catch (`:1023-1025`), so a snapshot that *throws* is logged and the apply continues — file advanced, no version row. That is `code-ref-4`, which the audit re-confirmed as **refuted / deliberate design**, not this row; it is named here only so nobody reads "R1 is closed" as "the version row is now guaranteed." A `.bak` compensator was **considered and rejected**: the `.bak` is timestamped to the second (`segmentParser.js:199`, `toISOString()` truncated to 19 chars), so picking "the right one" is a heuristic over a directory listing; the path is already self-healing via the `applied_at` retry gate, which a compensator would fight; and the localized track's house idiom (`localizationReviewService.js:237-238`) says outright that the write is the one step SQLite cannot roll back. **⚠️ Do not append DB work below the write** — that is precisely how item 20b widened the window; there is now a comment saying so at the site. **Honest residue (not zero):** a COMMIT failure after a successful write still diverges, and the `written.length === 0` branch is the one case where the file changed while the DB rolls back — both benign, since the next apply rewrites identical content. Tests: `server/__tests__/acceptanceApply.test.js` § "apply transaction: DB work precedes the file write" — written first and red on `main`; the re-apply case demonstrated the published file advancing while the DB rolled back.
  - **~~▶ R1 (P1) · `code-ref-3`~~ — original finding text, retained as the rationale:** Adjudicated **2–1 in favour of reopening**. A throw in the post-write, in-transaction work (`markApplied`/`markSuperseded` at `segmentEditorService.js:1059-1071`, then item-20b's `acceptanceService.lapseDrifted`/`stampApplied` at `:1077-1078`) rolls the DB back with `fs.renameSync` already landed and no file-side unwind. **Residue is file/DB divergence, not corruption** — and its sharpest form is that the step-4b `contentVersionService.snapshotModule` row (`:1015`) rolls back *after* the file advanced, so the pre-apply content never enters version history and is invisible to "Saga útgáfa" (recoverable only from the `.bak` by hand). **⚠️ NARRATIVE CORRECTED:** the July refutation's leg (b) was **already false at the 2026-07-11 baseline** — the mark loops were post-write and in-transaction then too. Item-20b **widened an existing window, it did not open one**; do not repeat the "invalidated by later code" story. **Do not re-refute** — leg (b) was never true. Fits alongside **C6**.
  - **✅ R2 (P1) · `ed-drift-2b` — FIXED 2026-07-27** (same branch). Resolved as **"make it non-sequential"**, not "wire an advance". The discriminator: `grep -n "tm/\|generate-tm\|tmx" tools/cnxml-inject.js` → **0 hits** — injection never reads `tm/`, so TM generation is not causally a prerequisite. "Wire an advance" was rejected on three grounds: the producer is **book-level** (`tmService` → `generate-tm.js --book <book>`, no `--chapter`) so there is no per-chapter completion event; `regenerateTm` **deliberately never throws**, so hanging status off it re-creates a silent-failure channel; and a missing licence row makes the regen **silently warn-only stale** (I21-R2). New `NON_SEQUENTIAL_STAGES` in `pipelineStatusService.js` — the prerequisite walk steps over it (`injection`'s gate is now `linguisticReview`) and `currentStage` skips it (it used to pin there forever). **`tmCreated` deliberately stays** in `STAGE_ORDER`/`ALL_STAGES`/`BASE_STAGES`/the `stages` response/`status.json`/the schema, and can still be completed explicitly. Schema description at `chapter-status.schema.json`'s `tmCreated` description corrected off the retired "via Matecat Align" in passing. Tests: `server/__tests__/pipelineStatus.test.js` § "non-sequential stages (tmCreated)" — written first and red on `main`, with the pre-existing loop-based test that hand-completes `tmCreated` left untouched as proof explicit completion still works.
    - **⚠️ Intended behaviour change to name in review: `tmCreated` is now fully INERT through the HTTP API — neither advance nor revert names it.** `POST /api/pipeline-status/:book/:chapter/advance` completes whatever `getChapterStage` reports as `currentStage`, and it is the **only** route that transitions a base stage (there is no explicit-stage endpoint), so a head editor stepping a chapter forward now goes `linguisticReview → injection` in one click. `revertStage` walks the same sequential chain, so a revert can no longer land on it either. `transitionStage` still *accepts* the stage — `ALL_STAGES` is unchanged and a test pins it — but no route passes it, so in practice the stage is set by nothing. That matches its real status as a vestigial field, and it is a correctness gain as well as a convenience one: the old advance click asserted "TM created" for a chapter no TM had been generated for.
      - **`revertStage` was NOT part of the original design — the adversarial review caught it.** Revert must stay the inverse of advance; leaving the reverse scan on the full list meant a revert could land on a stage `/advance` can never restore. The first cut justified skipping it with "a never-complete stage is never the revert target" — **falsified by real data**: `books/efnafraedi-2e/chapters/ch03`+`ch04/status.json` carry a Matecat-era `tmCreated: complete`. **Durable rule: advance and revert must read the same stage list, or revert stops being an inverse.**
    - **⚠️ The SECOND read model had to be fixed too — found by the branch's adversarial review, not by the audit.** `routes/status.js` runs an independent status.json-shaped read model (`formatChapterStatus` + `suggestNextActions`) that sequenced over the **full** stage list. Fixing only the DB model made the two contradict each other: a fully published chapter would report every real stage complete while `nextStage` still said `tmCreated`, `progress` was capped at 6/7 forever, and `suggestNextActions` told editors to *"Create TM via Matecat Align"* — a retired tool. **Not cosmetic:** `nextStage` drives the dashboard's assignment proposals and the meeting agenda's "Næsta skref" line. Resolved by giving the list **one owner** — `NON_SEQUENTIAL_STAGES` now lives in `server/constants.js` and both read models consume it. **⚠️ Durable rule: this repo has two status read models; a change to stage sequencing must be applied to both, or they silently disagree.** Test: `server/__tests__/nonSequentialStages.test.js`, mutation-checked.
    - **⚠️ Correction to an inherited claim — the frozen ledger's "can only ever demote" does NOT generalise.** It is true of `bookRegistration.js:1043` (`STATUS_RULES.tmCreated` keys on `tm/chNN/<section>.tmx`, which `generate-tm.js` never writes). It is **false** of `routes/status.js`'s `/scan` route, which added a `hasBookTmx` check for the book-level `tm/<book>-<date>.tmx` layout the generator actually produces and therefore **does** promote. Do not restate the general form.
  - **~~▶ R2 (P1) · `ed-drift-2b`~~ — original finding text, retained as the rationale:** ⚠️ **Audit escalates this from an unrated trailing clause.** Nothing advances `tmCreated`; `pipelineStatusService.transitionStage:161-172` enforces a sequential prerequisite with `BASE_STAGES[4]='tmCreated'`, `[5]='injection'` → **every `advanceChapterStatus(…,'injection')` throws**, swallowed at `pipelineService.js:735-746`. The suite hand-completes `tmCreated` in a loop to get past it (`__tests__/pipelineStatus.test.js:105-117`). Fix = wire an advance from the TM path, or drop `tmCreated` from `BASE_STAGES`.
  - ~~**R3 (P2) · `ed-w6b`** — raw service errors reach a head-editor via `alert()`.~~ ❌ **WITHDRAWN 2026-07-26 — FALSE POSITIVE.** Two independent verifiers (2/2, high confidence) found the asserted defect **not present**: migration 039 rebuilt `segment_edits` and dropped the UNIQUE constraint that produced the raw message, so no such `SqliteError` can arise in `markForDiscussion`. The relay plumbing exists but delivers nothing — a latent-robustness note, not a finding. **The blind Fable run produced no row here and was right to. Do not implement this.**
  - **✅ R4 (P2) · `ed-w8` — FIXED 2026-07-27** (same branch). New `segmentEditorService.countDiscussEdits()` (a real `SELECT COUNT(*)`); the route uses it for the stat and `getDiscussEdits(5)` for the deliberately-paged items list. - **C11 · 🔴 Content-delivery path has no smoke detector, and its automatic leg is DEAD** — **[LEAD] + [CODE]** — found 2026-07-26 while assessing branch protection. Three findings that compound; **treat as one problem.**
  - **(a) 🔴 `Sync Content to Vefur` has NEVER worked — 34 runs, 34 failures, zero successes since 2026-06-16.** ⏸ **DEFERRED 2026-07-26 by lead decision** (manual `node scripts/sync-content.js` remains the route; it works). Failing step is **`Checkout vefur (web reader)`** (`.github/workflows/sync-content.yml:33-38`).
    - **⚠️ Root cause corrected — there is nothing to "rotate".** `gh secret list` returns **zero repository secrets**: `VEFUR_DEPLOY_TOKEN` has **never existed**, so `actions/checkout` receives an empty string. An earlier note in this register guessed "missing or expired" and then narrowed to "rotate the token" — wrong. **Memory's original "Sync Action unconfigured" was right.** This is first-time setup, not credential rotation.
    - **The credential needs WRITE access to `namsbokasafn-vefur`** — the workflow does not merely read it, it `git commit`s and `git push`es (`:78`, `:82`).
    - **▶ Recommended shape when picked up: a DEPLOY KEY, not a PAT.** vefur currently has **0 deploy keys**. A write-enabled deploy key is scoped to exactly one repository and **never expires**; a fine-grained PAT expires and — because of **(b)** — would fail *silently*, re-creating this outage on a hidden timer. Cost is one workflow edit (`token:` → `ssh-key:`, secret renamed `VEFUR_DEPLOY_KEY`). ⚠️ The deploy key **must** be added with *"Allow write access"* ticked, or checkout succeeds and the **push** fails — which presents as a different bug. Test via the workflow's existing `workflow_dispatch` (`-f book=all`); no synthetic commit needed.
    - ⚠️ **Never paste the key into a chat transcript.** Route it `gh secret set VEFUR_DEPLOY_KEY < keyfile`, then delete the local file. *(Precedent: the sister repo published two live Cloudflare tokens for ~5 months.)*
  - **(b) ✅ FIXED 2026-07-27 — a failed content push is now visible.** `scripts/git-backup.sh` writes `pipeline-output/.last-content-backup` on its two healthy terminal paths (a successful push, and nothing-to-commit **with an empty unpushed backlog**) and never on a failure, so **staleness is the alarm**. ⚠️ **The backlog condition is load-bearing, not a refinement:** content changes are far sparser than the 2-hourly cron, so most runs take the nothing-to-commit path — without it, a rejected push would be papered over two hours later, before the 6 h threshold could ever fire, and the non-fast-forward case (this register's primary detection target) would essentially never alarm. The check is `git rev-list --count origin/main..HEAD`, network-free because a successful push updates the remote-tracking ref; an indeterminate count fails loud — the inversion the off-box DB backup already used. `GET /api/health` gained `checks.content_backup` (`server/lib/contentBackupHealth.js`, threshold `CONTENT_BACKUP_STALE_HOURS`, default 6h) which gates `allOk`, and `scripts/deploy.sh` now **prints** the health verdict it used to discard — nothing else polls that endpoint, so this is where a human sees it, and the readout names the not-ok checks so a stale content backup is distinguishable from the ambient off-box `degraded`. Push failures also log ahead/behind counts, separating "GitHub unreachable" from "prod has diverged". **A rebase was deliberately NOT added to the cron:** `merge.ours.driver` is registered by `deploy.sh`, not by cron, so an unattended rebase over `translation-errors.json` would convert a visible push failure into a repo wedged mid-rebase on production. *Original finding:* `scripts/git-backup.sh` detected it correctly (`set -euo pipefail` at `:26`, so the `| tee` pipeline propagates) and writes `write_status "error" "git push failed"` at `:123` — into `pipeline-output/backup-status.json`, which is **gitignored and read by nothing**, with no `MAILTO` in `install-cron.sh`. Editors keep working, prod accumulates unpushed commits, nobody learns. **Note the cron never fetches before pushing, so non-fast-forward rejection was ALREADY a live silent failure mode** — that is the case the ahead/behind diagnostic now names. **Explicitly deferred once** at `docs/plans/2026-07-12-git-backup-atomic-add-design.md:68` — frozen evidence, correctly recording the decision made then; it was picked up here.
  - **(c) ✅ FIXED 2026-07-27 — `deploy.yml` no longer owns any deploy logic.** ⚠️ **Premise corrected:** the earlier note said to fix this "before the next `deploy.yml` run". **There has never been a run and there cannot be one** — `gh run list --workflow=deploy.yml` → `[]`, `gh secret list` → empty, and the `production` environment does not exist, so the SSH action's credentials are empty strings and the hard reset at `:50` was unreachable. Two further signs of abandonment: it restarted `namsbokasafn-efni` (live unit is `ritstjorn`, swallowed by `|| true`) and defaulted `DEPLOY_APP_DIR` to `/opt/...` (prod is `/home/siggi/repos/...`). **Lead decision 2026-07-27: collapse, not harden** — the workflow now just calls `scripts/deploy.sh`, which deletes the hard reset and inherits the DB backup (+30-file rotation), the Node/ABI pin, stash-and-reapply, both `npm ci --omit=dev` installs, the correct unit restart, and the health gate. Hardening in place would have invested in a divergent second deploy path, and *two deploy scripts that disagree* was itself the defect. Pinned by `tools/__tests__/deployPathSingleSource.test.js`.
  - **(d) ✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-03 12:00 — but read how, because it resolved itself by accident.** *Raised from the A4 runbook's L3 output, verified read-only the same day, and drained the same afternoon without anyone acting on it.* **Original finding: a content backup had been stranded on production for 13 days, and (b)'s alarm had never actually run.**
    - **▶ HOW IT DRAINED.** The 12:00 cron run had something to commit (the glossary files the export had just written — **§C14 ②**), so it took the commit-then-push path, and that push was a clean fast-forward `8ce45510..4fbbfa5f` **carrying the 13-day-old stranded commit with it**. Prod is now **ahead 0 / behind 0**, the heartbeat exists, and `/api/health` reads `content_backup: ok`. ⚠️ **Nobody fixed this. It was flushed as a side effect of the incident in §C14 ②** — the manual `git push` that had been prepared was never needed. *Worth keeping: a backlog that only drains when the producer happens to commit is not self-healing, it is waiting for a coincidence.*
    - **⚠️ THE UNDERLYING GAP IS NOT FIXED and will recur.** The cron pushes only on the path where it has just committed (`git-backup.sh:223`); the backlog check added by (b) makes a stranded commit **visible** but nothing makes it **drain**. Root cause is unchanged and confirmed from `backup.log`: `! [rejected] main -> main (fetch first)` — the cron deliberately never fetches, so any dev push between prod's last pull and the cron's next commit rejects it. It has fired **four** times: 2026-06-12, 06-13, 06-23, 07-21. **Next time there may be no convenient coincidence.**
    - **The original measurement (historical).** Prod was **ahead 1, behind 0** of `origin/main`. The stranded commit is `auto-backup: 2026-07-21 14:00`. `pipeline-output/.last-content-backup` **does not exist**, and `GET /api/health` reports `content_backup: {age_hours: null, stale: true, ok: false}` → overall **`degraded`**.
    - **⚠️ Why nobody could find it in the dev repo: the hash changes on every deploy.** Its author date is `2026-07-21T14:00:02Z` but its committer date is `2026-08-03T10:41:26Z`, and `git reflog` shows it being re-picked by `pull --rebase` on **2026-07-26** and again **2026-08-03**. Each rebase mints a new hash for the same content. **So "search dev's history for that sha" is structurally guaranteed to fail, and its age is invisible from `git log --oneline`** — only the author-vs-committer date gap reveals it. *This is the diagnostic to reach for; a hash lookup is not.*
    - **⚠️ THE REAL FINDING: (b) shipped 2026-07-27, but prod's previous pull was 2026-07-26 — so C11(b)'s code only reached production on 2026-08-03 at 10:41.** The 10:00 cron run that morning executed the **pre-(b)** script, which is why `backup-status.json` reads `no_changes / "Nothing to commit"` despite a backlog of 1, and why no heartbeat was ever written. **The current (b) code is correct and would have refused** — its no-changes branch checks the backlog *first* and `exit 1`s with `unpushed backlog: N commit(s)` before reaching `write_heartbeat`. **(b)'s first genuine execution on production is the next cron tick.** Watch that one.
      - **Evidence is at file level, not inferred from dates.** Prod's 2026-07-26 pull based on `9107ed1d`; `git show 9107ed1d:scripts/git-backup.sh` has **0** occurrences of `unpushed backlog` and **0** of `last-content-backup`. Today's tree (`8ce45510`, pulled 10:41) has **4** and **2**. `2977aed0` (C11(b)) is confirmed **not** an ancestor of `9107ed1d`.
      - **⚠️ Method note, because the first attempt at that check was VACUOUS and passed.** I first ran `git merge-base --is-ancestor 2977aed0 2c8da016` against prod's *previous* HEAD — but `2c8da016` is a prod-only rebased hash that does not exist in the dev repo, so the command exited non-zero because **the ref was unknown**, not because the ancestor test failed. It printed the answer I expected. The two accompanying `git show 2c8da016:…` greps returned `0` for the same reason. **A ref that does not resolve makes an ancestry check and a `grep -c` both fail *open*, looking like a clean negative result.** Verify against a ref you have confirmed resolves — and note this is the same class as `[[engineering-lessons]]`'s vacuous-pass entries, met on prod-only hashes rather than in a test.
    - **Why it never self-healed:** the cron only pushes on the path where it has just committed (`git-backup.sh` `:223`). Once the 2026-07-21 push failed, every later run found nothing to commit and therefore **never retried the push** — the backlog check makes that *visible*, but nothing makes it *drain*. ⚠️ Do not "fix" this by adding a fetch/rebase to the cron; (b) and CLAUDE.md both explain why. Draining it is a **[LEAD]** action from an attended shell.
    - **Not a credential or connectivity problem.** `git ls-remote origin` succeeds from prod over SSH (`git@github.com:…`), and `origin/main` is current at `8ce45510`. The push simply is not being attempted.
    - **Consequence for A2/L5.** This register and CLAUDE.md both say the git remote is the only off-box copy of editors' reviewed translations. With a stranded push there is **no current off-box copy at all** — **A2's urgency goes up, not down.**
    - **Riders.** The commit's content is four `.locked` MT-lock markers (`m68667`, `m68674`, `ch05/chapter-metadata`, `liffraedi-2e m66443`) — **exactly the modules §C16's ANSWERED block identified**, which independently resolves the ambiguity §C16 flagged as unresolvable from the repo: prod's 2026-07-21 `backfill-mt-locks --db` run *did* add markers, and they have been stranded ever since. It also qualifies the A4 runbook's *"git is the real index"* premise — part of git lives only on prod until this lands. *[severity: correctness/durability · blocks: A2 off-box backup framing]*
  - **⚠️ Sequencing:** (b) is the prerequisite for *any* control that could reject a push to `main` — adding one to a channel with no smoke detector is the worst available ordering. **Discharged 2026-07-27:** the detector exists, so C12 branch protection is no longer blocked by it. *[severity: correctness/durability · blocks: reader delivery (a is live now)]*
  - **Considered and NOT built (logged so they are not re-raised as oversights):** a scheduled watchdog Action reading the age of the newest `auto-backup:` commit on `main` (out-of-band, but false-alarms whenever there is genuinely nothing to commit); `MAILTO` on the cron (needs a working MTA on the box — **[LEAD]**); an admin health widget in the editorial UI. The heartbeat plus the `deploy.sh` readout is the whole detector. Also considered and rejected: escaping `$message` in `write_status` — no call site can emit a quote, and the *consumer* tolerates malformed JSON and is tested for it.

- **C13 · `<figure>`-in-`<para>`-in-`<note>` inject bug — reader-visible** — **[CODE]** — **P1** — **✅ MERGED 2026-07-27 — PR [#337](https://github.com/SigurdurVilhelmsson/namsbokasafn-efni/pull/337), main `950717c0`** (branch `fix/c13-figure-in-note-inject`, deleted on merge; 9 commits `184a0da6` red tests → `419385e6` fix → `a9052170` re-render → `d045b795`/`d572a989` review fixes → `8ad2843c` DOM re-count). Schema gate on `liffraedi-2e`: **6 errors in 5 files → 0 across all 11 modules**; fidelity discrepancies **9 → 1**. Local suite **3456 green** (251 files) — the authoritative gate — and **all 6 CI checks passed** (`lint`, `test`, `e2e`, `Validate Status Files`, `check-docs`, `audit`; 46s–2m35s, so real results, not the ~3s infra-failure signature). ⚠️ **Reader delivery still needs a vefur sync — see the riders below.**
  - **⚠️ MECHANISM — the original wording was the symptom, not the cause; corrected in place 2026-07-27 per CLAUDE.md § *One source of truth*.** It read *"inject emits the `<figure>` start tag after its `<media>`"*. The actual chain is three links:
    1. **Extraction** hoists a figure sitting in a `<para>` inside a `<note>` into a **top-level structure entry**, and flattens its `<media>` into the para's segment as `[[MEDIA:N]]` — **plus, when the figure is captioned, the caption prose**. The para segment is therefore *entirely figure-derived*.
    2. **`buildNoteDom`** lacked the per-para figure pre-scan its siblings have, so `replaceParaContentDom` inserted the expanded `<media>` **before** the block-preserved `<figure>`.
    3. **`deduplicateMedia`** (document level, `buildCnxml`) keeps the **first** occurrence of a media id — the orphan — and **deleted the figure's own copy**. *This third link is what turns a duplication bug into a destruction bug*, and it is why the fix had to remove the orphan rather than reorder anything.
  - **Reader-visible before-state was worse than "orphaned image":** a **duplicated paragraph** (the caption prose as body text), an **unlabelled image**, and an **image-less `<figure>`** that the preceding `<link target-id>` cross-reference pointed at (`Mynd 3.5` resolving to a caption with no picture).
  - **Census (all books, `01-source`) — one shape, which is what made the fix safe:** **71** occurrences (`liffraedi-2e` 70, `edlisfraedi-2e` 1), **every one** exactly one captioned figure with no other element and no other text in the para; **0** media without an `id`; **0** nested inside `<example>`/`<exercise>` (so `buildNoteDom`'s null-return path was never implicated). **6 were live** (injected): `liffraedi-2e` m66440 `fig-ch03_02_02`, m66442 `fig-ch03_04_02`, m66443 `fig-ch03_05_03`, m66374 `fig-ch05_02_06`, m66375 `fig-ch05_03_01` + `fig-ch05_03_04` — matching the gate's 6 errors **1:1**. The `edlisfraedi-2e` case is **latent** (that module is not injected yet).
    - ⚠️ **Counting unit and predicate, because a different one gives a different number.** Counted **per figure**, DOM-parsed: a `<figure>` whose **direct parent is a `<para>`** that has a `<note>` ancestor and no `<example>`/`<exercise>` ancestor. Counting per *para* instead gives **70**, because m66375 holds two such figures in one module — and per-figure is the right unit, since it is the one that matches the gate 6:6. Regex counting over this shape is unreliable (it is what produced the two wrong follow-up counts corrected below); re-derive with a DOM walk.
  - **The fix:** a `parasWithFigures` pre-scan in `buildNoteDom` + a new `paraContainsOnlyFigures()` predicate. ⚠️ **The predicate inspects the DOM parsed from `originalCnxml` — which inject loads from the READ-ONLY `books/<book>/01-source/` copy — on purpose.** (It does not itself name that path; the provenance is the `originalPath` join in `main()`.) The tempting alternative — strip the media, then drop leftover text if it equals the caption — compares **two independently editable translations**; it passes every test written today and silently stops matching the first time an editor revises either segment. `01-source` cannot drift.
  - **Second defect fixed as a consequence:** kept figure ids are now registered in `ctx.figuresHandledInNotes` **unconditionally**. The caption loop's `add()` sat *inside* its `if (captionSegId)` branch, so an **uncaptioned** note-para figure was never registered and `buildFigure` emitted a standalone duplicate. Latent today (**0** injected), but **32** such figures exist as direct note children corpus-wide — see the follow-up below.
  - **▶ ORACLE:** the jing/RelaxNG gate from #333 (`experiments/cnxml-validation-gate/`). ⚠️ `jing -i` is mandatory and jing **aborts the batch after the first `fatal:`** — see [[cnxml-schema-validation-gate]].
  - **▶ RIDERS (not done here):** re-render **is** done (ch03+ch05 mt-preview, **no filename changes → not a C9 rename**), so the remaining step is **[LEAD]**: `node scripts/sync-content.js --source ../namsbokasafn-efni` in vefur → build → deploy. Verify by fetching `/content/liffraedi-2e/chapters/03/3-2-kolvetni.html`, **never a page URL** (SPA fallback 200s everything).
  - **C13 follow-ups logged (all out-of-scope, none blocking):**
    1. **🟠 Kept-in-container figures bypass `buildFigure`'s image localization.** `resolveTranslatedImage` is applied only inside `buildFigure`/`buildMedia`; a figure preserved verbatim from `originalCnxml` (note *or* example/exercise path) never gets the swap. **3 of the 6 affected biology figures have a registered Icelandic image that is not being served** — `fig-ch03_02_02` → `Figure_03_02_02_is.jpg`, `fig-ch03_04_02` → `Figure_03_04_02_is.png`, `fig-ch03_05_03` → `Figure_03_05_03_is.jpg` (all in `books/liffraedi-2e/media/image-mapping.json`). **Not a regression** — the pre-fix orphan `<media>` was built from `inlineMedia` metadata, which carries no figure id, so it was not swapped either. Reader-visible: English artwork where Icelandic exists. `efnafraedi-2e` has 691 mapping entries, so its example/exercise kept figures are exposed to the same gap.
    2. **🟠 A CAPTIONED figure in a `<para>` inside an `<example>`/`<exercise>` leaks its caption prose into the body AND keeps its figure caption in English.** Live example: `efnafraedi-2e` m68764 (ch10, published) — `<para id="fs-idm164104512">` renders `… (heimild: Cory Zanker)` as body text while `<figure id="CNX_Chem_10_02_Needlefloa">` still carries `<caption>(credit: Cory Zanker)</caption>`. The `buildExerciseDom` pre-scan strips the media (so no orphan, and the schema gate stays green — **schema validity ⊥ fidelity**) but it neither drops the figure-derived caption prose nor translates the kept figure's caption, which only `buildNoteDom` does. `paraContainsOnlyFigures` does not apply — that para has real prose, so the fix needs the *caption-translation* half, not the figure-only half. ⚠️ **Corrected 2026-07-27 by the whole-branch review — the first count was wrong because the census script was silently scoped to `books/efnafraedi-2e/`.** True corpus count: **48** figure-in-para-in-container cases (`edlisfraedi-2e` 37, `lifraen-efnafraedi` 10, `efnafraedi-2e` 1), of which **23 carry a non-empty `<caption>`** (`edlisfraedi-2e` 22 + m68764). **Live today: exactly 1** (m68764) — only 9 edlisfraedi modules are injected and its one injected case is uncaptioned. **So onboarding `edlisfraedi-2e` turns 22 latent leaks live**; do this before that book ships. ⚠️ **These figures are DOM-counted** (`figure` whose parent is `para` with an `example`/`exercise` ancestor; caption = a direct non-empty `<caption>` child). A regex count over the same shape gives **24** because `edlisfraedi-2e` m42312 `fs-id1373551` has an **empty** caption — which has no prose to leak, so it is excluded here. An earlier "21 captioned / 20 latent" in this entry was a miscount from the regex pass and is superseded.
    3. **🟡 32 caption-less `<figure>` elements as *direct* note children are not registered in `figuresHandledInNotes`** (the same `if (captionSegId)` scoping, on the path C13 did not touch) → `buildFigure` would emit a standalone duplicate. **Latent: 0 of them are in an injected module.** ⚠️ **Corrected 2026-07-27 by the whole-branch review:** they are **`orverufraedi` 29 + `lifraen-efnafraedi` 3**, not "all in `lifraen-efnafraedi`" — that first attribution generalised from the three sampled examples the census happened to print. `orverufraedi` already has 10 injected modules, so re-check exposure whenever its coverage grows. Fix alongside follow-up 2 if that path is opened.
    4. **🟡 Every fixed para now renders as an empty `<p id="…"></p>`.** `renderBlockChildrenInOrder` hoists a para's block children out and emits the `<p>` regardless of whether anything remains. **Pre-existing render behaviour, not new** — 6 such empty paragraphs already sit in published `efnafraedi-2e` (5) and `edlisfraedi-2e` (1) output; C13 adds 6 more. Cosmetic (a stray margin inside the note aside). A render-side guard would also change those 6 pre-existing sites and force a re-render of two more books, which is why it was left out of this PR.
    5. **🟢 `node tools/cnxml-render.js --help` crashes** (`TypeError: The "path" argument must be of type string. Received null` — `book-rendering-config.js:71` via `getBookRenderConfig`). `--book <slug> --help` works. Pre-existing, trivial.
    6. **🟢 `edlisfraedi-2e` has 10 pre-existing schema-gate errors in 3 of its 9 injected files**, including `duplicate id` — unrelated to C13, never triaged. `lifraen-efnafraedi` and `orverufraedi` are clean; `efnafraedi-2e` is 153/153.
  - **Whole-branch adversarial review (5 opus lenses → dedup → 3-vote refute-default, 38 agents):** 11 raw findings → **3 real defects in MY fix, all fixed on-branch before the verify phase closed** (which is why the verifiers then refuted them as *stale* — read the raw findings, not the verdict): (a) the new branch swallowed the pre-existing `if (!paraText) continue`, so an untranslated para with prose **and** a figure would be blanked instead of keeping its English — a fail-open→content-loss flip; (b) it bypassed `paraHasFlattenedList` (audit #33), duplicating flattened list-item text; (c) **two register counts were wrong**, both from regex censuses — see the ⚠️ notes in follow-ups 2 and 3. Each is now pinned by a test. Suite **3456 green** (251 files). ⚠️ **Corpus counts in this entry are DOM-derived; do not re-derive them with a regex.**

That is the evaporation pattern recurring one day after the audit that named it. *[severity: correctness · reader-visible]*

- **✅ C14 · The glossary bridge — SHIPPED 2026-07-27, [PR #343](https://github.com/SigurdurVilhelmsson/namsbokasafn-efni/pull/343) MERGED, main `b09ede00`** (branch `fix/c14-glossary-export-runner`) — **[CODE]** — the corrected premise was still too kind: the bridge had **no caller AND no delivery path**. Its own header claimed the 2h git-backup staged `books/`; `git-backup.sh` had no `books/*/glossary/` pathspec, so a scheduled run would have written to prod's disk and never reached the dev checkout where `api-translate.js` primes MT. Shipped: `formatGlossary` drops entries whose EN or IS side is blank or not a string, reporting the count via an `onSkipped` callback — never on the return value, which is the outbound Málstaður request body verbatim; `loadGlossary` invokes that callback outside its catch-all and before its empty-check, with `glossaryStatusLine` extracted so the total-drop case is testable; `server/lib/glossaryExportDecision.js` holds pure write-if-changed and shrink-guard rules; `export-terminology.js` gained `runGlossaryExport`, a heartbeat, an exit-code contract, and a `--book` glossary-directory guard; `checks.glossary_export` in `GET /api/health`; and `scripts/git-backup.sh` now invokes the export **contained**, wrapped in a `timeout 120` (a failure *or a hang* logs a WARN and the backup proceeds — a hang is the one failure mode a bare `if ! node …` cannot catch, and it would otherwise block the script before the heartbeat is written, the exact outcome the containment exists to prevent) and stages `books/*/glossary/`. **Verified by mutation:** removing the containment from `git-backup.sh` turned **13 of 15** scripts tests red — including every C11(b) content-backup heartbeat test — because under `set -euo pipefail` an uncontained export failure aborts the run before the heartbeat is written; reverted, green. **Also found and fixed on the way:** `readExisting` originally swallowed every read error into `null`; a `null` baseline tells the shrink guard there is nothing to lose, so an `EACCES` would have stood the guard down on the very file it protects, overwritten it, and still written the heartbeat — `/api/health` would have stayed green through a silent overwrite. Now only `ENOENT` and a parse failure return `null`; verified by a `chmod 000` export holding 617 approved terms being left intact.
  - **⚠️ THE FINDING THAT MATTERS: there are TWO producers of `glossary-unified.json`.** Every committed copy was written by `tools/merge-glossary.js`, not by the exporter — so cron-ing it **swaps producers**, it does not refresh. `efnafraedi-2e` holds **1117 terms / 617 approved** (605 `chemistry-society-csv`); migration 032 **dropped** the `terminology_terms` table `merge-glossary.js:533` still writes to (error swallowed at `:727`); and `exportBookGlossary` is deliberately subject-strict (item 18). So the DB export can be far smaller, and nobody can know how much smaller without querying prod. **The first prod run is EXPECTED to refuse** and hold the merge-glossary files. That is the design working.
  - **▶ [LEAD] rider:** run `node server/scripts/export-terminology.js --dry-run` on prod, read the real approved-term counts, and only then decide per book whether to `--force`. **Do not `--force` before reading the numbers.**
  - **A durable lesson:** the same stale-status-claim defect recurred **four times** in this work — the exporter's header plus three comments — all phrased as present-tense descriptions of cross-file wiring that had not landed yet. Reworded to standing requirements ("X is *meant to run from* Y, and that invocation must be contained").

    2. **🟠 Two producers, one artifact** — `merge-glossary.js` (onboarding; 3 sources, Íðorðabankinn not among them) vs `export-terminology.js` (continuous; DB). The "one real code path" rule argues for resolving this, but the resolution is a lead call and needs the dry-run numbers first.

    4. **🟡 `docs/editorial/terminology.md:220`** still calls the CSV files "the authoritative source for approved terminology", which the DB redesign superseded. Tracked in the closure audit as `ed-dim-8`; not re-homed here.

The early return made the guard structurally inert for it: any export, including an empty one, would have been written and pushed. `shrinkVerdict` now also compares total term counts (`countTerms`), independent of status, so a shrink is caught even when the approved count starts and stays at zero.     7. **🟡 `chmod 0o000` blocks read *and* write**, so no test can isolate "read blocked, write allowed" to reproduce the literal silent-overwrite-plus-green-heartbeat scenario end to end.

That crash was real, but follow-up 5's fix (replacing those reads with the tolerant `countTerms`/`countApproved`) silently converted it into something worse instead of fixing it: with no baseline (`prev === null`, e.g. a book's first export) `shrinkVerdict.refuse` stays `false` regardless of `next`'s shape, so the malformed payload was written to disk as-is — exit 0, zero errors logged, `glossary-unified.json` reduced to `{"generated":...,"book":...,"stats":{}}` with no terms at all, committed by the 2h cron, `/api/health` green. `runGlossaryExport` now validates `next` immediately after the `exportFn` call — must be a non-null object with `Array.isArray(next.terms)` — before `readExisting`/`sameTerms`/`shrinkVerdict` ever run; a payload failing that check is a per-book failure (logged with the book name, `failures++`, loop continues, heartbeat withheld, nothing written). See the "malformed exportFn payload" describe block in `server/__tests__/glossaryExportRun.test.js`.

Extracted a pure, exported `parseArgs(argv)`; a missing value for `--book` is now a loud parse error, never a silent "all books" fallback.

  - **The arc, all five rounds (the block above records round 1 only).** Five whole-branch adversarial reviews ran on this branch before the PR opened, each fixing what the previous one found and each finding some of its own fixes wrong: **confirmed findings 8 → 5 → 6 → 2 → 0; worst severity CRITICAL → CRITICAL → important → important → none.** Round 1 is detailed above (the inert shrink guard). Round 2 (5 confirmed, 1 CRITICAL): `parseArgs`'s `if`/`else if` chain had no final `else`, so `--book=<slug>`, a bare positional, or a typo like `--books` silently meant "every book" while `--force` still applied — round 1 had closed only the bare-`--book` instance, not the class; plus the shrink-guard's own test fixtures were degenerate (`approved(n)` always had `total === approved`), so a mutation check showed the approved clause could be deleted with the suite still green. Round 3 (6 confirmed, 1 important, 0 critical): round 1's own fix (tolerant `countTerms`/`countApproved`) had silently turned a fail-loud crash on a malformed `exportFn` return into a **silent bad write** — exit 0, heartbeat green, an empty glossary committed; fixed with a shape guard ahead of any comparison. Round 4 (2 confirmed, both important, 0 minor — first round with no critical or minor survivors): `--book ''` parsed as present-but-falsy, so `parseArgs` and `runGlossaryExport` disagreed on what counted as "no book" and an empty value silently widened to every glossary at once; and the refusal/dry-run/success messages' total-term pair was unpinned by any test (every fixture in the file happened to have `total === approved`). Round 5: 0 confirmed — the branch's code was clean, but reading the raw findings (not just the verdict) caught one its own verifiers had wrongly refuted: the implementation plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-27-c14-glossary-export-runner.md`) still showed round 1's CRITICAL approved-only `shrinkVerdict` verbatim in its Task 3 code fence — accurate as history, dangerous as a rebuild recipe, since this repo's workflow is plan → TDD. Corrected in this pass (see below).
  - **Most findings in each round were introduced by the previous round's own fix**, not pre-existing — the same "review the branch, not just the original problem" lesson C10 taught. Two defect classes each recurred (the branch's own commits call out the third-and-onward recurrence explicitly rather than treating it as a fresh finding):
    - **(a) producer/consumer predicate mismatch** — a guard checked a value was *present* while its consumer checked it was *truthy*. Round 1's `--book` fix checked `!== undefined`; `runGlossaryExport` checked truthiness. `--book ''` is present-and-non-undefined (passes the first check) but falsy (fails the second), so an empty value slipped through the seam and silently widened scope to every glossary-bearing book, with `--force` on the same command line bypassing the shrink guard on all of them. Fixed by making both sides agree on an explicit `book === null` check, and by having `parseArgs` reject an empty/whitespace value outright.
    - **(b) operator-facing messages pinned by no test while the behaviour behind them was thoroughly tested** — the guard logic was correct and green, but the string an operator actually reads on a refusal, a dry run, or a healthy run was never asserted, so a message could regress to something misleading (or uninformative, per round 1's "0 approved (0 approved)" case) without turning the suite red.
  - **Five more instances of class (b) were found in round 4's sweep and deliberately left unfixed** — these are *uncovered*, not *misleadingly* covered (no test names them, so nothing licenses editing them the way a false-pinning test would), which is why they were logged rather than patched:
    1. **🟠 `scripts/git-backup.sh`'s `WARN: node not found in cron PATH` has ZERO test references** — and it is the signal that fires in precisely the scenario C14 exists to prevent: the export silently not running on production. The most worthwhile of the five to eventually cover.
    2. **🟡 The `${b}: unchanged (N approved) — not rewritten` line** — only the heartbeat side-effect is tested, never the `log()` call itself. It is also the line the lead sees on every healthy cron run.
    3. **🟡 `${b}: could not resolve book subject — ${err.message}`** — no test exercises `subjectFn` throwing.
    4. **🟡 `${b}: export failed — ${err.message}`** — the DB-locked test checks the exit code and heartbeat, not the logged text.
    5. **🟡 `${b}: could not read existing export — ${err.message}`** — the EACCES tests likewise check behaviour, not this string.

The independent 2026-07-30 re-extraction/re-MT hold also still applies.
    3. **`lifraen-efnafraedi` (organic) — ✅ REMAP its subject to `chemistry`.** Rationale: the Icelandic Chemical Society is responsible for **all** of chemistry, `organic-chemistry` is a subject **no translation anywhere carries**, and per-book preference (decision 6 below) means sharing a subject costs organic no ability to differ from `efnafraedi-2e` on any individual term. ⚠️🔴 **THAT VERIFICATION WAS SCOPE-LIMITED AND THIS SENTENCE WAS FALSE AS WRITTEN — corrected 2026-08-07 by the whole-branch adversarial review, which reproduced the counter-case end-to-end.** *(It read: "✅ VERIFIED SAFE BEFORE PROPOSING — the remap cannot trigger a write: … the producer gate keys on shape, not subject, so the outcome stays `refused-producer` either way.")* The **premise is still true** — organic's committed file is unambiguously `merge-glossary` shape (1,117 terms, all carrying `category`/`chapter`, 0 carrying `subjects`, no `producer` stamp) — and the conclusion holds **only for the unattended cron path, which is the only path the dry-run exercised.** On the `--adopt` path it is the reverse: `exportBookGlossary` filters on the book's subject **and nothing else**, so the remap makes organic's payload byte-equivalent to chemistry's, which **clears the shrink gate that had refused it with certainty at 1117 → 0**. A bare `--adopt` therefore became able to overwrite organic's glossary — a book it could not touch before. ✅ **Closed in the same branch by implementing decision 5** (`--adopt`/`--force` now require `--book`) and by making the producer refusal print `--adopt --book <slug>` instead of the unscoped form it had been advertising. **The durable lesson is the shape of the mistake, not the bug:** *a dry-run verifies the dry-run's path. Stating its result as a property of the system generalises one path to all of them* — the same "passes for the wrong reason" family this register has now recorded three times in one day. ⚠️ **It is NOT inert for editors**, which is the point: `findTermsInSegments` resolves tiers through the book's subject, so organic's editors go from **0** surfaced terms to chemistry's 709 the moment it lands.

A **visible, D6-clocked refusal and no write**, exactly as §C21 intends. ⚠️ **Commit a `.gitkeep`, do NOT `mkdir` on prod** — an untracked directory on the box is the same class of invisible prod drift as the `#CONTAINED-2026-08-03#` edit, and this one would silently vanish on a fresh clone. Two questions, two columns, neither overloaded. **The limit this entry told the lead to "decide now rather than discover later" is decided.**

Migration `029` sitting beside it, supplying `registered_by: 'system'` and working, is the tell; the fix is one word. **Production consequence is nil** — both books were registered through the admin UI — but a genuine fresh-clone bootstrap comes up two books short, and that is exactly the path CLAUDE.md advertises as working. It also explains a smaller puzzle: it is why migration 044's second remapping is unreachable on a fresh chain, which had been mis-attributed to "a fresh DB has no registered books at all". *[severity: correctness/bootstrap · blocks: nothing]*

  - **▶ ② THE PROD DRY-RUN RAN 2026-08-03 (A4 runbook L4). Read this before deciding any `--force`.** Output verbatim in the runbook's L4 section. The guard behaved correctly everywhere; what the numbers reveal is elsewhere.
    - **🔴 `liffraedi-2e`: terms 2262 → 13561, approved **0 → 13561**. DO NOT `--force` THIS BOOK YET.** `shrinkVerdict` (`server/lib/glossaryExportDecision.js:82`, `SHRINK_RATIO = 0.5`) refuses only on **shrink**, so a 6× growth is **structurally unguarded** — correctly, by design, but it means nothing stands between this and a write. That function's own comment records the committed biology file as *"2262 terms, all needs_review"*, i.e. **zero approved**; approved terms are substituted into published CNXML/HTML (follow-up 4 above), so this would flip an entire book's terminology into reader-visible output in one command. Shape matches a bulk Íðorðabankinn import, which `[[idordabanki-biology-seeding]]` says to adopt **per-term, never in bulk**. *[severity: correctness · blocks: reader-visible biology terminology]*
      - **The measurement (fact, and not in dispute).** `terminology_translations` is **28,903 rows**, every one `status='approved'`, `source='idordabankinn'`, `proposed_by = approved_by = 'idordabankinn-import'`, all stamped inside a **26-minute window on 2026-03-25**. Subject split `biology 13561 · mathematics 9137 · physics 5496 · chemistry 709` matches the dry-run exactly.
      - **⚠️ CORRECTED 2026-08-04 AFTER TALKING TO THE LEAD — the earlier reading of that measurement was WRONG, and the correction changes the fix.** This entry previously concluded *"there is no human-reviewed terminology in the database at all"* and inferred that the bulk `approved` stamp was provenance-laundering. **It is not.** The import was a deliberate, defensible lead decision: Íðorðabankinn (Árnastofnun) is **canon**, so re-adjudicating 28,903 entries by hand would restate what the source already establishes — and **provenance is recorded separately and correctly** in `source` + `idordabanki_id`. Nothing was disguised.
      - **🔴 THE REAL DEFECT: `status` is a SELECTOR, not a provenance stamp — and bulk-approving destroyed the thing it selects.** **7,601 of 20,272 headwords carry MORE THAN ONE translation** (6,502 have 2, 573 have 3, up to 13). Those siblings are not equal-status synonyms; they are **competing candidates for one English headword** — `atom` (headword 112) is `frumeind` **and** `atóm`, both `approved`. Three independent signals confirm the field was designed as a chooser: the schema **defaults `status` to `'proposed'`**; both consumers filter on it (`buildGlossaryMap`'s `if (t.status !== 'approved') continue`, `formatGlossary`'s `approvedOnly`); and `exportBookGlossary` already emits an **`alternatives`** array of the siblings. **With every candidate approved, nothing selects — see §C18 for what happens instead.**
      - **✅ THE FIX IS SMALL, PER-BOOK, AND EXACTLY WHAT THE LEAD PROPOSED: `approved` means "this is the term THIS BOOK uses."** Two different questions were written into one column — **attestation** ("is this legitimate Icelandic?", answered by Íðorðabankinn, recorded in `source`) and **preference** ("which attested term does this book use?", an editorial choice). Splitting them costs nothing because both columns already exist. **The workload is far smaller than the row count suggests** — `12,671 of 20,272` headwords have exactly one translation, where `approved` is meaningful by default and needs no thought:

        | Subject | Headwords | Translations | **Competitions to resolve** |
        |---|---:|---:|---:|
        | chemistry | 580 | 709 | **129** (119 headwords ×2, 5 ×3) |
        | physics | 3,949 | 5,496 | 1,547 |
        | mathematics | 6,810 | 9,137 | 2,327 |
        | biology | 9,744 | 13,561 | 3,817 |

        Chemistry is **124 decisions**, not 709 and not 28,903 — and they are real editorial judgements of exactly the kind the lead described: `atom` → *frumeind | atóm* · `atomic orbital` → *frumeindasvigrúm | atómsvigrúm* · `bond energy` → *tengjaorka | tengiorka* · `actual yield` → *raunheimtur | eiginlegur afrakstur*. **Flip them as an editor meets them**; no bulk pass is required.

        - **🔴 CORRECTED 2026-08-07 BY MEASUREMENT — THE COUNT IS RIGHT AND THE CHARACTERISATION IS WRONG. These are NOT 124 editorial judgements.** Every one of the 124 is an Íðorðabankinn **head form plus its own listed synonym(s), from a single entry**, flattened into sibling `approved` rows at import. The discriminator was never dropped — it is sitting in two columns nobody consulted: the head form carries **`idordabanki_id`**, the synonym carries **`NULL` + `notes = 'Íðorðabankinn synonym'`** (8,129 rows DB-wide carry that exact note).
          - **▶ Measured on prod 2026-08-07.** Of **7,315** competing *(headword, subject)* groups database-wide, **7,277 (99.5%) contain exactly one head form**. Unresolved by preferring it: **chemistry 0** · biology 6 · mathematics 16 · physics 16 — **38 in the entire database**. Derivation, so it can be re-checked rather than trusted: group the non-rejected translations by `(headword_id, subject)`, keep `COUNT(*) > 1`, and count `idordabanki_id IS NOT NULL` per group.
          - **⚠️ The four examples above are instances of this shape, not counter-examples — checked individually:** `atom` *frumeind*=**HEAD** / *atóm*=synonym · `atomic orbital` *frumeindasvigrúm*=**HEAD** / *atómsvigrúm*=synonym · `bond energy` *tengjaorka*=**HEAD** / *tengiorka*=synonym · `actual yield` *raunheimtur*=**HEAD** / *eiginlegur afrakstur*=synonym. The sentence above cites them as evidence that the 124 are real judgements; they are the clearest evidence that they are not.
          - **▶ LEAD RULING 2026-08-07: the head form is the DEFAULT and the editor may override it per term.** So the 124 become a **pre-answered review queue**, not 124 blockers, and **nothing waits on them**. Chemistry's outstanding count for the purposes of the adoption decision is **0**, not 124.
          - **⚠️ WHAT THIS DOES *NOT* DISSOLVE — a different population, and the one that is genuinely editorial.** Cross-**collection** disagreements are real: `antibiotic` = *fúkalyf* (LIFORD) vs *sýklalyf* (ERFDAFR), and the lead's judgement is that **the ERFDAFR term is the one in common use** — so a ranked-collection precedence rule would confidently produce the **wrong** answer here. The variation is **within LIFORD itself** (donated by several individuals over time, some archaic, some current), not between collections. **None of chemistry's 124 is of this kind.** Collection origin is therefore **evidence to show an editor, never a precedence key** — and it is not currently retained at all: all 28,903 rows carry `source = 'idordabankinn'`, the *bank*, while `fetch_idordabanki.py:63` maps collection → subject and discards the code (`LIFORD` and `LIFORD2` both become `biology`).
            - **▶ MEASURED 2026-08-07, in answer to "does a collection column exist?" — NO, and not in any table.** Grepping the **entire** schema for `ordabok|collection|dictionary|fkdict|corpus|ordasafn` returns nothing. Three supporting measurements: **`source` is uniform** — all 28,903 rows are the single value `idordabankinn`, so the column records the *bank* and carries no per-row information at all (the schema permits 11 sources; the data uses one, and the `openstax-glossary`/`openstax-mt`/`chemistry-society-csv` rows visible in the committed file were **never in the DB**); **subject coverage is total** — 28,903 of 28,903 translations carry at least one subject tag, none untagged; and **`idordabanki_id` splits 20,774 populated / 8,129 NULL**, exactly the head-form/synonym line.
            - **⚠️ TODAY THE LOSS IS ONE BIT, AND THE VALUE IS PROSPECTIVE.** Five collections were imported onto four subjects, so the *only* pair that collapses is `LIFORD` + `LIFORD2` → `biology`. A collection column would currently tell you one thing `subject` cannot. **It becomes load-bearing the moment more collections are imported** — `tools/idordabanki_collections.json` records, measured against this project's own biology headwords, that **111 of 283 headwords found in more than one collection have DIFFERENT Icelandic**. Without the column that import is a blind union in which load order silently wins. *That file's own instruction — "keep `fkdictionary` on every imported term and resolve precedence per domain" — was written 2026-07-26 and never implemented.*
            - **⚠️ A RE-FETCH BACKFILL JOINS CLEANLY FOR ONLY 20,774 OF 28,903.** The 8,129 synonym rows carry no `idordabanki_id` and therefore **no join key**; they would have to inherit their head form's collection via the shared headword. That is workable and it is an **inference, not a measurement** — record it as one if it is done, because a synonym's collection would then be an assumption wearing the same column as a fact.
            - **⚠️ AND IT DOES NOT SOLVE THE PROBLEM THAT MOTIVATED IT.** Per the lead 2026-08-07, the archaic-vs-current variation is **inside `LIFORD` itself** — donated by several individuals over time — so a collection column distinguishes LIFORD from ERFDAFR and says nothing about the terms the lead actually needs separated. **Retain it for editor evidence and for the safety of the next import; do not expect it to resolve anything.**

A `git revert` of an adopted file restores the `merge-glossary` shape (no stamp; `category`/`chapter` per term), so the next **bare** cron tick detects the mismatch and lands on `refused-producer` — and the cron cannot reach `--adopt`. **The revert holds.** That makes a first adoption a git-visible act the lead can undo, not a one-way door. *The older warning is left in place as the record of the pre-guard mechanism.*

An operational consequence of the decision, not a defect.

A book that is *out* of the loop gets **none of that, forever** — no line, no clock, no nag.
