  - **▶ ③ THE ROOT-CAUSE FIX (step 4) IS BUILT — branch `fix/c14-glossary-export-provenance`, 2026-08-05.** 8 plan tasks over **7 review dispatches** — tasks 4 and 5 were merged on a pre-flight ruling because plan Task 4 deliberately leaves the tree non-compiling until Task 5, which **no independent task review can pass**; tasks 1–7 were each independently reviewed (several with fix rounds), and **this closing task is the exception — it is unreviewed pending the whole-branch adversarial pass.** *(PR number + merge commit: ⬜ **not yet opened**, and the whole-branch adversarial review ⬜ **not yet run** as of this line — fill all three in when it lands.)*
    - **⚠️⚠️ READ THIS BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE IN THIS BLOCK: SHIPPING THIS DOES *NOT* MAKE THE GLOSSARY EXPORT SAFE TO SWITCH BACK ON.** Production still carries the uncommitted `#CONTAINED-2026-08-03#` edit in `scripts/git-backup.sh`, and **adoption is per book, `[LEAD]`, and UNMADE.** Lifting the containment today makes the cron **refuse every book that HAS a committed glossary file** rather than write them — that is the guard working, but it is not a decision anyone has taken, and §C14 ②'s standing per-book positions (biology *do not write*, organic *decide what its glossary should be first*, chemistry *a conscious call on 408 terms*) all still hold. **What this branch changes is that switching it back on stops being a silent write and becomes a decision with a visible outcome.** It does not take the decision. *A register line reading "step 4 shipped ✅" without this paragraph is exactly how someone lifts the containment and re-runs the unattended write within 2 h.*
      - **⚠️⚠️ CORRECTION 2026-08-05 (whole-branch adversarial review) — "refuses EVERY book" WAS FALSE, and the exception is the dangerous half.** ⚠️ **This bullet used to enumerate the sites ("this paragraph, the D6 bullet below and `export-terminology.js`'s header — all three corrected"). That enumeration was WRONG: two more branch-introduced copies survived in code comments, one of them inside `glossaryExportHealth.js`, the file implementing the very alarm the claim misdescribes. A count is exactly what CLAUDE.md § *One source of truth* says not to trust — including, it turns out, a count written while correcting a false claim.** Re-derive instead of trusting: `grep -rn "refuses EVERY book\|refuses every book\|refuse every book" --include=*.js --include=*.md .` — every live hit must carry the "that has a committed file" qualifier and the absent-baseline exception. The frozen `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-04-…-design.md` copy is **evidence, not status**, and is deliberately left uncorrected. The claim holds only for a book **with a committed `glossary-unified.json`**. A book that has a `glossary/` **directory** but **no committed file** makes `readExisting` return `{kind:'absent'}` → `prev = null` → `producerVerdict` returns `refuse:false` **and** `shrinkVerdict`'s `prevTotal > 0` clause is false: **BOTH GATES ARE STRUCTURALLY INERT.** The bare cron then **writes, exits 0, writes the heartbeat**, and `git-backup.sh` commits and pushes it — with `/api/health` reading **ok**. That is the **`orverufraedi` third of the 2026-08-03 incident** (its file was *created*, not overwritten), and **this branch does not change it.** The producer gate cannot fire on a file that does not exist; there is nothing to fingerprint. *Reading the corrected sentence as "so it is contained" is the exact error this correction exists to stop: the books it does not cover are the ones nobody is watching.*
      - **🆕 `stjornufraedi` IS LIVE IN THAT STATE — add it to the standing per-book positions.** Derivation, so it can be re-checked rather than trusted: `refused-no-mapping` is only reachable **after** `hasGlossaryDir()` passes ⇒ it **has** a `glossary/` dir; the register already records a loud `stjornufraedi` no-mapping refusal on prod (see the bullet above about book coverage) ⇒ confirmed. `git ls-tree -r HEAD` lists **three** committed `glossary-unified.json` files — `efnafraedi-2e`, `liffraedi-2e`, `lifraen-efnafraedi` — and `stjornufraedi` is **not** one ⇒ its baseline is **`absent`**.
        - **⚠️ THE PERVERSE CONSEQUENCE, STATED EXPLICITLY:** the only remedy for its current refusal — **inserting the `book_subject_mapping` row** — is **precisely what triggers the ungated write on the next tick.** Today the mapping guard is the only thing holding it, and that guard is upstream of the two gates that would otherwise have something to say. Fixing the refusal *removes* the last obstacle rather than adding one. **Decide what `stjornufraedi`'s glossary should be BEFORE adding the row**, exactly as organic's position says. `edlisfraedi-2e` remains silently absent from the export entirely (no `glossary/` dir) — a different hole, unchanged.
      - **⬜ TWO FOLLOW-UPS LOGGED, NEITHER TAKEN HERE — both are real decisions, deliberately not made late in a fix wave.**
        1. **Should an `absent` baseline REQUIRE `--adopt`** for a book that never had a committed glossary? It would close the `orverufraedi`/`stjornufraedi` hole above by making "first write" an explicit act rather than the default. Against it: it also makes every legitimately new book need a manual step, and `--adopt`'s current meaning is "override the producer gate", which is not what would be happening. *[severity: correctness/containment · blocks: nothing]*
        2. **Should `--adopt` REQUIRE `--book`?** Bare `--adopt` is the adopt-every-book form; run against prod's committed state it reproduces the 2026-08-03 incident's two writes, same numbers, in one command. The header's usage example was corrected to `--book <slug> --adopt` in this fix wave, but **the behaviour is unchanged** — documentation is the only thing between a copy-paste and a corpus-wide swap. Same question applies to `--force`. *[severity: robustness · blocks: nothing]*
    - **🚨🚨 DEPLOY TRAP — REQUIRED PRE-DEPLOY STEP, READ BEFORE RUNNING `./scripts/deploy.sh` WITH THIS BRANCH MERGED.** This branch **rewords the `WARN:` line that sits INSIDE the block prod's uncommitted `#CONTAINED-2026-08-03#` containment comments out.** `deploy.sh` **stashes** local changes, pulls, then **pops** — and *both* outcomes are traps:
      - **The pop CONFLICTS** → `scripts/git-backup.sh` is left with conflict markers, i.e. a **bash syntax error**, which **silently kills the 2-hourly CONTENT backup** — a *different* subsystem (**§C11(b)**), the one whose whole point is that a failed content push is visible. Meanwhile `deploy.sh` prints **"Deploy complete. Server healthy"**, because it does not parse the file it just clobbered.
      - **The pop SUCCEEDS** → **THE CONTAINMENT SURVIVES**, so this entire fix ships **INERT** while the register (and the lead) believe the export is guarded *and running*. Nothing in the deploy output distinguishes this from success.
      - **▶ MITIGATION — do this by hand; do NOT rely on stash/pop to round-trip it.** On prod: **(1)** `git diff scripts/git-backup.sh` and save the output — that is the only copy of the containment; **(2)** REMOVE the containment (restore the file to HEAD); **(3)** run `./scripts/deploy.sh`; **(4)** if the per-book adoption decisions are **not yet taken**, re-apply the containment **deliberately**, from the diff captured in step 1. Then confirm which state you are in — read `scripts/git-backup.sh` on the box, do not infer it from the deploy output.
      - *Why this is worth four steps: the containment is an **uncommitted working-tree edit on a production box**, which is the one kind of state neither git nor CI nor `/api/health` can tell you about. The deploy readout this fix wave just corrected reports the export's **outcomes**; it cannot report that the export was never invoked.*
    - **What it replaces.** The guard was `SHRINK_RATIO = 0.5` on **file size** — so the 2026-08-03 wholesale producer swap passed (chemistry −36.5%, under a *halving* threshold) and biology's growth was structurally invisible. That is now the *second* gate. The first is a **measured producer fingerprint** (`server/lib/glossaryProducer.js`, pure): the two producers emit disjoint per-term shapes — `merge-glossary` writes `category`/`chapter`, `exportBookGlossary` writes `subjects` — re-measured against the real committed files by `server/__tests__/glossaryProducer.test.js` rather than trusted from a comment. New exports also carry a self-identifying top-level `producer` stamp, so the fingerprint is only the fallback for pre-stamp files. A payload carrying **both** fingerprints is `unknown` **on purpose** → refuse: when we cannot tell what we would destroy, a human decides.
    - **Gate order is load-bearing and is producer-FIRST.** A producer swap is categorical; a shrink is quantitative. Reporting "1117 → 709, a 36.5% shrink" about a file *another program wrote* invites the operator to reason about two numbers that count different things. ⚠️ **This ordering has no visible symptom when wrong** — see the landmine under *deferred minors* below before touching the shrink message.
    - **The rest of the mechanism.** `--adopt` overrides the producer gate and is **independent of `--force`** (two risks, two acknowledgements — neither implies the other). **The cron invokes the script bare (`scripts/git-backup.sh:139`), so neither override is reachable unattended** — that is the structural answer to "a guard that only gates the manual path is not a gate". The failure counter became **per-book outcomes**; **exit 1 now means a genuine error only**, so one book's correct refusal no longer marks the whole exporter unhealthy — which is what let `/api/health` read `ok=false` straight through the run that wrote and pushed (the 🟠 SEPARATE DEFECT bullet above: **resolved here**). A per-book status file (`pipeline-output/.glossary-export-status.json`, gitignored) carries the breakdown, and `./scripts/deploy.sh` prints every refusal **even when the check is `ok`** — the only surface a human routinely reads.
    - **Two degenerate cases in that readout, observed and deliberately left alone — both fail SAFE, and neither is introduced here.** Recorded because the deploy printout is the only glossary surface a human routinely reads, so what it does when things are odd is worth knowing before someone "fixes" it: the route's catch shape prints a bare `glossary export: not ok` (redundant with the top line, harmless); and `checks.glossary_export: null` makes the **pre-existing** top-line filter throw, yielding `Health: (unparseable response)` and **exit 0** — noisy but non-blocking, which is correct, since a deploy must never be gated on a glossary reading.
    - **🆕 DECISION D6 (made during execution, human-ruled 2026-08-05 — amends the design's D2).** A refusal no longer stays `ok` **forever**. The status file records **`since` per book** (when its *current* outcome was first observed, carried forward while unchanged), and `/api/health` goes **not-ok** once any book has been refusing longer than **`GLOSSARY_REFUSAL_STALE_DAYS` (default 7)**. **Why it was needed:** D2's insight stands — a refusal is not an error, and a permanently-red check gets tuned out — but every committed glossary is `merge-glossary`, so **the first cron run after this ships refuses every book that has one** *(corrected 2026-08-05 from "EVERY book" — see the correction bullet under this block's opening caveat; a book with an `absent` baseline is not refused, it is WRITTEN, both gates inert)*, and under plain D2 that steady state reads `ok` **indefinitely**, with evidence only in a gitignored log. A refusal must not be allowed to become a permanent *silent* state. Reuses the repo's existing staleness doctrine (`content_backup`, `offbox_backup`).
    - **✅ D6's clock survives a transient error — FIXED 2026-08-05 in the whole-branch fix wave (both reviewers, independently; human-ruled).** `withSince` carried `since` only when the outcome **string** was identical, and all five `fail()` sites record `outcome:'error'` — so **one** erroring run in the middle of a refusal streak restarted the seven days. At the 2-hourly cadence that is not a corner case: `git-backup.sh`'s own comment predicts the error class (the export opens `sessions.db` as a **second** process while the live editorial server holds it). Demonstrated: refusing from day 0, one `SQLITE_BUSY` on day 6 → on day 8 `findStaleRefusals` returned `[]` for a book that had not exported in eight days, and **any** error recurring more often than weekly suppressed D6 **indefinitely**. `since` is now carried when the previous and current outcomes are **both unresolved** (`error` or any `refused-*`), so an error interlude neither manufactures a streak nor resets one and the alarm means *"not successfully exported since X"*. A **resolved** outcome (`wrote`/`adopted`/`unchanged`) still resets it, and `wrote → error` still resets, so the rule stays symmetric. The load-bearing new test asserts the **consequence** through the real health lib — `stale_refusals` must still contain the book on day 8 — not merely the intermediate `since` value.
    - **🆕 RULING — option (c), 2026-08-05: a filtered (`--book`) run writes NO status file, and there is no `filtered` field.** The first draft let a `--book` run overwrite the whole-corpus map, so the next unfiltered run found no previous entry for the untouched books and **reset their stale-refusal clocks**. That reset is **correlated with the alarm's firing window** — a lead hand-runs `--book <slug> --adopt` precisely *while the other books are still refusing and their clocks are running* — so it lands preferentially on the runs that matter. Option (c) makes the status file exactly parallel to the heartbeat: both whole-corpus, both withheld on a filtered run, **one rule instead of two**. The `filtered` field was **deleted rather than left permanently false** (a dead field implying filtered runs write is worse than no field). **Accepted, documented cost:** after `--book --adopt` the status file shows the stale outcome for ≤2 h — harmless, because it can only *delay* an alarm, never manufacture one.
    - **🔒 SECURITY FIX made during execution (found by the Task 6 review, treated as a requirement).** `GET /api/health` is **UNAUTHENTICATED** (`server/index.js`) on an internet-facing server, and this repo is public. The per-book map's **`detail` can embed `err.message` verbatim, including absolute server filesystem paths** (`EACCES … open '/srv/.../glossary-unified.json'`). Health now projects each book down to **`{outcome, since}`** via an allowlist **in `server/lib/glossaryExportHealth.js`, not in the route** — so a future second caller gets the projection for free instead of re-leaking by omission — and the projection **fails closed** (a non-object entry becomes `null`, never a passthrough). `detail` stays in the gitignored status file on the box.
    - **Mutation-verified whole-branch, 2026-08-05 (evidence, re-run after all seven implementation tasks had landed together).** Six mutations, each applied → run → reverted → re-confirmed green: **(M1)** `detectProducer` forced to return `export-terminology` → the real-committed-file cases and the producer-gate cases go RED; **(M2)** the `if (pv.refuse && !adopt)` **call site** deleted → only the *runner*-level tests go red while `glossaryProducer.test.js` stays green — M1 proves the detector, M2 proves it is **wired**; **(M3)** gate order swapped to shrink-first → the ordering test goes RED on `would fall`, **while `/producer/i` still matched**, empirically confirming that the *negative* assertions are the only thing distinguishing the two orderings; **(M4)** `refused-producer` renamed → the exact-outcome-string tests go red and the health/deploy suites do **not**, confirming the `refused-` naming contract is pinned at the *producer* end exactly as `glossaryExportHealth.js`'s comment claims; **(M5)** the health projection reverted to a passthrough → the three security tests go RED; **(M6)** `detail` added back into `withSince`'s carry comparison → the detail-drift test goes RED. *A guard nothing fails on is not a guard.*
    - **⚠️ DEFERRED MINORS — carried here because the SDD workspace is deleted when this branch finishes.** None blocking; none fixed here.
      1. **⚠️⚠️ THE SHRINK MESSAGE IS A LANDMINE — do not reword it casually.** The gate-ordering test in `server/__tests__/glossaryExportRun.test.js` distinguishes producer-first from shrink-first **solely by NEGATIVELY asserting** that the refusal message does *not* contain `would fall` and the arrow form `<prevTotal> → <nextTotal>`. ⚠️ **The test's *positive* `/producer/i` assertion offers NO residual protection — it is inert as a discriminator, because BOTH refusal messages contain the word.** Verified by execution 2026-08-05, running all three of the test's regexes against the two real message strings: the **producer** message matches via *"Writing would SWAP PRODUCERS, not refresh"*, the **shrink** message via *"may come from a different producer (`tools/merge-glossary.js`)"* — so `/producer/i` is TRUE under **either** gate ordering, and the two negative assertions are the **entire** discriminator. **Reword either fragment away and that test passes VACUOUSLY with the whole suite green**, leaving gate order unpinned — and gate order has **no visible symptom when wrong**. Its wording is also mildly stale (it still speculates "may come from a different producer", which the producer gate now answers categorically). **Either leave it alone, or preserve both fragments verbatim AND re-run the gate-order mutation to prove the test still goes RED.** Verified live 2026-08-05 (M3 above): the test *is* currently sensitive.
      2. **On the corrupt-file path, `--adopt` effectively implies `--force`** — there is no readable baseline to measure a shrink against, so the shrink gate cannot fire. Structurally unavoidable; the **message** should say so and does not.
      3. **`export-terminology.js`'s header lists "an unreadable existing file" under exit 1**, but the *corrupt* (unparseable) refusal exits 0. Misleading for triage — distinguish **EACCES** (an error) from **unparseable** (a refusal).
      4. **`producerVerdict`'s JSDoc annotates `prevProducer: string`** while the null-prev branch returns `null` — deliberate (it distinguishes "no file" from "unclassifiable file"); the annotation should read `string|null`.
      5. **`producerVerdict` returns `refuse:false` when BOTH sides are `unknown`**, which nominally contradicts the detector's "cannot tell → refuse" posture. **Verified unreachable in production**: the call site's shape guard rejects a malformed `next` first, and every `next` now carries the stamp, which short-circuits before the terms check. *Recorded because the reasoning, not the code, is what makes it safe — and the stamp is what makes it unreachable.*
      6. **A test asserts the literal `'export-terminology'`** instead of importing `PRODUCER_EXPORT`, unlike its sibling suite. Plan-level inconsistency, not an implementer deviation.
      7. **The health invariant rests on a hand-built `detail` shape:** `{...health, ...detail}` lets `detail` win the collision, so a future `{...parsed}` passthrough could overwrite `stale`/`age_hours` **untested**.
      8. **The staleness threshold boundary is untested** (`>=` vs `>`).
      9. **Health exposes no age for the STATUS file** (only for the heartbeat). An early `return 1` before `writeStatus` leaves a stale breakdown reading as current, with `stale: true` the only cue.
    - **⚠️ PRE-EXISTING, NOT INTRODUCED HERE (found during this branch, deliberately unfixed):** two older `chmod` tests in `glossaryExportRun.test.js` — *"does NOT treat an unreadable existing export as 'no baseline'"* and *"an unreadable book does not skip the books after it"* — have **no root guard and would pass VACUOUSLY when run as root**. The new `writeStatus` test uses `it.skipIf(process.getuid?.() === 0)` and documents the older gap in place. *[severity: test-integrity · blocks: nothing]*
    - **🟠 OPEN CONCERN, flagged by the Task 7 review and deliberately unfixed — it needs its own decision.** The `/api/health` route's **own catch branch** (`{ok:false, error: err.message}`) can leak the same class of absolute server path that the projection above exists to prevent, if a lib throws. It is a **repo-wide pattern with precedent elsewhere in the same handler**, so fixing it here would have been an inconsistent one-off. *[severity: security/info-leak · blocks: nothing]*
    - **Follow-ups logged from the design's §9 (not built here):**
      1. **`tools/merge-glossary.js` should refuse to clobber an ADOPTED file** — the reverse swap. The detector makes this ~5 lines, but `merge-glossary.js` is a second tool with its own call sites and belongs in its own change. ⚠️ It also still has **3 sources (Íðorðabankinn is not one)** and its `--db` upsert targets the `terminology_terms` table **migration 032 dropped**, so it needs a look anyway.
      2. **`stats.disputed` as a corroborating producer signal is unused.** The fingerprint keys on `category`/`chapter`/`subjects` only; if it ever needs hardening, the second signal is already measured.
    - **Lessons worth keeping (all cost real review time on this branch):**
      - **A controller's enumeration in a brief is not reliable — it under-counted twice on one task**, and the implementer caught both (a "four sites" table that had five; "three contract-changing tests" that were six). **Briefs should say "find all N and report the count", never enumerate.**
      - **A wrong number propagates faithfully through every derived document.** A term total was wrong in the design, and was transcribed unchanged into the plan → the task brief → a source comment → a commit message. **No assertion could have caught it**: the tests compute counts from disk and never hard-code a total — which is *why* they were right and the prose was wrong. Fixed **forward**, not amended, so the record shows the correction.
      - **A replacement for a vacuous test can itself be vacuous.** One rewrite used an empty `terms` array and passed either way; only running the mutation caught it. **Re-run the mutation against the NEW test, not just the old one.**
      - **The controller was wrong and the implementer right on a health-semantics claim**, confirmed in source by the reviewer: the justification for writing the status file before the error return is the `errors > 0` path (a persistently erroring exporter shows a **fresh `ran` beside a growing `age_hours`** — "running and failing" vs "cron stopped"), *not* the empty-book-list path, which returns above both writes.
      - **`npm run format:check` globs only `tools/**/*.js` and `scripts/**/*.js`** — so it matches **no `.mjs` file anywhere and nothing under `server/` at all**, and prettier drift in those trees is invisible to CI. **A clean `format:check` is not whole-tree evidence.** (The `server/` tree is still formatted, but by the lint-staged pre-commit hook, which only sees *staged* files — so a `server/` file changed outside a commit is checked by nothing.)
    - **⚠️ DO NOT SYNC the frozen quotes.** The **old** `git-backup.sh` WARN text is still quoted verbatim in the 2026-07-27 C14 spec and plan and in the 🟠 SEPARATE DEFECT bullet above. Those are **frozen evidence** per CLAUDE.md § *One source of truth* and must **not** be updated to match the new wording.

- **C15 · Two status/dashboard reporting defects found while doing C10 (both pre-existing, neither caused by it)** — **[CODE]** — **P3.** Logged per the standing "log every out-of-scope find" rule; deliberately *not* fixed in the C10 PR.
  - **(a) `suggestNextActions`'s `publication` branch is unreachable.** It tests `rawStatus.publication?.complete`, but `getStatusDataFromDb` (`getStatusDataFromDb`) only ever builds `publication.{mtPreview,faithful,localized}.complete` — there is no top-level flag, so a chapter that has published all three tracks is still told to "Publish to web". Note `formatChapterStatus` gets the same question **right** a few lines away (it ANDs the three sub-tracks), which is why `progress`/`nextStage` are correct while `actions` is not — the two disagree inside one response. Pinned as a comment in `server/__tests__/nonSequentialStages.test.js`.
  - **(b) The editorial-progress route's `getDiscussEdits(50)` is a GLOBAL page used as a per-book set** (`routes/status.js`, `GET /:book/editorial-progress`). The route takes the newest 50 discuss edits across every book and *then* filters to the requested book (`.filter((e) => e.book === book)`), so a book's blocked segments silently vanish from its editorial-progress view once 50 newer ones exist elsewhere. Same family as C10-R4 (a page used where a set was meant) but a different call site and a different fix — it needs a book-scoped query, not a count. The meeting-agenda sibling (limit 20) is a genuine display list and is fine. *[severity: correctness · editor-visible · blocks: nothing]*

- **C12 · Branch protection — decided 2026-07-26** — **[LEAD]** — 5-lens analysis; all five converged. **Adopt force-push + deletion blocking only; adopt nothing else.** Required status checks are **moot, not merely unwise**: they gate direct pushes too, and a cron commit has no check runs, so no configuration coexists with the content cron (verified: 1 collaborator, 0 deploy keys → no bypass identity exists). Required PRs would be theatre (admin bypass) or a silent content outage (no bypass). Measured: required checks would have blocked **58 merged PRs / 522 commits** during the 2026-07-12→25 window. **⚠️ Traps if ever revisited:** check-run names are the lowercase job ids (`lint`, `test`, `e2e`, `audit`) — `Tests` is **two** checks; and `Validate Status Files` + `check-docs` are `paths:`-filtered on `pull_request`, so requiring them blocks PRs **forever** on the PRs where they don't report. **⚠️ CLAUDE.md is wrong** — `validate` is *not* push-to-main only (`validate.yml:13` has a path-filtered `pull_request` trigger), which makes it *more* dangerous, since it appears in the required-checks picker looking safe. *[severity: hardening · blocks: nothing]*

- **C16 · Partially-live legacy from the Markdown/Matecat era — audit + triage** — **[CODE]** — **P1** — *logged 2026-07-29. **Spec + plan written the same day for the re-attach sub-project; the clean break itself is [LEAD]-gated on scoping.***
  - **✅ THE AUDIT IS EXECUTED — 2026-08-07, artifacts (a)–(e) plus the era-bounding survey. Full measurement, frozen: [`docs/audit/2026-08-07-c16-partially-live-legacy-audit.md`](../audit/2026-08-07-c16-partially-live-legacy-audit.md).** It delivers §C16's specified deliverable — the **classification** (*dead → remove · partially live → decide · load-bearing → keep and pin*), **not** a fix and **not** a fix direction. Read it before scoping the remediation. Method: A/B of the **real** `reverseInlineMarkup` with `hasApiMarkers` forced, so the delta is exactly the legacy branches' contribution — deliberately **not** a shape regex, which is the method §C16 itself records as having mis-scored chemistry's MathML blanks.
    - **🔴 (a) IS BOTH MORE LOAD-BEARING AND LESS CURRENTLY-CORRUPTING THAN THIS ENTRY IMPLIES — and both halves matter.** Measured: the legacy branch actually **alters output on 168 of 22,004** legacy-era segments (**0.76 %**), of which **164 are correct conversions** and **4 are corruption**. Forcing the guard the *other* way (the C13 flip hazard, **never previously measured**) would corrupt **4 of 8,643** API-era segments and **0 of 117** faithful-track ones. **So of 177 firings across both tracks, 173 are correct conversions, and the guard prevents 4 further corruptions on the flip side — it cannot simply be deleted**, and deleting it breaks the human-verified `03-faithful-translation/ch01/m68664` file, which has 9 segments depending on it. ⚠️ **The C13-class design defect is still real and still unfixed** — and per this entry's own 2026-07-30 correction the mixed-dialect state *regenerates*, so a re-MT does not retire it. What changed is the **size** of the live exposure, not the verdict on the design.
    - **⚠️ THE 71.7 % HEADLINE IS BRANCH-TAKEN, NOT AT-RISK — this entry's most misleading figure.** Re-derived: 30,647 segments, 22,004 guard-false, **71.8 %** — the census reproduces. But "71.7 % take the legacy path" and "71.7 % are at risk" are different claims and only the first is true; the altered set is 168.
    - **🔴 THE STARTING SET WAS WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** Of the 9 modules named below, **`ch03/m68700`, `ch09/m68750` and `ch17/m68825` never fire** (shape match ≠ converter match — exactly the caveat this entry wrote). **Four modules not listed do fire:** `efnafraedi-2e ch01/m68670`, and the three corruption sites. **10 firing modules, not 9.**
    - **🔴 THE READER-VISIBLE CORRUPTION IS IN TWO BOOKS, NOT ONE — a new instance was found.** `liffraedi-2e ch03/m66440` (`3-exercises.html`) carries the same blank-mangle as the two `orverufraedi` modules: **4 instances / 3 modules / 3 published pages**. All four are live in `03-translated/*.cnxml` and in rendered HTML. ⚠️ **Reader-visibility is not uniform** — biology ch03's vefur sync is [LEAD]-HELD (→ [LEAD] queue ①), microbiology's is not; confirm per book against `/content/<book>/chapters/<NN>/<file>.html`, never the page URL.
    - **🔴🆕 (f) A SIXTH ARTIFACT: `/intake-source` is a LIVE, ENABLED, `Write`-permitted slash command teaching three retired eras — and taking it means taking (c) WITH it.** `.claude/settings.local.json` disables only four other commands. It sources from `01-source/docx/`, instructs *"Upload to malstadur.is for machine translation"* (against the standing **translations = API only** rule) then *"proceed to Matecat alignment"*, and **writes `books/{book}/chapters/ch{NN}/files.json` — which IS artifact (c).** `server/routes/status.js:1266` guards on `existsSync(filesPath)`, false today only because 0 such files exist; creating one flips it TRUE and `:1279` serves the contents. **So (f) is what resurrects (c)'s dead branch** — *a route that fails closed masks what it would otherwise hit; reviving one re-exposes it.* **Fixing either alone leaves the other armed.** ⚠️ **RANKED WITH (b), NOT (a) — and this register carried the stronger claim for part of one session.** The audit's first pass said (f) writes a **`status.json`** whose four retired stage names `npm run validate` accepts; the control/treatment run was real but **against the wrong file** (`validate-status.js` reads only `status.json`). **Retracted** → audit §4.1/§6a. What survives independently — **but the mechanism I first gave for it was WRONG, corrected 2026-08-07 by the review**: I wrote that *"`schemas/chapter-status.schema.json` declares no `properties`/`additionalProperties` under `stages`"*. **There is no `stages` key at all** — the block is **`status`**, and it declares `additionalProperties: false` **plus** all 8 canonical stage names. **The schema is fine.** My probe asked for `properties.stages`, got `undefined`, and I read *undefined* as *unconstrained* — a probe returning nothing because it asked the wrong question, mistaken for evidence of absence. **The observed effect is still real and reproduced** (a real status file with four retired stage names reports `1/1 files valid`), and the cause is **solely that `scripts/validate-status.js` implements no `additionalProperties` check**. **That inverts the fix: the validator needs work, the schema needs none.** **(g)** `/pipeline-status`'s Matecat row is display-only; do not inflate it. **🆕 (i)** `docs/_generated/{tools,routes}.md` are generated, committed and **surfaced nowhere** — README lacks the markers `update-readme-sections.js` needs, so it is a permanent no-op. **That is the mechanism behind the ROADMAP/README drift**, not a separate coincidence.

It **was** true when written and was fixed **the same day** by `9343827b` (2026-07-29), the Directory-Structure rewrite, incidentally. **(e) collapses from "higher-severity than a stray directory" to (d)-class**: one `README.md`. *(The severity argument it made — "a wrong entry in a permissions table is the kind of fact an agent obeys" — is sound and now applies to **(f)** instead.)*

      - **🔴 THE FIRST CUT'S ESCAPED-DIALECT GUARD WAS INERT — caught by the adversarial review, fixed in `400652aa`.** `/(?<!_)\_\_([^_]+)\_\_(?!_)/` guards on `_`, but an escaped run is `\ _ \ _ \ _ \ _`, so the character adjoining a `\_\_` delimiter is a **backslash** and the trailing lookahead **could never fire** — measured 0 of 36 escaped cases. **The comment above it claimed a protection that did not exist, which is worse than the missing protection.** Reachable, not theoretical: **10 corpus segments** carry escaped runs with `hasApiMarkers === false`, including escaped blanks like `(a) 612 g = \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_ mg`; they survived on the luck of holding a single run. The first cut also had **zero tests for the escaped dialect**. Fixed with `(?<!\\?_)`/`(?!\\?_)`. **Generalises: when a guard protects a second dialect, write the guard in THAT dialect's alphabet — and test it there, or you have written a comment, not a guard.**

Corpus blank runs are length 4–15; every length-2 run is a real term.

That is not what happened.** Both finders were **Fable** (its regex and missed-sites lenses); Opus's regex lens did not find it, and **Opus's missed-sites lens never reported at all**. **So this is convergence across two LENSES, not across two MODELS** — still meaningful, and still the reason the finding is trustworthy, but a materially weaker claim than the one I wrote. *(⚠️ Generalises, and it is the same error the audit itself made twice: **an absent result is not a concurring result.** A silent reviewer had to be chased, not counted.)*

        - **✅ MY APPENDIX-D JUDGEMENT HELD under attack from both.** `<link>` is a **void** element in HTML, so `Appendix D` was already a text sibling and nothing was ever clickable; `<a href>` count is **7→7**, and `03-translated` still carries `<link document="m58949">`, so the pointer is regenerable. **🆕 But the reviewers found the real gap behind it:** `resolveCrossModuleHref` *can* emit `/vidauki/D` and is wired **only** into `buildKeyTermsItems` — so **appendix references in body prose have NEVER been navigable**, and **17 raw `<link document=` instances remain in published pages**. → logged with §C30 as the same "published output predates the current renderer" family.

      index is partial and an `approved` row is never superseded by a later save. The second
      write therefore took the UPDATE branch and **overwrote an editor's text while the
      reconciliation gate reported success** — because that gate counts plan buckets, not DB
      rows. Now refused (exit 4) in both dry-run and apply. *A reconciliation that counts its own
      bookkeeping cannot see a loss that happens downstream of it* — and the four task tests were
      structurally unable to see it, because a temp DB starts empty and never takes the UPDATE
      branch. **A green suite was the expected result here, not evidence.**
  - **⚠️ [LEAD] gates before any of it runs** — **A2 off-box DB backup restore-tested** (after the
    re-MT the snapshot is the only copy of the editorial work outside a gitignored SQLite file on
    one host), editorial server stopped, backup cron paused. Spec §4.
  - **⚠️ The clean break must clear `05-publication/<track>/` before re-rendering, and capture the
    published filenames FIRST.** Re-MT re-translates titles → slugs → filenames, and C9's
    prune-on-rename is still unfixed here (the chem ch10 duplicate is on disk today). Regenerating
    from empty makes the tree authoritative **and closes one of C9's three efni tasks as a side
    effect** — but the before/after file lists **are** the old→new slug map vefur needs for
    redirects, and clearing destroys the old names permanently. Spec §12. Surfaced while retiring the `chemistry-reader-tags` skill: the CNXML migration removed the *tools* of the Markdown-and-`:::`-directives era but left **consumers of that era still on live code paths**, operating on current data under retired assumptions. **This is deliberately NOT filed as dead code (item 22).** Dead code is unreachable and therefore harmless; the artifacts below are *reachable*, and two of them can produce a wrong or empty result **while every test stays green**. That is a correctness class, which is why this is P1 and item 22 is P3.
  - **The triage axis — one question per artifact: *can this silently produce a wrong result on current data?*** That, not "is it still referenced", is what should order the work. Applying it to what has been found so far:
  - **(a) ⚠️ HIGHEST — `hasApiMarkers` in `tools/cnxml-inject.js:1250` infers a segment's ERA from its own translated text, and it is the DEFAULT path.** It regex-sniffs for marker shapes to decide whether a segment is API-era; when false, three blocks of Markdown-era converters fire (`*text*`→italics, `~text~`→sub, `^text^`→sup, `~*t*~`→sub+emphasis, at `:1420`/`:1481`/`:1553`). The code comment states the reason plainly — those converters "would create false-positive markup from translated content (chemical formulas, etc.)" — i.e. **this guard is live code written to stop stale code from corrupting current data.** Measured over `02-mt-output` (30,932 segments): **22,163 (71.7%) have `hasApiMarkers` = FALSE**, because ordinary prose carries no inline markers, so the legacy path is the *majority* path, not an edge case; **139 of those (0.45% of all segments) also contain text matching a legacy shape** and are the concrete inspection list (approximate — the shape regex will have false positives; it is a starting set, not a defect count).
    - **⚠️ This is the SAME defect class the project already banned.** CLAUDE.md § *Extract-Inject-Render* carries the C13 durable rule (adopted 2026-07-27): *never decide inject behaviour from translated text, because **every segment is independently editable** and the test silently stops matching the first time an editor revises one side — with the whole suite still green.* `hasApiMarkers` does exactly that: an editor deleting the last `[[i:]]` from a segment flips it from "API-era" to "legacy-era" and silently arms the Markdown converters on modern text.
    - **⚠️ LIVE, NOT LATENT — inject has already run over every affected module.** *(🔴 **The module list in this paragraph was FALSIFIED 2026-08-07 and is kept as the original claim.** Measured by actual converter firing rather than shape match: **10 modules**, not 9. `ch03/m68700`, `ch09/m68750` and `ch17/m68825` **never fire**; `ch01/m68670` (4 segments), `liffraedi-2e ch03/m66440`, `orverufraedi ch01/m58782` and `ch05/m58805` **do** and are absent below. Per-module counts also differ: 62/39/24/9/16, not 61/36/24/5/3. → §C16's audit block.)* All **9** real modules holding a legacy-shape segment have `03-translated/` output. Concentrated in chemistry ch01: `m68674` (61 segments), `m68683` (36), `m68690` (24), `m68664` (5), `m68667` (3), then singles in ch03 `m68700`, ch08 `m68747`, ch09 `m68750`, ch17 `m68825` (plus 5 in `__e2e-fixture__`). **This is the audit's starting set.** ⚠️ Injected ≠ proven corrupted — a shape match is not a converter match, and the output may be benign; **verifying whether any produced wrong markup is task one**, and it is cheap because the module list is this short.
    - **⚠️ FIX DIRECTION IS UNRESOLVED — do not inherit one from this entry.** The tempting design ("hoist the guard out of the per-segment path and read the per-module `*-provenance.json` oracle instead") **rests on a premise that does not hold**: the in-code comment claims formats never mix, but **136 of 247 module files contain BOTH the `{{…}}` and `[[…]]` marker families** ( `{{…}}`-only 9 · `[[…]]`-only 55 · neither 47). Both families are API-era, so this does not by itself prove two *eras* coexist in one module — but it does mean **a per-module property cannot be assumed**, and a per-module oracle may be unable to answer a per-segment question. **Establish which case holds BEFORE designing the fix**; the C1d note above is the standing warning that a register's fix *design* can be wrong while its root cause is right.
  - **(b) `terminologyService.importFromKeyTerms` — a live HEAD_EDITOR route that cannot import anything.** Parses `:::definition{term="…"}` from `*-key-terms.md`; the render path has emitted HTML since the CNXML migration (**0 such `.md` repo-wide vs 27 `*key-terms*.html`**), and it returns `{success: true, added: 0}`, so total failure is shaped like a clean no-op. **Was logged under item 22 on 2026-07-29 and is now owned HERE** — item 22's entry is reduced to a pointer, so this is single-homed. Carries a second, independent defect: it builds a **`ch`-prefixed publication path** (`chapters/ch03`) where publication dirs are BARE (`chapters/03`) — the **fourth** instance of the two-conventions trap and the first that is neither commented nor test-pinned; **it survives fixing the format**, so both must be addressed. Do **not** "fix" this by regenerating Markdown — the `:::` system is retired, not broken.
  - **(c) `server/routes/status.js:1264` reads `books/<book>/chapters/<ch>/files.json`** — the retired review model — behind an `existsSync` that is now permanently false (`find books -name files.json` → **0**). Dead branch in a live endpoint plus a phantom `filesData` field in the response contract. *Cosmetic: cannot produce a wrong answer, only a permanently absent one.* The matching `books/**/chapters/**/files.json` write-grant was removed from `.claude/settings.json` on 2026-07-29.
  - **(d) `books/*/for-align/`** — Matecat Align staging, 1 file, retired with the external alignment step. Cosmetic; confirm nothing reads it before removing.

On disk it holds **1 file**, while Pass 2 output goes to `04-localized-content/` — and `.claude/settings.json` grants write to `books/**/04-localized-content/**` but **not** to `04-localization/**`, so the permissions table and the enforced grant already disagree. Either the directory is a live working stage the settings file wrongly denies, or it is retired and CLAUDE.md documents a stage that no longer exists. **A wrong entry in a permissions table is higher-severity than a stray directory** — it is the kind of fact an agent obeys. Resolve the direction first, then fix whichever side is wrong (per § *One source of truth*: fix document B, do not log it as a to-do in A).
  - **⚠️ CORRECTION 2026-07-30 — the markdown family is the EDITOR'S CURRENT TOOLBAR
    VOCABULARY, not residue. The table below mislabels it "pre-API".** Verified in the code:
    `server/public/js/segment-editor.js:972-977` binds the toolbar to `**` (Ctrl+B, *Feitletrað*),
    `*` (Ctrl+I, *Skáletrað*), **`__` (Ctrl+T, *Hugtak* — a TERM marker, not bold)**, `++` (U),
    `~` (subscript), `^` (superscript); `:2616` inserts a glossary term as `__term__`;
    `tools/cnxml-inject.js:1484` maps `__x__` → `<term>` alongside `{{term}}…{{/term}}` and
    `[[term:text|id]]` — **all three dialects are accepted**; and `:1770-1790` renders each in the
    preview. **Consequence for C16(a), and it is the important one: the mixed-dialect state does
    not decay — it REGENERATES.** Extraction emits `[[…]]`, the editor writes markdown, so the
    first Ctrl+T after a re-MT puts markdown back into the faithful file. **A re-MT therefore does
    NOT retire `hasApiMarkers`**; only changing what the editor writes, or what inject accepts,
    would. Any future editor-facing tag redesign should be scoped together with (a) for that
    reason. *(This also caused a real defect: the C16 re-attach rules classed the markdown family
    as "úrelt snið" and would have told an editor their current formatting was obsolete on 6 of
    the 7 toolbar buttons. Fixed and test-pinned in `scripts/lib/segment-edit-reattach-rules.js`.)*
  - **🔴 CONFIRMED LIVE READER-VISIBLE INSTANCE of (a), found 2026-07-30 — and the re-MT does
    NOT fix it.** Until now §C16(a) was a census plus a predicted defect class. Here is an actual
    one, in published output:
    - `books/orverufraedi/02-mt-output/ch01/m58782-segments.is.md:197` is a fill-in-the-blank
      exercise: `Haeckel lagði til að bæta ríkjunum ________ og ________ við þróunartré sitt.`
      It carries **no bracket marker**, so `hasApiMarkers` is false and the Markdown-era
      converter at `tools/cnxml-inject.js:1484` fires. Its regex `__([^_]+)__` takes the last two
      underscores of the first blank, the text ` og `, and the first two of the second blank, and
      wraps them: published output reads
      `______<dfn class="term"> og  (e.  and )</dfn>______`. A student sees the word "og" marked
      as a glossary term with an empty English gloss.
    - **Reach:** 2 modules (`orverufraedi` ch01 `m58782`, ch05 `m58805`), 2 published pages
      (`1-fill-in-the-blank.html`, `5-fill-in-the-blank.html`). ⚠️ Chemistry's `__` hits are
      **not** this defect — they are MathML `<mo>_____</mo>` blanks, protected during inject.
      Verified individually; a regex census alone would have mis-scored them.
    - **⚠️ Re-extraction + re-MT does NOT clear it.** The blanks come from the source CNXML, and
      the segment will still carry no bracket markers afterwards, so the guard still resolves
      false and the converter still fires. **This is a code defect, not stale data** — the
      distinction §C16 draws between "dead code" and "partially-live legacy", now demonstrated.
    - **It also fixes the fix direction for (a).** The converter cannot simply be deleted (the
      editor's Ctrl+T writes `__term__`, so real term markers would stop converting) and cannot
      be left (it mangles blanks). The resolution is to make the editor's term marker
      unambiguous — id-anchored `[[term:text|id]]` rather than `__text__` — after which `:1481-1485`
      can go entirely. **That is the same work as the planned editor-facing tag redesign, which
      is why the two should be scoped together.**

    | Family | Files | Status |
    |---|---|---|
    | `{{i}}` / `{{b}}` | **77 — all in `efnafraedi-2e`** | stale residue in un-re-extracted modules of the oldest book; **this is what the `!hasApiMarkers` back-compat exists for** |
    | `{{term}}` / `{{fn}}` | 131 | legacy but **always parsed, never gated** — a different class, do not conflate |

    | markdown `*` `**` `__` `~` `^` `++` | 38 | pre-API; `++text++` now at **0** |

That is a far safer fix shape than editing the guard, and it should be evaluated first. **The deliverable is a CLASSIFICATION, not a delete list:** fully dead → remove · partially live → decide · load-bearing back-compat → keep **and pin with a test**.

What was recorded as a *don't-break-it* note is, read the other way, a **standing correctness exposure**: the same branch that protects 77 old files is the branch 71.7% of all segments take. **The new information in C16 is the blast radius and the direction, not the existence of the back-compat.** Read that memory before scoping.

The 7 `para:fs-id*` rows are the ones carrying work the new MT does not have — `__term__` markup and restructured sentences. **So the hand pass is realistically ~7 judgements, not 12** — and the rendered document already tells the editor to make exactly that call.
