For chemistry that is right — the snapshot is taken *before* the re-MT. **For biology it is not: the re-MT already happened, so "Fyrri vélþýðing" here IS the current baseline.** The renderer is not wrong, its assumed sequencing just does not hold for this book.
    - **▶ WHAT THIS LEAVES OPEN.** Capture is done and cannot be undone, so the remaining C17 question is no longer *"will we lose these?"* but *"is the ~7-judgement hand pass worth doing before or after the next re-MT?"* — a scheduling call, not a data-integrity one. **Option (2) (widen C16's clean break to include `liffraedi-2e`) is unaffected and still open.**
  - **✅ A4 RUNBOOK L3 EXECUTED 2026-08-07 — FIRST TIME EVER, and it found 10 hand repairs a whole-book `--force` re-MT would have reverted.** This was listed as an unexecuted prerequisite for months. Method: `git log` over `books/*/02-mt-output/`, classify MT runs from hand repairs, then check per file whether the repair still stands. **Provenance cannot answer this** — a hand edit does not rewrite `*-provenance.json` — so git is the index, as [[mt-output-hand-repairs]] says.
    - **6 hand-repair commits touching 13 files. 11 repairs still stand; 2 were ALREADY silently overwritten by later MT runs** (`appendices/m68866` by `7658de89`; `ch16/m68818` by `0b2defd1`). **The hazard is not hypothetical — it has already fired twice**, unnoticed, which is the strongest argument for running L3 before any re-MT rather than after.
    - **🔴 Of the 11 surviving repairs, only ONE is protected by an MT lock** (`ch05/chapter-metadata`, from `827424da` — the `Varmefnafræði`→`Varma**e**fnafræði` missing-*a* fix). **The other 10 sit in UNLOCKED modules and would be reverted**: `ch05/m68723`, `m68724`, `m68726`, `m68727` (same commit), `ch14/m68803`, `ch18/m68831`, `ch19/m68842` (`7439d07e`, null-byte degree-sign repair), `ch17/m68823` (`d440b5b8`, `[[docref:]]` restore), `appendices/m68865` (`334d800d`, marker relabel), and **`liffraedi-2e ch03/m66441`** (`4e5be912`, the *Fitusýrur→Lípíð* title fix — which is also a C9 rename source). **Any clean-break runbook must protect these explicitly; nothing in the tooling does.**
  - **✅ CHEMISTRY SNAPSHOT TAKEN 2026-08-07 (read-only, prod tree unchanged) — and the row count has MOVED since the 2026-08-03 measurement.** `pipeline-output/c16-chemistry-snapshot/` (gitignored — carries `editor_id`; repo is public) + `~/c16-efnafraedi-2e-snapshot-2026-08-07.json` on prod. **151 rows across the same 7 modules, all 151 restorable, all 151 carrying full `en` + `mtAtSnapshot` context.** The register's figures were **148 rows / 147 restorable (1 `rejected`)**; there is now **no `rejected` row at all**. Module scope is unchanged. Exit-4 re-checked: **no duplicate restore keys, will not fire.** ⚠️ **Re-run both queries immediately before any run — this is the third different row count in five days.**
  - **🔴 CORRECTION — `--force` DOES NOT OVERRIDE AN MT LOCK. It is explicitly IGNORED.** `api-translate.js:1275-1281` prints `🔒 … LOCKED (editing started) — MT re-run refused (--force ignored)` and `continue`s; the dry-run summary says `7 of these locked — --force refused`. **The only way to re-MT a locked module is to DELETE its `.locked` marker** — there is no flag. *(An earlier reading in this session asserted the break "necessarily runs with `--force`, and `--force` is what reverts the hand repairs". Both halves were wrong: `--force` cannot touch a locked module, and the hand-repair hazard lands on the UNLOCKED ones.)* **This is a strong safety property: the tool refuses exactly the 8 modules that hold editorial work** (7 chemistry + `liffraedi-2e m66443`). The destructive step in any runbook is therefore a deliberate `rm` of lock markers, which should be its own gated step with its own consent.
  - **💰 COST MEASURED 2026-08-07 via `--force --dry-run`:** chemistry **163 modules · 3,387,920 chars · ~33,879 ISK**; biology **12 modules · 188,567 chars · ~1,886 ISK**. **~35,765 ISK for both books.** *(Without `--force` both books estimate 0 ISK — everything is already translated — so a costed decision requires the forced dry-run.)*
  - **Sequencing.** This blocks the biology re-MT, which in turn gates **[LEAD] queue ①** (the held C13 ch03 sync). *[The onward link to **P0-3/P0-4** is an inference from the queue's own ordering, not an established dependency — treat it as a prompt to check, not as a load-bearing chain.]* It does **not** block any chemistry work. *[severity: correctness/data-loss · blocks: biology re-MT → C13 reader delivery]*

- **C18 · 🔴 Competing glossary terms collide SILENTLY — row order decides which Icelandic word is used** — **[CODE]** — **P1** — *found 2026-08-04 while the lead and I reconciled what `status` means in the terminology tables (→ §C14 ②, which owns the data-model half). This entry owns the code defect.*
  - **✅ GUARD MERGED 2026-08-04 — [PR #349](https://github.com/SigurdurVilhelmsson/namsbokasafn-efni/pull/349), main `3a88f536`** (branch `fix/c18-glossary-collision-guard`) — 12 commits, 7 tasks, each independently reviewed, plus a whole-branch adversarial review on Opus and one fix wave. **It resolves NO term** — that stays §C14 ②'s per-book editorial work (chemistry = 124 decisions). ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-07: "editorial work" overstates chemistry's share — its 124 are head-form/synonym pairs that a mechanical rule resolves in full, leaving 0. → §C14 ②.** *(This does not weaken the guard's value: its job was to make the competition visible rather than silent, and it is what allowed the shape to be diagnosed at all.)* What it does:
    - `tools/lib/glossary-collisions.js` — one pure detector, the single definition of "competition"; both consumers and the gate share it.
    - **Render path reports, never decides differently.** `buildGlossaryMap` returns `{map, collisions}`; both consumers warn once per book (`cnxml-inject.js` on resolver cache-miss, `cnxml-fidelity-check.js` which was silently discarding the report). **Byte-neutral, proven twice**: re-injecting chemistry ch1 left `03-translated/` byte-identical, and the final review imported base-vs-HEAD `math-label-substitute.js` side by side over the real glossary — identical 602-entry Maps, identical `resolveLabel` output across all 706 keys.
    - **MT path stops sending contradictions.** `formatGlossary` omits **all** candidates of a competing headword (never one — picking would be the defect) plus comma-list values; **27 of 617 chemistry rows, 4.4%**. Reporting rides callbacks only: that return object **is** the outbound HTTP body.
    - `tools/validate-glossary.js` + `npm run validate:glossary`, with per-book baselines that record the **chosen term** per key, not a count. A changed `chosen` on an unchanged candidate set fails the gate — that is the drift worth catching. Baselines are **worklists**: they should shrink as §C14 ② resolves terms.
    - A `npm test` regression fence, mutation-verified by two agents on two different keys. **Absence of a baseline FAILS**, so biology's competitions announce themselves on onboarding rather than being absorbed.
  - **⚠️ THE SEVERITY IN THIS ENTRY WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED ABOVE** (see the dated bullet under §C14 ②). Render exposure measured **0 in every book**; the live damage was the MT path. Method + numbers → [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-04-c18-glossary-collision-guard-design.md`](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-04-c18-glossary-collision-guard-design.md) §1.1a.
  - **Still open after this branch:** the per-book `approved` flips (§C14 ② step 2/3) and the export's producer/provenance guard (step 4). **This branch does NOT make it safe to re-enable the glossary export** — prod still carries the uncommitted `#CONTAINED-2026-08-03#` edit, and lifting it re-runs the unattended write within 2 h.
  - **No reader-visible change.** Substitution runs at inject time, so nothing reaches readers without a re-inject, a re-render **and** a manual vefur sync.
  - **The mechanism.** `tools/lib/math-label-substitute.js:15` `buildGlossaryMap` builds a **`Map` keyed on lowercase English** and writes every approved term into it:
    ```js
    for (const t of terms) {
      if (t.status !== 'approved') continue;
      const en = (t.english || '').trim().toLowerCase();
      const is = (t.icelandic || '').trim();
      if (en && is) map.set(en, is);   // ← LAST WRITE WINS, silently
    }
    ```
    `exportBookGlossary` emits **one entry per translation**, so a headword with two approved translations produces two entries with the same `english`. The second `map.set` overwrites the first. Ordering is `ORDER BY h.english COLLATE NOCASE ASC` then insertion order within the headword group — i.e. effectively **row id**.
  - **⚠️ This is not "undecided", it is "already decided, arbitrarily, and invisibly."** Approved terms are substituted into published CNXML/HTML via `substituteMathLabels`. Row order picks the winner; it is stable enough between runs that nobody would notice, and it would flip on a re-import, a row-id change, or a collation change — a content change with no commit, no review and no diff in any source file.
    - **⚠️ CORRECTED 2026-08-04 BY MEASUREMENT — this bullet previously said "for `atom` the reader currently gets whichever of *frumeind* / *atóm* happens to sort later." That is FALSE today, for every book, and the correction relocates C18 rather than shrinking it.** Substitution only touches exact-match leaf `<m:mtext>`/`<m:mi>` labels: chemistry has **133** such tokens across 149 `.cnxml`, organic **11** across 342, biology **10** across 259 — element symbols, units, short axis labels — and **none of the competing headwords is one of them**, so **render-path exposure is currently 0 in all three books**. Nor is the overlay the protection: `lifraen-efnafraedi` holds the same 13 collisions with **no `math-label-map.json` at all** and is still 0. **The live damage is the MT path** — `formatGlossary` sends the whole approved glossary regardless of math labels, so every chemistry `api-translate` run sends **13 contradictory instructions** to Málstaður. Render is a trap that fires the day a competing headword *is* a math label, which nothing prevents; **biology is where it gets dangerous** (3,817 competitions, no overlay file). Measurements + method → [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-04-c18-glossary-collision-guard-design.md`](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-04-c18-glossary-collision-guard-design.md) §1.1a. *Same lesson as §C14 ②'s (b): I assigned severity from the code path without checking whether the data ever traverses it.*
    - **⚠️ The mechanism PREDATES the Íðorðabankinn import**, so C18 is not gated on the export being switched back on. `tools/merge-glossary.js`'s homograph branch (`:263-289`) mints same-key/different-value `approved` pairs, and `merge-glossary.js` wrote **every committed glossary** — chemistry carries **13 collisions on disk today**. Two independent producers, one defect, because the defect is in the **consumer's** assumption that English is a unique key. Also: **`pos` cannot discriminate homographs** — `inferPos` (`:216`) is a suffix heuristic that tagged *miðlæg teningslaga* a verb for ending in `-a`.
  - **A second consumer has the same input and a different failure.** `tools/lib/malstadur-api.js:200` `formatGlossary` filters `approvedOnly` and emits `{sourceWord, targetWord}` pairs — so it sends **both** `atom→frumeind` **and** `atom→atóm` to the Málstaður API in one request: contradictory instructions for a single source word, resolved by whatever the API does with duplicates. **MT quality is affected, not just rendering.**
  - **Scale, measured on prod 2026-08-04:** **7,601 of 20,272 headwords** carry >1 approved translation. Per book (competitions, not rows): **chemistry 129 · physics 1,547 · mathematics 2,327 · biology 3,817**.
  - **Worked example — `protein`, which a biology text uses on nearly every page, has FOUR approved candidates:** `eggjahvíta, prótín` · `hvíta` · `hvíta, prótein` · `prótín`. Row order picks one. Nothing records that a choice was made.
  - **🟠 A SECOND, INDEPENDENT DEFECT ON THE SAME PATH: 1,622 of 28,903 `icelandic` values are THEMSELVES COMMA-SEPARATED LISTS**, and nothing splits them. `anion` → `anjón, mínusjón, neijón` · `power` → `fjöldatala, stétt, styrkleiki` · `reversible` → `afturkvæmur, jafnhverfur, umhverfur`. Per subject: **biology 709 · mathematics 662 · physics 230 · chemistry 21**. `buildGlossaryMap` stores the value verbatim (`t.icelandic.trim()`), and `substituteMathLabels`'s `FORBIDDEN_XML` check does not reject commas — so if such a row wins, **the rendered math label becomes the whole list.** The MT side is worse-scoped: `formatGlossary` sends `targetWord: "anjón, mínusjón, neijón"` to Málstaður as a *glossary instruction*, affecting all prose translation rather than just math labels. **Scope honestly:** the render exposure is confined to exact-match leaf `<m:mtext>`/`<m:mi>` labels and is outranked by `math-label-map.json`, so chemistry's 21 are largely masked today — **biology's 709 arrive with the biology onboarding.**
  - **⚠️ A tempting inference that is WRONG, checked and recorded so it is not re-raised.** I expected Íðorðabankinn's known internal errors (→ `[[idordabanki-biology-seeding]]`: `polypeptide` → their typo `fjölpeðtíð`, `phospholipid` → minority-form `fosfórlípíð`) to show up here as a *typo competing with a correct form*. They do not: **each of those headwords has exactly ONE translation**, so there is no collision — the wrong term is simply the only term and substitutes unconditionally. **That is a separate problem from this one** (single-candidate quality, owned by the per-term adoption rule in that memory) and a determinism guard will never surface it. Do not conflate the two.
  - **The fix is independent of all editorial work and should land FIRST.** Make the collision loud instead of silent: on a duplicate English key with differing Icelandic, either fail (matching the project's fail-loud posture) or warn and pick deterministically. ⚠️ **Do not "fix" it by picking a stable tiebreak and stopping** — a deterministic-but-arbitrary choice is still an unreviewed editorial decision; the guard's job is to surface the competition so §C14 ②'s per-book flip can resolve it. **`buildGlossaryMap` has tests** (`tools/__tests__/math-label-substitute.test.js`), so this is a small TDD change.
  - **⚠️ The two `<!-- SEG -->`-style traps do NOT apply here, but one does:** the substitution runs at **render** time, so a fix reaches readers only after a re-render **and** a manual vefur sync (CLAUDE.md § *Content delivery*). Fixing the code changes no published page by itself. *[severity: correctness · blocks: reader-visible term selection; MT priming quality]*

- **C19 · ✅ MERGED 2026-08-05 — `GET /api/books/:bookId/download` threw on every call; `archiver` 8 is ESM-only and the CJS `require` no longer yields a function** — **[CODE]** — **P1** — *found 2026-08-04 while re-establishing that `main` is green; NOT caused by that day's merges.* **[PR #356](https://github.com/SigurdurVilhelmsson/namsbokasafn-efni/pull/356) → `main` `cc7619ae`** [branch deleted on merge]; all five CI checks green **bound to the merged head sha** (verified via `check-runs`, not the summary line). **✅ DEPLOYED 2026-08-05** — `./scripts/deploy.sh` on prod (`7c53336d..e4937396`) with the `systemctl restart` run by a human at the password prompt, service `active` since **14:46:07Z**. **Verified ON THE BOX rather than inferred from the deploy log**: `books.js` carries the named import and `new ZipArchive`, and under prod's own Node **v22.23.1** `require('archiver')` yields an object whose `ZipArchive` constructs with working `file`/`pipe`/`finalize` — the `require(ESM)` fact the whole fix rests on, now confirmed on the machine that runs it. ⚠️ **Still NOT proven end-to-end**: nothing has exercised the route through `requireAuth` with a real editor session. The cheapest real proof is one download click in the UI. No data-op, no re-render, no vefur sync needed (editor-facing only).
  - **Confirmed, not inferred.** `server/node_modules/archiver/package.json` is `"version": "8.0.0"`, `"type": "module"`, `"exports": "./index.js"`, and its namespace exports **classes, not a factory**: `{ Archiver, JsonArchive, TarArchive, ZipArchive }`. Under Node 22's `require(ESM)` support, `server/routes/books.js:14`'s `const archiver = require('archiver')` therefore binds an **object**, and `books.js:457`'s `archiver('zip', { zlib: { level: 9 } })` throws `TypeError: archiver is not a function`. Reproduced directly (`typeof` → `object`) *and* observed as a real thrown stack in the suite log.
  - **⚠️ THE SUITE IS GREEN ANYWAY, AND THAT IS THE FINDING.** `server/__tests__/books-routes.test.js` settles its promise inside a `res.setHeader` shim the moment `Content-Disposition` is set (`books.js:455`) — **two lines before the `archiver(...)` call**. So the appendices/numeric-chapter assertions genuinely pass, the route then explodes, and the `TypeError` surfaces only as an `ERROR: Download error` log line the runner does not fail on. The tests assert **zip *naming***; nothing asserts the zip is ever produced. *Textbook `[[engineering-lessons]]`: the dangerous check is the one that passes for the wrong reason.*
  - **Blast radius is one call site** — `require('archiver')` appears exactly once across `server/`, `tools/`, `scripts/`. Editor-facing (`requireAuth`), **not reader-facing**: nothing in the publication path uses it.
  - **⚠️ CORRECTED 2026-08-05 — this entry's own "the choice is not obvious" framing was WRONG, and the wrong half was the expensive one.** It claimed adopting v8 meant "a rewrite of the handler's archive block, not a one-line import change" because "the constructor/stream contract differs from the v7 factory". **Measured, not inferred: the contract does not differ.** `ZipArchive extends Archiver`, and `Archiver.prototype` still carries `file`, `directory`, `glob`, `append`, `finalize`, `abort`, `pointer`; the instance is still a Transform, so `.pipe(res)` works unchanged; `finalize()` still returns a promise. **The adopt was two lines** — the top-of-file `require` becomes `const { ZipArchive } = require('archiver')`, and the one construction in the download handler becomes `new ZipArchive({ zlib: { level: 9 } })` — with every other call in the handler untouched. *(No `file:line` here on purpose: the bullet above cites `books.js:14`/`:457` for the **pre-fix** tree, and this change itself moved both.)* *Recorded because the error is instructive and is the same shape as the #341 `npm view` mistake: an API-break severity was assumed from the release notes' "ESM-only, exports classes" rather than measured against the prototype.*
  - **Option (2) — pin back to `archiver@^7` — is dead, and staying on v8 is the reason.** Downgrading re-opens the `archiver → readdir-glob → minimatch → brace-expansion` advisory chain **#350 just bumped `overrides` for**, which would also walk straight into the standing trap that *an `overrides` pin can itself become the vulnerability*. Staying on v8 leaves the server tree's `overrides` untouched. Verified against the **server** tree (the prod, internet-facing one), not root.
  - **Two runtime facts the fix rests on, both confirmed rather than reasoned.** (a) **Prod runs Node v22.23.1** — `systemctl cat ritstjorn` → `ExecStart=/usr/bin/node index.js`, `WorkingDirectory=…/server`; `require(ESM)` is unflagged from 22.12, so the named import is safe on the box, not just on dev. (b) **`.file()` on a missing path emits `warning` (`ENOENT`), not `error`** — `finalize()` still resolves and nothing goes uncaught, so the adopt introduces **no new** need for an `archive.on('error')` listener and the handler's existing `fs.existsSync` guard keeps its meaning. *(Had it emitted `error`, an unhandled stream `'error'` would take the process down rather than the request — which is why this was checked before shipping.)* ⚠️ **Read that as a v7→v8 DELTA claim, which is what it is — not as "archive errors here are warn-only".** They are not: a file unlinked *after* the `existsSync`/`statSync` guard but *before* archiver's lazy read stream opens emits `error`, which the handler's `try/catch` does **not** catch, and that kills the process. That is **pre-existing and byte-identical under v7** — verified against the v7.0.1 tarball — so it is not this change's defect, but this change **makes it reachable again** by reviving a route that had been failing closed. Logged as **§C20**, which is the correct home for it.
  - **The test was the actual deliverable.** A new `describe` runs the handler to **completion** — settling on the response stream's `end` (success) or `res.json` (the route's own catch) instead of on `Content-Disposition` — and parses the returned bytes' **central directory**, asserting one entry per `.html` file in the chapter dir. Byte length + `PK` magic would not have been enough: an empty archive satisfies both. **Mutation-checked** — both new tests fail with the `TypeError` on unfixed `books.js` and pass after. The pre-existing `Content-Disposition`-settling block was **left intact**; it pins C1c appendices zip-naming and deleting it would have silently dropped that guard. Fixture is `orverufraedi` `pub-mt-preview` ch01 — the smallest publication chapter dir the route will actually serve (~120 KB over 10 files; `efnafraedi-2e` ch `00` is smaller but chapter 0 is rejected), all 10 files **committed and byte-identical on disk and in git**, so CI sees the same fixture. The expected entry list is derived from disk at run time, so content churn cannot make it flaky. *[severity: correctness · editor-visible 500 · blocks: nothing]*

- **C21 · 🔴🔴 The absent-baseline hole is MANUFACTURED BY ORDINARY BOOK ONBOARDING, and two books are ONE `mkdir` from an ungated, pushed glossary write** — **[CODE]** — **P1, ahead of C20** — *surfaced 2026-08-05 by a targeted sweep after the `stjornufraedi` dir removal; **every fact below was re-measured first-hand on prod before being written here**, per the lesson §C20 records.*
  - **🆕 ✅ THE GATE HAS NOW FIRED ON A REAL BOOK IN PRODUCTION — first observed 2026-08-08, incidentally, while verifying §C36 B2.** `/api/health` lists **four** books in the export loop, not three: `edlisfraedi-2e` → **`refused-absent-baseline`**, `since 2026-08-08T12:00:01.290Z`. **Traced, not guessed:** PR #371 (`c656a0e0`) + `75e4b430` added `books/edlisfraedi-2e/glossary/.gitkeep`, which reached prod at 10:27:49. **That 12:00:01Z tick was the FIRST to see it is a measurement, not mtime arithmetic:** `since` is `withSince`-carried — it advances only when a book's outcome *changes* and is otherwise held (this block records that property at the 2026-08-05 second-tick entry, where it is what makes D6's deadline real) — so `since: 2026-08-08T12:00:01.290Z` **is** the first-observation stamp for this outcome. The 10:00Z tick ran before the directory existed and the 14:00/16:00/18:00/20:00 ticks did not move it. **This is the 2026-08-07 lead decision — *physics gets a `glossary/` dir but no adoption* — executing exactly as designed, and the refusal is the correct outcome, not a regression.** Two consequences: ① **§C21's gate is no longer sandbox-only.** This entry and §C14 ③ both record that the post-deploy ticks could not demonstrate it "because no book is in the state it guards", and that the proof was a sandbox on prod. It has now run unattended, on a real book — the §C11(b) precedent (a shipped detector that had never actually run went unnoticed for 13 days) is what makes a first live firing worth pinning. ② **A SECOND D6 deadline now exists, and it is NOT the one this file elsewhere quotes:** `edlisfraedi-2e` clocks from 2026-08-08 12:00Z, so it flips not-ok around **~2026-08-15 12:00Z**, a week after the **~2026-08-12 14:00Z** the other three share. Per §C14 ②'s ruling, letting both fire is correct for deliberately unadopted books. ⚠️ **And this spends §C14 ②'s measurement that `edlisfraedi-2e` "is silently outside the export with no clock and no alarm"** — it is now inside, clocked and alarmed. Evidence → [test-results/b2-prod-population-2026-08.md](../../test-results/b2-prod-population-2026-08.md) § *An unrelated observation*.
  - **⚠️ THIS REFRAMES §C14 ③. `stjornufraedi` was never the hole — it was its LEAST dangerous instance**, and removing its directory (2026-08-05, correct and still correct) closed one occupancy, not the hole. The register's framing — "`stjornufraedi` is the ONLY book in that state" — was true as a *census* and misleading as a *risk model*, because it measured who was standing in the hole rather than what digs it.
  - **📊 Measured on prod 2026-08-05** (`registered_books` LEFT JOIN `book_subject_mapping` — note the mapping keys on **`book_id`, not slug**; a slug-keyed query errors out):

    | book | subject mapping | `glossary/` dir | state |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `efnafraedi-2e` | chemistry | ✓ 6 files | gated — both gates armed |
    | `liffraedi-2e` | biology | ✓ 2 files | gated |
    | `lifraen-efnafraedi` | organic-chemistry | ✓ 2 files | gated |
    | `stjornufraedi` | **none** | ✗ | **safe** — no dir *and* no subject |
    | `orverufraedi` | microbiology | ✗ | 🔴 **LOADED GUN** |
    | `edlisfraedi-2e` | physics | ✗ | 🔴 **LOADED GUN** |

  - **(a) 🔴 `orverufraedi` and `edlisfraedi-2e` are strictly MORE dangerous than `stjornufraedi` ever was.** Both hold a `book_subject_mapping` row, so **the `refused-no-mapping` early return that kept `stjornufraedi` dormant does not protect them.** If a `glossary/` directory appears for either — by any means — the next 2-hourly tick finds a mapped book with an absent baseline, **both gates are structurally inert**, and it writes, commits, pushes, exits 0 and reports `ok`. There is no early refusal in the way. **Nothing stands between them and an ungated write except the absence of a directory.**
  - **(b) 🔴 `createBookDirectories()` (`server/services/bookRegistration.js`) SCAFFOLDS an empty `glossary/` for every book it creates** — verified by reading the function: its fixed directory list contains `'glossary'` (and `'tm'`), each created with `mkdirSync(..., {recursive:true})`. It is reachable over HTTP via `POST /api/admin/books/register`. **So ordinary onboarding puts every new book into the absent-baseline state by default**, and that is almost certainly how `stjornufraedi` got there. The hole is not a legacy artefact to be cleaned up once; it is regenerated by the happy path.
  - **(c) ⚠️ The remedy the system PRINTS is the trigger.** A newly registered book emits `refused-no-mapping`, whose message tells the operator to add a `book_subject_mapping` row — which is exactly the step that converts a harmless refusal into a mapped book with an absent baseline. D6 then nags until they do it. **The alarm, the remedy text and the deadline all point the same wrong way.** Same trap §C14 ③ recorded for `stjornufraedi`, now shown to be systemic rather than one-off.
  - **(d) A `.gitkeep` precedent exists** — `books/efnafraedi-2e/glossary/.gitkeep` is tracked. Committing one for `orverufraedi` or `edlisfraedi-2e` (a plausible "stop empty dirs vanishing on clone" tidy-up) would make an absent-baseline directory **git-restorable on every clone and every deploy pull** — permanent, and re-created by the one mechanism that currently cannot recreate it.
  - **✅ FIXED, MERGED AND DEPLOYED 2026-08-05 — [PR #360](https://github.com/SigurdurVilhelmsson/namsbokasafn-efni/pull/360) → `main` `1046a09b`, live on prod 16:57:31Z — the absent baseline now FAILS CLOSED.**
    - **✅ DEMONSTRATED ON PROD'S OWN DEPLOYED CODE, not merely installed.** This gate is one that, working correctly, **never fires** — all three in-loop books hold committed files, so no amount of waiting can show it works, and the last real export (16:00:02Z) ran *before* the 16:57 deploy. That is exactly the §C11(b) trap (a shipped detector that had never run went unnoticed for 13 days), so it was exercised deliberately: `runGlossaryExport` was invoked **on prod** against a `mkdtemp` sandbox — temp `booksDir` **and** temp `projectRoot`, so neither `books/` nor `pipeline-output/` could be touched — holding one book with a `glossary/` dir and no file. Result: **no `--adopt` → exit 0, nothing written, `refused-absent-baseline`; `--force` → still nothing written; `--adopt` → written, `adopted`.** Prod verified untouched afterwards (heartbeat still `16:00:02.423Z`, status file still 3 books at `since 14:00:01.119Z`, `git status` still only the two §C3 TMX files, no sandbox leakage).
    - ⚠️ **The 18:00Z tick is a REGRESSION check, not gate proof** — expect 3 books, all `refused-producer`, `since` still `14:00:01.119Z`, `errors: 0`. The gate cannot appear in it, because nothing is in the state it guards.
  - **The mechanism:** `prev === null` used to mean *allow*; it now means **`refused-absent-baseline` unless `--adopt`**, which the cron — invoking the script bare — cannot reach. That closes the hole for **every** book at once regardless of which directories exist, making (a)–(d) harmless rather than merely unlikely. `--force` deliberately does **not** satisfy it: that flag answers "is the shrink intended", a different question about a different risk (two risks, two acknowledgements). The new outcome starts with `refused-` so `isUnresolved` and `glossaryExportHealth` pick it up by their existing **prefix** convention — no enumeration to update, which is why that convention was chosen.
    - **⚠️ A flaw in the fix, caught by TDD, worth recording.** With the gate added, an `--adopt`ed first export was still recorded as **`'wrote'`** — the routine-refresh label — because the `'adopted'` ternary keyed only on `corrupt || pv.refuse`, and an absent baseline leaves `pv.refuse` false for exactly the same reason the gate exists. So the single most consequential write for a new book, its wholly unreviewed first one, was labelled its most routine. **Identical to the `corrupt` clause bug the same line already carries a warning about** — the comment was there and the new case still walked into it. Now `corrupt || absent || pv.refuse`.
    - **⚠️ REVERSAL — do NOT delete `'glossary'` from `createBookDirectories` after all.** This entry originally called that "worth doing too". With the gate in place it is now **actively worse**: a scaffolded directory makes a new book *visible* in the export loop as a refusal (`refused-no-mapping`, or `refused-absent-baseline` once mapped), both D6-clocked; removing the scaffold would instead make every new book **silently absent from the export**, which is the failure mode this register already dislikes elsewhere (`edlisfraedi-2e is silently absent from the export entirely`). **Visible-and-refusing beats silent.** The scaffold was only dangerous while the write was ungated.
    - **The operator-facing half changed too.** `deploy.sh` gained a `refused-absent-baseline` remedy, and — more importantly — the **`refused-no-mapping` remedy was CORRECTED**: its tail promised "…after which it exports on the next tick", which was both false and *the dangerous half*, since adding the row was precisely the step that armed the ungated write. It now says the row alone does not make it export, and that the first export is a separate deliberate decision. Pinned by `scripts/__tests__/deploy-health-readout.test.mjs`, which round-trips every printed command through the real `parseArgs`.
    - **📍 Prod impact today: ZERO, and that is the point.** All three in-loop books hold committed files, so `readExisting` returns `kind:'ok'` and the new gate — which keys strictly on `kind:'absent'` — never applies to them at all; they go on refusing at the **producer** gate exactly as before. *(An earlier draft of this line said the producer gate "fires before this one". That is the wrong reason for a right conclusion: the absent check actually runs FIRST, ~18 lines ahead of `producerVerdict`. It is mutual exclusivity, not ordering, that makes the impact zero.)* `orverufraedi`/`edlisfraedi-2e` have no directory so they are not in the loop at all. The gate changes nothing until a directory appears — which is exactly the moment it is needed. **Do not read "no change on prod" as "no effect".**
    - **Test cost, recorded because it is the honest measure of how load-bearing the old behaviour was:** 12 call sites (14 test cases — one is an `it.each` of three) seeded a book with no committed file purely as a convenient fixture and expected a write. They now use a `seedRefreshable()` helper that seeds a one-term baseline, so each states its intent — *routine refresh* — instead of silently depending on the hole. One test literally pinned the defect (`writes a first export when no file exists, and returns 0`) and now pins the gate. *[severity: correctness/integrity — unattended reader-visible write · blocks: onboarding any new book safely]*

- **C22 · 🟠 The "Sækja birta HTML" download button CANNOT EVER APPEAR — it is gated on a field the service never emits** — **[CODE]** — **P2, small** — *found 2026-08-05 while trying to prove §C19 end to end; pre-existing.*
  - **Measured, not inferred.** `server/views/books.html` gates the button on `pubFaithful.complete || pubMtPreview.complete || pubLocalized.complete`, but `publicationService.getPublicationStatus()` emits **`published`**, never `complete`. Live on prod for `efnafraedi-2e` ch3: `faithful {published:true, fileCount:7}`, `mtPreview {published:true, fileCount:10}` — and `.complete` is `undefined` on all three. So the condition is `undefined || undefined || undefined`, and `#btn-download-pub` keeps `display:none` **for every book and every chapter, always**.
  - **🔴 CORRECTED 2026-08-06 — THE MECHANISM ABOVE IS WRONG, AND THE FIX IT PRESCRIBES WOULD MANUFACTURE THE BUG IT DESCRIBES. The bullet above measured an endpoint this button's code never calls.** `loadChapterStatus()` fetches **`/api/status/<book>/<chapter>`** (`server/views/books.html:2123`), *not* `/api/publication/…/status`. That route (`server/routes/status.js:1239`) builds its payload from `getStatusDataFromDb` → `pipelineStatusService.getChapterStage()`, which reads `chapter_pipeline_status` rows keyed **`publication.<track>`** and emits exactly `publication.<track>.complete` (`status.js:63`); `formatChapterStatus` then **deliberately preserves** those sub-objects onto the stage entry, under a comment that says so (`status.js:1530-1534`). Rows are written on a successful publish by `publicationService.js:237`. **`.complete` is therefore a field this payload really does emit.**
    - **Falsified against real data, not re-reasoned:** in the dev DB `efnafraedi-2e` chapters **1–5 hold `publication.mtPreview = complete`** (24 rows per track; `faithful` and `localized` all `not_started`). On that data `pubMtPreview.complete` is `true` and **`#btn-download-pub` is shown**, so *"cannot ever appear — for every book and every chapter, always"* is false as written. *(This bullet then said the prod measurement had not been made, and predicted that if prod's rows were all `not_started` the cause would be DB/disk divergence. **The measurement has now been made and the prediction was right — but it understated the scale.**)*
    - **✅✅ SETTLED ON PRODUCTION 2026-08-06 (read-only query, authorized) — a THIRD story, not either of the two on file.** The button **is** permanently hidden on prod, so **C22's conclusion stands** — but for neither stated reason. Prod's `chapter_pipeline_status` holds **16 rows in total**: `extraction complete ×8` and `mtReady complete ×8`. **There are no `publication.*` rows at all** — no book, no chapter, no track — so `getChapterStage` returns its `not_started` default, the payload emits `{complete: false}`, and the gate evaluates `false || false || false`. Meanwhile **7 published HTML files sit on disk** for `efnafraedi-2e` ch3 faithful. **The DB says nothing was ever published; the disk says it was.**
      - **🔴 The prescribed fix is therefore worse than wrong — it is a NO-OP THAT LOOKS LIKE A FIX.** `.complete` → `.published` swaps a field that is emitted-and-false for one that **is not emitted at all** on this payload. The button stays hidden either way, the PR shows a plausible diff, and the defect survives review with a green suite.
      - **The real defect is DB/disk divergence.** Those rows are written by `publicationService.js:237` on a successful publish *through the server*, gated on `rendering` being complete — and prod has no `rendering` row either. Publication reached the disk by another route. **The fix is on the write/reconcile side, or on gating the button against the disk-derived `/api/publication/…/status` instead** — neither is a one-line edit, which is why C22 must not be worked from its original "fix shape" line.
      - **⚠️ And it is bigger than this one button → §C27.**
    - **🔴 Do NOT apply the prescribed fix.** `.complete` → `.published` swaps a field this payload *does* emit for one it never emits, and **`activeTrack` is not on this payload at all** — it exists only on `getPublicationStatus`'s output. Applied as written it would hide the button permanently, for real, and look like a fix.
    - **✅ (b) SURVIVES — and its severity goes UP: it is the DEFAULT path, not an edge case.** `downloadPublishedHtml()` hardcodes `type=pub-faithful` (`books.html:2253`) while the gate passes on **any** track. The dev rows above are exactly that shape — five chapters where `mtPreview` shows the button and the download then requests `pub-faithful`, which is `not_started`. **(b) is the real defect in this entry.**
    - **The second control on the same field is FINE, not a second casualty:** `btn-publish-localized` (`books.html:2176`) reads `(publication.faithful || {}).complete` off the same payload — same field, same read model, so it behaves correctly. Recorded because it looks like a second instance and is not.
    - **Durable lesson — two services answer "is this chapter published" from two different sources of truth:** `getPublicationStatus` from **disk** (`published`), `getChapterStage` from the **DB** (`complete`). Neither is wrong; they answer different questions. **Name the endpoint before judging a field.**
  - **⚠️ The consequence for §C19 — ⚠️ ITSELF NOW IN DOUBT, see the correction above: there is NO UI path to an end-to-end proof.** The route is component-verified (mutation-checked tests) and the fix is deployed, but no editor can reach it. The book-download feature was broken at **both** ends simultaneously — an unclickable button in front of a route that threw on every call — and neither end failed loudly. A hidden element simply stays hidden.
  - **(b) A second bug sits behind it:** `downloadPublishedHtml()` hardcodes `type=pub-faithful` while the (broken) condition checks **any** track. Fix (a) alone and a chapter with only `mt-preview` published 404s. The service already computes **`activeTrack`** (`"faithful"` for ch3) — derive the type from that. ⚠️ Only `efnafraedi-2e` ch **01** and **03** have a complete faithful track on prod, so those are the only two places a naive fix would appear to work.
  - **Fix shape:** `.complete` → `.published`, derive `type` from `activeTrack`, and **pin it with a test** — a visibility rule keyed on a misspelled field is exactly what a test would have caught on day one. *[severity: correctness — feature 100% unreachable · blocks: §C19 end-to-end proof]*

- **C23 · ✅ A production request that ran past nginx's timeout left NO server-side trace — there was no request-duration logging at all** — **[CODE]** — **P1 (observability)** — **FIXED, DEPLOYED AND DEMONSTRATED 2026-08-05; it named C24's culprit on its first real incident** — *found 2026-08-05 during a live 504, which could not be diagnosed for exactly this reason.*
  - **The gap:** `grep -rn "pino-http|responseTime|Date.now() - start"` over `server/index.js` and the middleware returns **nothing**. Express is wired with a rate limiter and `express.json({limit:'1mb'})`, but **no timing middleware**, so no request's duration is ever recorded. p95 latency on this server is currently **unknowable**, and a 504 names no endpoint.
  - **📍 THE INCIDENT THIS CAME FROM, recorded as evidence because the negative results are the useful part.** The lead reported the chapter-detail panel spinning for 2+ minutes, then an nginx **504**; switching book and back made it load fine, and it has behaved since. Measured on prod during and after:
    - `/api/health` answered in **2.7 ms** → the event loop was **not** blocked.
    - The server process went to **1,236 MB RSS** and later returned to **169 MB** — on a box with **3,915 MB total**. That spike is real and remains **unexplained**.
    - ⚠️ `ps -o %cpu` is a **lifetime average, not instantaneous** — the 27–29% reading reflected that past spike and was briefly misread as ongoing load.
  - **Four hypotheses, all FALSIFIED by measurement — do not re-raise them without new evidence:**
    1. *A slow chapter-detail endpoint.* The panel `Promise.all`s five loaders, so one slow call hangs it — but `getBookData` is a **require-time cache** (O(1)) and `listChapterModules` is only `existsSync` calls.
    2. *Large modules are memory-hungry.* Every ch3 module loads in **5 ms / ≤1.2 MB**; the largest is 282 segments.
    3. *The concurrent glossary dry-runs starved the box.* Peak **129 MB, 0.23 s** each — two orders of magnitude too small.
    4. *The server was wedged.* It was serving health checks throughout.
  - **⚠️ The one untested lead:** the segment editor's **terminology/concordance** calls. The DB holds 20,272 headwords / 28,903 rows and the editor highlights terms across hundreds of segments — the only thing seen so far of the right order of magnitude for a ~1 GB allocation. **Recorded as a lead, NOT a diagnosis.**
  - **▶ THE FIX IS INSTRUMENTATION, NOT A SPECULATIVE CODE CHANGE.** Add request-duration logging with a slow-request `log.warn` above a threshold (method, path, duration, status). Then the next occurrence names its own culprit, the terminology hypothesis becomes testable rather than speculative, and the standing "large modules are slow" impression becomes a number. *[severity: robustness/observability · blocks: diagnosing any future prod latency incident, incl. §C23's own]*
  - **✅ FIXED, MERGED AND DEPLOYED 2026-08-05 — `server/middleware/requestTiming.js`, [PR #364](https://github.com/SigurdurVilhelmsson/namsbokasafn-efni/pull/364) → `main` `c4162854`, live on prod (unit restarted 19:26:58Z). Instrumentation only; the terminology lead is now TESTABLE, not tested.**
    - **✅ DEMONSTRATED ON PROD'S OWN DEPLOYED CODE, both shapes, not merely installed.** Prod at `c4162854`, timer mounted in the deployed `server/index.js`, `ExecMainStartTimestamp` **after** the merge — so the answering process is the new code, not an inference from the deploy log.
      - **Routine line:** `GET /api/health` → `{status:200, duration_ms:3, completed:true}` and `GET /api/c23-demonstration-probe` → `{status:404, duration_ms:4, completed:true}`. **p95 is now computable from prod's journal for the first time**, and the 3 ms corroborates the 2.7 ms measured during the incident.
      - **Abort shape — the one C23 exists for:** a POST stalled inside `express.json()` and then abandoned logged **`request still in flight` `{threshold_ms:1000}` at `…124221`, then `slow request` `{status:400, duration_ms:1405, completed:false}` at `…124622`. The watchdog fired 400 ms BEFORE the request died** — which is precisely what makes the trace survive a process death (§C20).
      - Probe used a **nonexistent** path so body parsing hung before any route or DB was touched. Prod verified untouched afterwards: `git status` still only the two §C3 TMX files, **same `ExecMainPID` 458123** (the abort did not kill the process), health 200.
    - **⚠️ Out-of-scope observation, logged not fixed:** a client disconnect also emits the pre-existing `Unhandled request error` at **level 50 (ERROR)** — `BadRequestError: request aborted`, `ECONNABORTED`. A client going away is not a server error, so this inflates the ERROR channel on exactly the events C23 makes visible. Pre-existing, unrelated to this change, and now easy to spot because the two lines sit adjacent. *[severity: log noise · blocks: nothing]*
    - **⚠️ THE DECISION THAT DECIDES WHETHER THIS WORKS AT ALL: it hangs on `res.on('close')`, NOT `res.on('finish')`.** Measured on Node v22.22.2 against a real server — normal: `finish(writableFinished=true)` → `close(writableFinished=true)`; a response whose client goes away mid-handler: **`close` only**, `writableFinished=false`. **nginx's 504 *is* that second shape** — it gives up and drops the upstream connection while the handler still runs. **A `finish`-based middleware would have logged nothing for the very incident C23 came from**, and would have looked correct in every test that never aborted a request. Pinned: swapping `close`→`finish` fails every abort case in the suite.
    - **🔴 THE REVIEW CAUGHT A HIGH-SEVERITY DEFECT IN THE FIRST CUT, and its shape is the lesson: an observability feature that MANUFACTURES the incident class it detects.** `express.static` is mounted above the timer and does an **async `fs.stat`** before calling `next()`, so a client that disconnects in that window makes `res` emit `close` **before** `res.once('close')` is attached. The listener then never fired — no terminal line — while the already-armed watchdog still did, leaving a lone **`request still in flight`** warning with **no terminal partner**. That is exactly the signature the middleware documents as *"the process died mid-request"* (§C20), so an operator triaging a real hang would have been sent hunting a phantom one. **Measured against the real stack: 30 of 200 aborting requests — exactly 30 phantom warnings and 30 missing terminal lines, 1:1. After the `res.closed` entry guard: 200/200 logged, 0 phantoms, 0 missing.** ⚠️ **The unit tests could not have caught it**: the fake `res` had no `closed` property at all, so the path was structurally unreachable and every test over it passed **vacuously** — a fixture that cannot express a state is not coverage of it.
    - **The already-closed line deliberately carries NO `duration_ms`.** The request cannot be timed, and a fictitious `0` would silently drag p95 down — defeating the one measurement C23 exists to enable.
    - **`status` is keyed on `res.headersSent`, not on completion.** A stream truncated mid-body did send a real status line; collapsing it to `null` would make it byte-identical to a request that never answered at all, which is the one distinction this log exists to draw. `null` now means *nothing was ever sent* — which is what the pure 504 shape reports.
    - **⚠️ `{method, path}` is captured AT ENTRY, and the first cut documented the WRONG REASON for it.** Express **strips a router's mount prefix** from `req.url` during dispatch and restores it on the way out, so `req.path` read inside the close handler is the **post-strip** value whenever the client aborts mid-dispatch. Measured on express 5.2.1: `/api/segment-editor/module/hang` at entry, **`/module/hang`** at close. `index.js` mounts 19 routers under `/api/…`, so reading it late would name **no endpoint** in precisely the aborted-request case this diagnoses. The behaviour was right but **unpinned** — a mutation reading `req.path` late kept the whole suite green.
    - **A second warn fires while the request is still IN FLIGHT**, at the threshold, `unref`'d. A close-time-only design logs nothing if the process dies mid-request — and **§C20 is a live P1 uncaught stream error that kills this exact process**, while C23's own incident included the unexplained 1,236 MB RSS spike. The `unref()` stops an in-flight request holding the event loop open through graceful shutdown.
    - **Mounted directly after `express.static`** — not at the top, and not where the old logger sat. Everything below is inside the measured window (cookie parsing, rate limiter, CORS, body parsing), so the number approximates what nginx measures rather than route time alone. Two measured consequences: a rate-limited **429 is now logged** (`RATE_LIMIT_MAX=3`, six requests → all six logged incl. three 429s; the old logger sat *below* the limiter and never saw one), and **static assets are still not logged** (`/css/common.css` → 200, no line), so log volume is unchanged.
    - **The routine line is `info` on purpose.** `logger.js` sets level `info` in production, so a `debug` line would be dropped there and *"p95 is unknowable"* would have stayed true. It **replaces** the old entry-time line rather than joining it.
    - **✅ DEMONSTRATED END-TO-END against the real wired server**, per the §C21 precedent that a detector which has never fired is not a shipped detector: a normal request logged `status 200 / duration_ms 3 / completed true`; a request stalled inside `express.json()` and then abandoned logged **`request still in flight`** at the threshold *while it hung*, then **`slow request` `status 400 / duration_ms 906 / completed false`**.
    - **The wiring is pinned in `startup.test.js`**, because a middleware that is written but not mounted is **§C22 verbatim**. ⚠️ Two of those assertions were themselves **vacuous** in the first cut: `toContain('SLOW_REQUEST_MS')` can never fail while `toContain('DEFAULT_SLOW_REQUEST_MS')` passes — one is a **substring** of the other — so deleting the env override entirely went undetected. Now asserted against `process.env.SLOW_REQUEST_MS`, plus an **absence** check for a hardcoded literal.
    - **Threshold:** `DEFAULT_SLOW_REQUEST_MS` lives in the middleware beside the comment justifying it, `SLOW_REQUEST_MS` overrides, injected from `index.js` — the `CONTENT_BACKUP_STALE_HOURS` convention, no literal in `index.js`.
    - **⚠️ PROCESS LESSON, worth more than the code:** the review agents were told to mutate-and-restore files in the **live working tree**, and one ran `git checkout -- server/index.js` on a diff it did not recognise. Nothing was lost only because everything was committed. **Run whole-branch reviews in an isolated worktree.** The same trap then bit the author: a mutation-check script using `git checkout` to revert its own mutations silently reverted three uncommitted fixes alongside them.
    - **🎯 IT PAID FOR ITSELF THE SAME DAY — the first real hang after deploy named its own culprit → §C24.** The lead reproduced the hang on prod; the log showed two ~100 s terminology requests and nothing else. **That is the entire purpose, delivered on the first incident.** ⚠️ **But read §C24's two corrections to how these logs must be interpreted — the watchdog did NOT fire, and queued requests log durations that understate what the user experienced.**

- **C24 · 🔴 `findTermsInSegments` recompiles ~49,000 regexes per call and scans every term against every segment — SYNCHRONOUSLY. Two editor endpoints take ~100 s each and BLOCK THE EVENT LOOP for the duration** — **[CODE]** — **P1** — *THE DIAGNOSIS OF THE §C23 INCIDENT. Measured on prod 2026-08-05 from the C23 instrumentation's first real hang; **pre-existing**, filed separately per the standing log-out-of-scope rule.*
  - **📍 The reproduction, straight from prod's journal.** The lead pressed "back" from the module editor and the UI spun. Every other request in the window ran in 1–12 ms:

    | time | duration | endpoint |
    |---|---|---|
    | 20:01:57 | 12 ms | `GET …/3/m68700` |
    | **20:03:37** | **99 858 ms** | `GET …/m68700/terminology-report` |
    | **20:05:15** | **98 563 ms** | `GET …/m68700/terms` |

    They ran **back to back, not concurrently** (each is stamped at completion): `terminology-report` 20:01:57→20:03:37, `terms` 20:03:37→20:05:15. **~3.3 minutes of editor for one "back" press.**
  - **🔴 CONFIRMED AGAIN 2026-08-06 ON A DIFFERENT MODULE, WITH REAL USER IMPACT — "the editor is basically unusable".** The lead opened `m68667` (**chapter 1**, not ch3) and got a spinner then a timeout. Journal: module loaded in **13 ms**, then `terminology-report` **77,674 ms** and `terms` **77,085 ms**, back to back — **~2.6 minutes of frozen server for one module open.** So it is **not module-specific and not chapter-specific; it is every module.** ⚠️ **A `HEAD /` probe in that window logged `completed=false`** — the first observed third-party casualty of the block. ⚠️ **No in-flight warning again**, exactly as §C24(c) predicts for a synchronous block. ⚠️ **The save was never reached** — no `POST …/edit` appears, so the ~45 s save projection remains unconfirmed; the page-load freeze sits in front of it. **One editor opening one module blocks all ~5 editors for 2.6 minutes.**
    - **⚖️ LEAD DECISION 2026-08-06: NO STOPGAP — go straight to C24.** A stopgap was offered and declined. It was available and small (both endpoints are **advisory only** and both front-end callers degrade gracefully — `loadTermData()` only adds highlights, `loadTerminologyReport()` only fills a head-editor panel), so an early return or an on-demand control would have taken the freeze off the module-open critical path without touching the matcher. **Recorded so nobody re-proposes it as though it were overlooked.**
    - **🔴 CONSEQUENCE OF THAT DECISION: C24 now BLOCKS EDITORIAL WORK OUTRIGHT, not merely degrades it.** Production stays as it is until the matcher is fixed, so **no editor can use the editor in the meantime**. This is no longer "a P1 among P1s" — it is the only thing standing between the team and any editing at all. **Do not let C24 expand in scope**: the measured, audited answer is **one narrow swap** of the "first occurrence of term T in segment S" primitive, keeping `translationTier`, the partition, the `consumed` tiler and the translation ranking byte-for-byte. Everything else in this item is context for doing that swap safely, not additional work.
  - **✅ MECHANISM, MEASURED not inferred** (counts run against prod's own DB; module size counted on disk). Both endpoints funnel into the one function — `/terms` calls it directly, `terminology-report` reaches it via `buildModuleTerminologyReport`.
    - It loads **every** approved/proposed translation row on **every call** and builds a fresh `RegExp` for each: one per headword plus one per translation's inflection alternation. **28 903 rows / 20 073 headwords / 120 340 inflection forms → 48 976 RegExp objects compiled PER REQUEST**, none cached. Each carries `\p{L}\p{N}` lookbehind+lookahead under the `u` flag, i.e. the expensive kind.
    - Then the inner loop runs **every term against every segment**: 20 073 × **282 segments** in `m68700` = **5.66 million regex executions**.
    - **Nothing in that path is async.** No `await`, no yield, no batching — so the whole ~100 s runs inside one tick.
  - **🔴 (a) THE EVENT LOOP IS BLOCKED, not merely busy — this is a whole-server outage, not a slow page.** For ~100 s at a stretch **no other editor can be served**. Evidence: zero requests logged across either window, then **8 requests draining in the same second (20:05:15)** — a backlog releasing, not a browser burst; an `/api/health` curl issued from this session during the window did not return. **Every editor loses the server while one editor presses "back".** That is why it is P1 and not a performance nit.
  - **⚠️ (b) THIS CORRECTS A §C23 FALSIFICATION.** C23 hypothesis 4 (*"the server was wedged — it was serving health checks throughout"*) was falsified from a **2.7 ms `/api/health`**. That sample was taken *between* blocks. The loop **is** blocked, intermittently, for ~100 s at a time, and a fast health check proves only that you sampled outside a window. **A liveness probe cannot falsify intermittent blocking — it must be sampled DURING the suspect operation.** The 1 236 MB RSS spike C23 left "unexplained" is also consistent with this: ~49 k compiled regexes plus the term array, per call. *(Consistent with — not yet measured. Do not upgrade this to a diagnosis without measuring RSS across one call.)*
  - **⚠️ (c) TWO CORRECTIONS TO READING THE §C23 LOGS, both learned here:**
    1. **The in-flight watchdog did NOT fire**, despite 100 s ≫ its 1 000 ms threshold. A blocked event loop cannot run timers, and when the loop frees, the handler's `close` → `clearTimeout` runs before the timers phase is reached. **So the watchdog covers slow-but-ASYNC hangs (what it was proven against) and is structurally blind to synchronous blocking** — the case it looks most useful for. The terminal `slow request` line is what caught this. Not a defect in the guard, but its advertised reach is narrower than the C23 entry implies.
    2. **Durations of QUEUED requests understate what the user experienced.** The timer starts when Express reaches the middleware, not when the connection arrived — so the 8 requests that drained at 20:05:15 all logged 1–3 ms after waiting up to 100 s. **During a block, believe the blocker's duration, not its victims'.**
  - **▶ Fix direction — RESEARCHED AND BENCHMARKED 2026-08-05, then independently audited. Read this before designing; it overturns the obvious lever.**
    - **⚠️ THE ENTRY ABOVE MIS-FRAMES THE CAUSE. "Recompiles ~49,000 regexes per call" is NOT the headline — caching them is not the fix.** Measured with the **unmodified production function** against a materialised prod-scale DB: current **136–139 s** per call on the dev box (prod's own journal: 99.9 s). With every regex **pre-compiled and cached**: **~128 s cold / ~64 s warm.** Still a total server block. The dominant cost is the **5.66 M executions of a `\p{L}\p{N}` lookbehind**, and per-exec cost degrades **~67×** with the number of distinct regexes V8 has *seen* — which neither caching, chunking (still 27.9 µs/exec at 200 live regexes) nor loop-reordering (only 2×) rescues.
    - **✅ THE ANSWER IS AHO-CORASICK, AND ITS OUTPUT IS BYTE-IDENTICAL.** With the row-order confound removed, production vs. an AC pass over the same SQL rows produced **byte-identical 52,445-byte output JSONs** — every field, every ordered `translations[]`, all 31 fallback / 25 primary flags, all 139 issues — at **6.8 ms vs 133.9 s**. Corroborated by a randomised differential (1,000 fixtures, 0 mismatches). **Replace ONE primitive, not the function:** keep `translationTier`, the partition, the `consumed` tiler and the translation ranking byte-for-byte; swap only *"first occurrence of term T in segment S"*.
    - **❌ Tokenise + hash (the intuitive fix) is NOT equivalent — it silently ADDS matches.** 137 of 600 randomised fixtures diverged, always by over-matching, plus 7 extra matches on the real module. Rejected on correctness, not speed.
    - **⚠️ TWO AMENDMENTS FROM THE AUDIT, both of which turn a "win" into a bug if missed:**
      1. **Do NOT move the boundary check out of the regex for `buildInflectionRegex`.** A post-hoc boundary test **abandons a position when the longest alternative fails**, instead of backtracking to a shorter one as the lookbehind does for free. Verified counterexample: forms `['mól','mól (m)']` against `mól (m)x` yields a **false "fannst ekki" QA warning**. Structurally unreachable on the English side (always one alternative) — safe there, unsafe on the Icelandic side.
      2. **Build the inflection regexes LAZILY, or AC merely RELOCATES the block.** They are constructed 28,903 times but executed ~313 times. Without laziness the AC cold build is a **6–11 s event-loop stall on every cache invalidation — i.e. on every terminology approval, an editor action.** With lazy `isRegex` the cold build is **under 1 s**.
    - **⚠️ PIN THE SQL TIE-BREAK BEFORE WRITING ANY EQUIVALENCE TEST.** `ORDER BY LENGTH(h.english) DESC` has **no tie-break**, so SQLite's arbitrary order among equal-length headwords changes match-array **order** in 6–10 of 274 segments (membership and issues unaffected — measured). `GROUP_CONCAT(ts.subject)` is unordered too. An unsorted JSON diff will report spurious regressions; add `, h.id ASC` **first**, then capture the golden.
    - **📍 The 1,236 MB is no longer unexplained.** One production call peaked at **1,167 MB** (`process.resourceUsage().maxRSS`). Upgrade the RSS line above from *consistent with* to **measured**. AC: 167 MB.
    - **🔬 How Miðeind solves the harder version of this problem** (their BÍN has 3.1 M unique forms and GreynirCorrect spellchecks against it live): **tokenize first, then look up each token in a memory-mapped radix trie of enumerated full forms** — never N patterns over text, cost O(tokens) not O(lexicon × text). Multi-word patterns are gated by a **trigger-lemma set intersection**, documented in their source as "an optimization only, to save unnecessary matching". Inflections are **enumerated**, which validates our `inflections` array as the right idea — but ours holds **4.16 forms per translation** against a full Icelandic paradigm of 8–16, a **silent-miss surface**. The Python sidecar is the wrong home for matching, but a good home for *generating* inflections.
    - **⚠️ ALREADY FILED IN MAY AND NEVER FIXED:** `docs/audit/security-audit-2026-05-29.md` **SA-18** — "unbounded synchronous regex matching… event-loop amplification". C23's instrumentation did not discover a new problem; it made a known, ignored one impossible to ignore.
    - **Coverage is better than assumed for the core, worse for the routes.** `terminologyService.test.js` genuinely pins the tier/homograph policy (8 core + 10 subject-scoping + 5 Icelandic-boundary cases). But **no test calls `getSegmentTerminologyWarnings` or `getModuleTerminologyReport`**, and the only route-level exercise of `/terms` **explicitly accepts 404 or 500 as a pass** — it passes whether the endpoint works, is missing, or throws. A golden test on the real m68700 is feasible and runs in **3.5 s** today against 787 real EN↔IS pairs, deterministic across runs — use `toEqual` against checked-in JSON, never `toMatchSnapshot` (whose `-u` regenerates silently). Assert the perf property as a **compile count**, never wall-clock.

      | save (`/edit`) | 1 | **~45 s** | **~0.4 s** |

Brainstormed per this entry's instruction, then hardened by a 5-agent survey, two authorized read-only prod censuses, and a blind adversarial review under Fable (0 Critical / 5 Important / 5 Minor, all dispositioned). **Baseline re-measured on `main` `65a26034`: 268 files / 3,868 tests green** — the register's L1 figure predated seven merges, as it said.

Lead decision: proceed, **measure RSS on prod** at verification.

Only 306 (4.1%) are separated by subject — 7,096 (95.9%) collide WITHIN one subject**, where the tier partition cannot help, `isPrimary` ties, and (all rows being `approved`) the ranking sort returns `0` on every comparison. **Raw SQL row order therefore decides what an editor is shown for ~35% of all production headwords** (7,096 / 20,272). Per-book editorial cost: **chemistry 124** *(independently confirms this register's "chemistry is 124 decisions")* · physics 1,399 · mathematics 2,237 · biology 3,555.
      - **🔴 CORRECTED 2026-08-07 — THE COLLISION COUNTS ARE EXACT; "EDITORIAL COST" IS OVERSTATED BY ~99.5%, IN EVERY SUBJECT.** Re-measured on prod, this census's four per-subject figures **reproduce exactly** — chemistry 124 · physics 1,399 · mathematics 2,237 · biology 3,555 — which is worth stating plainly: *the census was right, and re-running it is what showed what it counted.* Applying the head-form rule (`idordabanki_id IS NOT NULL`) to the same groups leaves:

        | Subject | Competing *(this census)* | **Unresolved by head form** |
        |---|---:|---:|
        | biology | 3,555 | **6** |
        | mathematics | 2,237 | **16** |
        | physics | 1,399 | **16** |
        | chemistry | 124 | **0** |

        **38 in the entire database.** The overwhelming majority of every subject's "collisions" are one Íðorðabankinn entry's head form beside its own listed synonyms — an import artifact, not a disagreement. **Biology is the headline: 3,555 → 6**, and biology was the book this census called "where it gets dangerous". → **§C14 ②** owns the derivation and the lead ruling.
      - **⚠️ COUNTING UNITS — three now live in this entry and they are NOT interchangeable.** This census counts **headwords** (7,402 with >1 in-scope translation; 7,096 colliding within a subject; 306 separated by subject). The 2026-08-07 measurement counts **(headword, subject) groups** — **7,315** of them — because a headword competing inside two subjects is one headword and two groups. The four per-subject figures happen to agree because each of those subjects' competitions is single-subject. *State which unit you mean; §C14 ②'s correction exists precisely because a right number was read as answering a question it did not.*
      - **Approval provenance — four bulk stamps, no row ever approved individually by a person.** All 28,903 rows carry `approved_by_name = "Íðorðabankinn"`, `source = "idordabankinn"`, and one of exactly four `approved_at` seconds on **2026-03-25**: chemistry 709 @ 16:18:23 · biology 13,561 @ 16:34:26 · physics 5,496 @ 16:40:02 · mathematics 9,137 @ 16:44:40. So the auto-accept was **not** chemistry-only. 20,774 of 28,903 carry an `idordabanki_id`.
      - **⚠️ Collisions skew hard to abbreviations, symbols and prefixes** — `W` (*vatt* | *vött*), `pH` (*sýrustig* | *pH*), `DNA`, `ATP`, `F1`, `a-`, `ot`, and genuine homographs like **`os` → *bein* | *munnur***. Some cannot be resolved by picking a winner at all, and the homograph mechanism is subject-scoped so it is useless when both senses sit in biology.
      - **🆕 A data-shape defect, logged not fixed:** several rows hold **comma-separated alternatives inside one `icelandic` field** (`"án, ekki, -laus-"`, `"deoxýríbósakjarnsýra, DNA"`). `buildInflectionRegex` escapes the whole string literally, so such a translation **can never match running text** — dead for matching — and when it lands at `approvedTranslations[0]` the editor is shown „a-" → „án, ekki, -laus-" fannst ekki. Editor-facing nonsense from data, not from code. C24 reproduces it exactly.
      - **🆕 `mathematics` has 9,137 translations and NO `book_subject_mapping` row**, so every one is permanently `fallback` for every book — it can surface as a badged cross-subject suggestion but never be a book's own term. Its 2,237 collisions may not be worth resolving at all; that is a free decision, and cross-cutting enough for `/decision-record`.

Raising the stored forms (a live opportunity: BÍN would take the mean from 4.16 to ~11) multiplies the Icelandic alternation ~2.5× and, worse, **changes which spans get claimed** under longest-first + `consumed` precedence — i.e. it moves the correctness oracle while C24 rewrites the implementation beneath it. - **C25 · 🟠 Icelandic grammar QA is reachable on the API key we already hold — and the Python sidecar built for it has never run** — **[CODE]** — **P2** — *found 2026-08-06 by the Miðeind toolchain survey; the sidecar half is pre-existing.* **Full evaluation and the alternatives rejected → [docs/decisions/2026-08-06-mideind-toolchain-evaluation.md](../decisions/2026-08-06-mideind-toolchain-evaluation.md).**
  - **`POST /v1/grammar` exists on `api.malstadur.is`** — same host, same `X-API-KEY` scheme `tools/lib/malstadur-api.js` already uses. Called with the existing key: **HTTP 200**. No new service, no new vendor, no new secret.

  - **🔴 BLOCKING for any integration: `/v1/grammar` CORRUPTS OUR INLINE MARKERS.** Measured: `[[i:vatns]]` → **`[[i: vatns]]`** — the spaced form that CLAUDE.md warns parses to an **empty list** rather than erroring — and, in a *different* call, `[[xref:kafli|1]]` → `[[xref:kafli>1]]` while `[[i:]]` was left alone. **Context-dependent and inconsistent, so one round-trip fixture will not catch it.** Worst: the corruption comes back **as `diffAnnotations`**, so it reaches the editor as a *suggestion* — "accept all" silently breaks the segment. ⚠️ **`test-results/api-marker-survival.md`'s 77/77 does NOT cover this**: every check in it exercises `/v1/translate`. A mask/unmask layer is mandatory.
  - **Cost is the MT rate — 10 ISK/1,000 chars.** Grammar-checking costs what translating costs, so this is a per-segment editor action, not a live panel.
  - **⚠️ COST BASIS IS UNRESOLVED — do not design UX or quote a figure until Miðeind answer in writing.** "API fees" appears **only** in Málstaður's pay-as-you-go tier, so the ISK 3,000 subscription may cover the *web app* only — and our own probe returned a per-call `usage.cost`, which a flat-rate API would not itemise. It decides the UX too: metered ⇒ explicit per-segment action; flat-rate ⇒ an always-on panel becomes viable. Four questions, plus the corrected arithmetic → **[docs/decisions/2026-08-06-bin-licensing-corrected-and-malstadur-integration.md](../decisions/2026-08-06-bin-licensing-corrected-and-malstadur-integration.md)**.
  - **🔴 MASKING IS NOT "MASK EVERYTHING".** `[[i:vatns]]` wraps **real prose** — *vatns* is a genitive noun doing grammatical work — so an opaque token **deletes a word from the sentence the checker sees**, degrading the agreement detection that justified the endpoint. `[[i:]]` needs **unwrap-and-rewrap** (design it with §C16's re-attach work); `[[xref:]]`/`[[link:]]`/`[[docref:]]` may be opaque. **Unmask must structurally validate and fail closed per suggestion** — the spaced form parses to `[]` *silently*.

- **C26 · 🟠 Big-picture editor audit — hunt the defect class that produced HALF this session's findings: features built, merged, and never once exercised** — **[CODE] + a short [LEAD] confirmation pass** — **P2, but high expected yield** — *proposed and logged 2026-08-06 at the lead's request, deliberately BEFORE it could evaporate — the closure audit's failure mode is exactly "a good idea nobody wrote down".*
  - **📍 The case for it is the hit rate, and the fact that the finds RHYME.** Six items surfaced in two days, and they are not six unrelated bugs — they are **one defect class**: §C22 (a button gated on a field the service never emits — invisible for months), §C19 (a route that threw on **every** call, unnoticed because nothing could reach it), §C25's sidecar (built, merged, **never executed once** — two faults prove it), §C24 (a ~100 s freeze already filed in the May security audit and never diagnosed), plus `GET /jobs` and `POST /api/terminology/check-consistency` (**zero callers**). **An unreachable feature raises no error — it produces silence, and silence is what a green suite and a quiet log both look like.** That class is *systematically* findable, which is what makes this worth scheduling rather than merely interesting.
  - **▶ Three passes that need NO lead time, run first to produce a short list:**
    1. **Reachability** — for every route, is there a caller? For every UI control, is there any state in which it appears? Three hits already exist without looking.
    2. **Contract diff** — every field name the front-end reads vs. what the service actually emits. §C22 was `.complete` vs `.published`; that whole class is catchable mechanically.
    3. **Traffic** — ⚠️ **this capability did not exist before §C23.** A route that has never appeared in the production journal is either dead or unreachable, and the log now tells them apart **by evidence rather than by reading code**.
  - **▶ Then, and only then, a SHORT interactive pass** — "these N controls look unreachable, confirm in the browser" — rather than an open-ended exploration. Higher yield, and it respects the one-feature-per-session rule.
  - **⏳ SEQUENCING, and the reason is concrete: do NOT start this before §C24 lands.** The interactive half happens *in the editor*, and opening any module currently freezes the server for ~2.6 minutes — the investigation's own tooling is the broken thing. **Then let a week of real editing accumulate**, so pass 3 has traffic to analyse. ⚠️ **A local pass is viable earlier if wanted** — the dev DB holds 6 terminology rows against production's 28,903, so the §C24 freeze barely manifests locally and a browser can be driven directly — but local will not reproduce anything scale-dependent, which is most of what is currently interesting. *[severity: unknown by construction — that is the point · blocks: nothing · blocked by: §C24]*
