- **C28 · 🟠 `/api/health` reports `content_backup: ok:true` while carrying `last_status: "error"` — the check gates on staleness alone and ignores a failure it has already recorded** — **[CODE]** — **P2, small** — *measured 2026-08-06 on prod during the §C3 verification; **pre-existing**, and it was masking a live push failure at the moment it was found.*
  - **The live payload, verbatim:** `{"age_hours": 2, "stale": false, "last_status": "error", "message": "git push failed (local ahead 1, behind 3)", "ok": true}` — with overall `status: ok`. The heartbeat was last written at 20:00 by a healthy run; the 22:00 run failed to push and therefore (correctly, per C11(b)) did **not** rewrite it. Because `CONTENT_BACKUP_STALE_HOURS` defaults to **6**, the check reads `ok` until ~02:00Z.
  - **So the alarm is not broken — it is late, by up to 6 hours, on information it already holds.** C11(b)'s design principle is *"staleness is the alarm"*, which is right for the **silent** failure mode it was built for. This is the **loud** mode: the run recorded `last_status: "error"` and a human-readable cause, and the check reported ok anyway.
  - **Fix shape:** `ok` should be false when `last_status === 'error'`, independently of age — keeping staleness as the separate signal it was designed to be. ⚠️ **Check the deploy readout too**: `./scripts/deploy.sh` prints not-ok checks, so today it would print nothing about a failed content push. *[severity: hardening — detection delayed, not absent · blocks: nothing]*

- **C20 · ✅ MERGED AND DEPLOYED 2026-08-07 — [PR #368](https://github.com/SigurdurVilhelmsson/namsbokasafn-efni/pull/368) → `main` `12fe0743`, live on prod since `05:37:30Z` — the download route's archive stream had NO `error` listener and no client-disconnect abort, so an `error` there killed the `ritstjorn` PROCESS, not the request** — **[CODE]** — **P1** — *surfaced 2026-08-05 by C19's whole-branch adversarial review; **pre-existing**, filed separately per the standing log-out-of-scope rule.* ⚠️ **Every mechanism claim below was re-run first-hand rather than copied from the review's prose.** The first draft of this entry was written in the register's confident "measured" voice from a *subagent's* summary — which is the closure audit's "built from summaries of itself" failure reproduced one more level down, and it was caught before this entry was pushed. Re-measuring changed the content: the review's one-line trigger ("a file unlinked after the stat") is only one of three crashing shapes, and the most obvious trigger of all — unlinking right after `.file()` — turns out **not** to crash.
  - **⚠️ Filed P1, not P2, on one criterion: it does NOT fail safe.** Every other hardening row in P2 over-refuses or leaks read-only info. This one takes the whole editorial server down — every editor loses their session mid-work — so it does not belong in the same tier despite blocking nothing.
  - **Not introduced by C19, but MADE REACHABLE by it, which is the whole reason it is filed now.** Checked against the `archiver@7.0.1` tarball directly (`npm pack`, per the #341 lesson — not `npm view`): v7's `_modulePipe` is `this._module.on('error', this._onModuleError.bind(this)); this._module.pipe(this);` and its `_onModuleError` is `this.emit('error', err)` — **the same two statements as v8's**, and neither attaches a user-facing `error` listener. v7's `package.json` has `main: index.js` and no `"type": "module"`, and its default export is the callable factory `var vending = function(format, options) { return vending.create(format, options); }` — so the route **worked** under v7 and the hazard was live from project inception until the 2026-05-12 bump to v8 broke the route outright. For ~3 months it has been **dormant only by accident**: a dead route cannot race. C19 revives the route, and with it this.
  - **(a) Uncaught `error` → process exit. MEASURED, four scenarios, and the boundary is sharper than "a missing file".** The handler's `existsSync`/`statSync` guard does not close the window because `archive.file()` builds a **lazy** read stream. What decides the outcome is whether the failure lands **before or after archiver's own stat**:

    | Scenario | Outcome | Repeatability |
    |---|---|---|
    | unlink **immediately** after `.file()` (archiver's stat fails) | `warning ENOENT`, `finalize()` **resolves**, exit 0 — process survives | 6/6 |
    | unlink one tick later, **after** the stat (`setImmediate`) | **UNCAUGHT `ENOENT` → exit 1** | ⚠️ **1 of 6** — a genuine race with a narrow window |
    | `chmod 000` after `.file()` — stat succeeds, read denied | **UNCAUGHT `EACCES` → exit 1** | 6/6 — **the deterministic reproducer** |
    | an appended stream that errors mid-read | **UNCAUGHT → exit 1** | 3/3 |

    In every crashing case the handler's `try/catch` around `await archive.finalize()` **does not fire**: `_modulePipe` registers `_onModuleError` on the module *before* `finalize()` installs its reject listener, so `_onModuleError`'s `this.emit('error', err)` throws out of the module's `emit` and the rejection path never runs. ⚠️ **The first row is exactly the measurement §C19's fact (b) reports** — which is why both are true and why (b) must be read as scoped to that condition. ⚠️ **Use the `chmod` row to reproduce, not the unlink row** — unlink-after-stat crashed only 1 run in 6, so a fix "verified" against it will look green ~83% of the time on broken code. ⚠️ **Not hypothetical**: "Vista + Birta" rewrites `05-publication/` on the same box the download reads from, so a re-render concurrent with a download is a real trigger, not a contrived one.
  - **(b) Client disconnect leaks the request.** There is no `res.on('close')` → `archive.abort()`. An editor who cancels a large book download leaves the archiver writing into a dead socket and the handler promise pending.
  - **(c) The C19 test cannot see either.** It settles on the response stream's `end` or on `res.json`, so a mid-stream failure is outside what it observes. **Do not read C19's green as coverage of this.** A fix needs its own harness — one that injects a read failure mid-archive, which likely means making the file-enumeration seam injectable.
  - **🔴 THE OBVIOUS ONE-LINE FIX IS A TRAP — MEASURED 4/4, and this is the most useful thing in this entry.** Adding just `archive.on('error', …)` — the fix anyone would reach for — **does not fix it; it converts a crash into a hang.** With a listener attached and the deterministic `chmod` trigger: the listener fires with `EACCES`, and then **`finalize()` never settles** (still pending at 3 s) and **`res` is never ended**. So the process survives and the editor's browser instead holds an open chunked download that never completes and never errors, while the handler's promise and the `Archiver` leak for the process lifetime. A "fix" validated only by *"the server stopped crashing"* will ship exactly this.
  - **Shape of the fix (not prescribed — decide when taken):** the `error` handler must **also end the response** (destroy `res`) and stop waiting on `finalize()`, plus `res.on('close')` → `archive.abort()` for (b). ⚠️ A second constraint makes the reporting non-trivial: once `archive.pipe(res)` has written a byte, `res.headersSent` is **true** and the existing catch block's `res.status(500).json(...)` is already unreachable — so the handler cannot report a mid-stream failure to the client as JSON, only truncate. Whatever ships must decide what a half-written ZIP looks like to the editor. **Verify against the `chmod` reproducer and assert on *both* — no crash AND the response settles.** *[severity: robustness/availability — process-level · blocks: nothing]*
  - **✅ WHAT SHIPPED (2026-08-06, branch `fix/c20-download-stream-error`).** The handler races `finalize()` against a failure promise and never awaits `finalize()` unconditionally — *the race is the fix; awaiting finalize IS the hang.* On any failure it **destroys** `res` rather than ending it, and **aborts the archiver on every failure kind** (not only on disconnect). `res.on('close')` with a `writableFinished` guard is what detects a client disconnect. Spec/plan: [`spec`](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-06-c20-download-stream-error-design.md) · [`plan`](../superpowers/plans/2026-08-06-c20-download-stream-error.md). **Five tests in the new file, and all seven mutation rows measured red.** *(⚠️ This bullet said "**Four tests**, all mutation-verified" and "4 commits". Both were wrong: **three** tests were added in the first cut — the fourth counted was the pre-existing C19 test — and a commit count is stale the moment it is committed. **Do not put a commit count in prose**; `git log main..HEAD` is authoritative.)*
  - **⚠️ D1's RATIONALE was wrong; D1 itself is unchanged and better supported.** The claim that ending cleanly hands the editor "a truncated `.zip` that opens and is silently missing files" is **false** — a stream ended mid-archive has no end-of-central-directory record, so `unzip -t` reports *"cannot find zipfile directory"* and Python's `zipfile` raises `BadZipFile`. **The decision (destroy, not end) stands**: a saved file that cannot be opened at all is still worse than a transfer the browser flags as failed, because only the latter tells the editor to retry. This is a wording correction, **not** a reopening of the lead's decision.
  - **⚠️ THIS ENTRY'S OWN CRASH MECHANISM WAS WRONG, and the boast above it did not hold.** This entry opens *"Every mechanism claim below was re-run first-hand rather than copied from the review's prose"* — and the `_modulePipe`/`_onModuleError` story is precisely the claim that failed. **Measured:** on the `chmod 000` reproducer the error is emitted **on the Archiver**, by `_moduleAppend`'s append callback (`archiver/lib/core.js:179`, reached from `_onQueueTask`), from an async stream callback already outside the handler's `try/catch`. **`_onModuleError` (`core.js:318`, bound to `_module` at `:234`) never runs, and `_module` emits no `error` at all** — which is *also* exactly why `finalize()` hangs, since its promise settles only on `_module`'s `end`/`error`. The self-correction is worth more here than the corrected mechanism: **re-running a claim first-hand is not the same as re-running the RIGHT claim**, and this entry asserted the former while doing neither.
  - **✅ The simpler reproducer held: `chmod 000` applied BEFORE the request, 3/3** — `exit=1`, empty stdout, `Error: EACCES … Emitted 'error' event on ZipArchive instance`. The entry's after-`.file()` framing is unnecessary; permission lives on a file's *content*, so both stats succeed and only the `open` fails. **No mid-stream timing and no injectable seam** — which retires this entry's clause (c) guess that a fix "likely means making the file-enumeration seam injectable". It does not.
  - **✅ Confirmed: the crashing child prints NO report line**, so the parent keys on the exit code and asserts it **first**; otherwise the pre-fix RED reports the wrong defect.
  - **🔴 THREE PREDICTIONS IN THE SPEC/PLAN WERE FALSIFIED BY ACTUALLY RUNNING THE MUTATIONS. Two changed the shipped code.** All five rows were run; results are in the spec's corrected §4.5 table.
    - **① The hang does NOT time the child out (4/4).** The plan predicted "child never exits → parent kills → `killed: true`". Measured: with the error listener attached and no race, the child **exits `0` in ~400 ms, silently**. `finalize()` genuinely never settles — so the report line never prints, which is *how* it is caught — but this harness has no listening socket holding the event loop open, so node drains and exits cleanly. **In production the socket does hold it open, which is why the same defect presents there as a request that hangs forever.** The assertion that catches the hang is the missing-`REPORT`-line check, which the spec had framed as a mere diagnostic.
    - **② The plan's client-disconnect test was VACUOUS and would have passed its own mutation.** As specified, T2 asserted only that the handler *settles* — which it does on the happy path regardless. Under the "delete `res.on('close')`" mutation it settled in 264 ms and stayed **green**. It was strengthened with `expect(res.destroyed).toBe(true)` (`res.emit('close')` does not set `destroyed`; only the handler's own `res.destroy()` does), and the mutation then turned it red. Its `setImmediate` was also dropped for a synchronous emit — the handler body has no `await` before `finalize()`, so deferring only raced compression.
    - **③ ⚠️ THE MOST GENERALISABLE RESULT — a mutation can be REAL and INVISIBLE, and the spec's prescribed disposition for that case would have written a FALSE claim into the source.** Removing the `writableFinished` guard turned **nothing** red across the entire **1852-test server suite**. The spec offered exactly two dispositions: *T3 red ⇒ load-bearing*, or *nothing red ⇒ defensive, downgrade the comment to say so*. **Neither was correct.** Probing `outcome` directly, **3/3 each way: without the guard a fully successful download resolves `{kind:'disconnect'}` and runs `archive.abort()` + `res.destroy()` against a completed response; with it, `outcome` is `null`.** Measured happy-path order is `finish → close(writableFinished=true) → handler returns`, so `close` arrives **while the handler is still awaiting the race**. The guard is **load-bearing**; the suite was simply blind to it, because the spurious abort lands after every existing observer has settled on `end` with complete, correct bytes. **A fourth test was added** — *"does not abort or destroy on a fully successful download"* — which spies `Archiver.prototype.abort` (⚠️ `abort` is **not** on `ZipArchive.prototype`; spying there misses it) and is red under that mutation. **Durable lesson: "no check went red" is not evidence the mutated line is optional. Distinguish *the change has no effect* from *no check can observe the effect* by probing the value directly before concluding either.**
  - **Mutation results, all five run:** ① remove `archive.on('error')` → T1 exit code red ✅ · ② unconditional `await finalize()` → T1 red, but on the REPORT line, not the timeout ⚠️ · ③ `res.end()` for `res.destroy()` → T1 **and** T2 red on `destroyed` ✅ · ④ remove `res.on('close')` → T2 red **only after strengthening** ⚠️ · ⑤ remove the `writableFinished` guard → nothing red across 1852 tests, **new test added** 🔴.
  - **Plan gaps found while executing (both corrected in the branch, neither in the plan's control):** the plan's Global Constraints said "`server/` is CommonJS: `require`, not `import`" — true of runtime code, but **Vitest cannot be `require()`d**, so the test file is ESM reaching CommonJS via `createRequire`, as `books-routes.test.js` already does. And the plan's parse check `node -e "require('./server/routes/books.js')"` **throws without `JWT_SECRET`**, the same load-time throw the plan documented for the child but not for itself.
  - **🆕 Residual logged, NOT fixed here (scope, spec D2) — a `statSync` race in `addFilesFromDir` yields a "zip" containing JSON.** `addFilesFromDir` runs *after* `archive.pipe(res)`, so a file removed between its `readdirSync` and its `statSync` — exactly the "Vista + Birta re-render concurrent with a download" trigger this entry was filed for — throws into the existing `catch`. **Measured: no crash, no hang, handler settles, `500 {"error":"Download failed"}`.** But `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="…zip"` is already set, so the editor's browser saves a `.zip` whose contents are the JSON error. ⚠️ This also **narrows this entry's own closing clause**, which worried that `res.headersSent` would be **true** by then: it is **false**, because `archive.file()` only queues and no byte has been written during the synchronous enumeration — so the catch's `!res.headersSent` guard does fire, and the defect is the misleading header, not a silent hang. Fails safe; low severity; not folded in.
  - **🔴 THE WHOLE-BRANCH ADVERSARIAL REVIEW FOUND THREE REAL BLIND SPOTS IN THE FIRST CUT — all in what the tests could SEE, none in what they claimed.** 79 agents over four lenses (correctness · test-quality · production-concurrency · docs-accuracy), each finding independently refuted from three angles; 25 raised, 16 survived. **Every one of the three below was a mutation that left the entire suite green.**
    - **① `archive.abort()` ran only for `kind === 'disconnect'`, so a failed download left the archiver working.** archiver does **not** stop its queue on an entry error — `_moduleAppend` does `this.emit('error', err); setImmediate(callback)` (`core.js:178-180`) — so the remaining entries were still read and deflated into a response already destroyed. **Measured with 40 files: `queueIdle:false` at settle and 38 further `entry` events over the next 300 ms; with `abort()` on every kind, 0.** ⚠️ **The review called this a leaked file descriptor per failed download, "surviving three forced `global.gc()`". That did NOT reproduce** — against a real `http.Server`, `fds` measured **0** at settle, +300 ms and +2.3 s; the read streams open and close normally as the queue drains. **It is wasted CPU/IO that terminates on its own, not an unbounded leak.** Fixed as waste. **A finding can be right about the mechanism and wrong about the consequence — check the consequence separately.**
    - **② NOTHING PINNED `archive.abort()` AT ALL.** `res.destroy()` runs on every failure kind, so asserting `res.destroyed` cannot distinguish a live abort from a deleted one, and the success-path test's `abortCalls === 0` is satisfied *a fortiori* when abort never runs. Deleting `archive.abort()` outright left the whole server suite green — i.e. **register defect (b), the entire client-disconnect half of this item, shipped unpinned.**
    - **③ NOTHING PINNED THE FLAG THE DISCONNECT GUARD READS, and a `PassThrough` structurally cannot.** On a **real `http.ServerResponse`, `destroyed === true` when `close` fires on BOTH the success and the abort path** (measured: `/abort CLOSE destroyed=true writableFinished=false`, `/success CLOSE destroyed=true writableFinished=true`), whereas a `PassThrough` reports `destroyed=false`. So the one-word mutation `!res.writableFinished` → `!res.destroyed` passed **every** test while making the disconnect branch **dead in production** — the C20 hang, reintroduced with the suite green. ⚠️ **The test doubles were unfaithful on exactly the flag that decides the branch.** Note this measurement also *confirms* `writableFinished` is the correct guard.
  - **✅ THE FIX: two tests over a REAL `http.Server` and real sockets**, driving the same handler. They pin ①②③ together, and they are also the closest thing this item has to end-to-end proof — **real HTTP, though still not UI** (§C22 hides the button), which the spec had declared unobtainable. **All seven mutation rows now turn red** — ⚠️ *with one distinction worth keeping, by the same standard applied to row ② above: on rows ① and ② the real-socket error test goes red by **hitting its 20 s test timeout**, not by failing an assertion. Red is red, but a timeout says "it never finished", not "it did the wrong thing"* — ① delete `archive.on('error')` · ② unconditional `await finalize()` · ③ `res.end()` for `res.destroy()` · ④ delete `res.on('close')` · ⑤ delete the `writableFinished` guard · **⑥ delete `archive.abort()`** *(new — caught only by the real-socket tests)* · **⑦ `!writableFinished` → `!destroyed`** *(new — caught only by the real-socket disconnect test)*.
  - **🆕 Pre-existing, logged not fixed:** a client that disconnects **before** the handler runs is undetectable by this design — `res` has already emitted `close`, so the listener attached inside the handler never fires. Same shape as the `res.closed` entry-guard defect §C23's review caught. Not introduced here; not in C20's scope.
  - **⚠️ Fixture hygiene:** the C20 fixture dir is `99`, which the whole-book download branch's `/^\d{2}$/` entry filter matches, so an interrupted run could leave a mode-`000` file that every later whole-book download of `orverufraedi` would enumerate. `beforeEach` now removes residue **before** creating the fixture.
  - **⚠️ CI DID NOT RUN — and it is the outage, not a red gate.** `total_count` for the branch head is **`0`**, and `githubstatus` reports **`Actions: major_outage`** at the time of the push (the same outage this register logged for PRs #365/#366 earlier on 2026-08-06). **No run object exists**, so there is nothing to time and duration is not the diagnostic here. **The gate was local root `npm test`: 273 files / 3962 tests green** — 272+1 file, 3957+**5** tests, and the arithmetic closes exactly. *(This line read "3960 … 3957+3" before the adversarial review added the two real-socket tests.)* **No claim is made about CI on this branch.**
  - **⚠️ Ships with NO end-to-end UI proof — §C22 still hides the button.** The route stays reachable only by test and by a hand-typed URL. Accepted, not overlooked (spec D2). **No deploy, no re-render, no vefur sync needed** — server-only, nothing reader-facing moves. ⚠️ **But `ritstjorn` on prod keeps running the OLD code until someone deploys**, so the crash remains live in production until then.
  - **§C5's authz row was deliberately NOT folded in** (`GET /download` is `requireAuth`-only, no book scoping) — mixing an authz change into a stream-failure fix muddies both reviews.

- **C31 · 🔴 The SEGMENT EDITOR re-implements the `__term__` vocabulary in its own JS, unguarded — it mangles fill-in-the-blank segments in the preview pane TODAY** — **[CODE]** — **P2** — *found 2026-08-07 by the C16(a′) adversarial review (both missed-sites lenses, blind, converged); verified here against real committed content.*
  - **Live, not latent, and demonstrably worse than the pipeline bug it mirrors.** Four `server/public/js` sites use `/__(.+?)__/g` — and `.+?` **matches underscores**, so it does not merely bridge two blanks, it destroys them. On the real committed segment `orverufraedi m58782:problem:fs-id1171360186862` — **the very segment this branch just repaired in published HTML** — the preview renders `…lífveru <span class="preview-term">_</span><span class="preview-term">_ hennar og </span><span class="preview-term">_</span>___.`
  - **Sites:** `segment-editor.js:1777` · `localization-editor.js:1468` (preview panes) · `marker-highlight.js:106` (highlight overlay, loaded by `segment-editor.html:1619`) · `segment-validation.js:115` (`/__/g` parity count → a false *"unmatched term pair"* advisory on every save of a blank-bearing segment; **24 committed segments** qualify, e.g. the 10-underscore runs in `liffraedi-2e ch05 m66374/m66375`). Plus **`tools/lib/mt-normalize.cjs:61,65`**, whose consumers are `server/services/segmentParser.js`, `acceptanceService.js` and `tools/export-corpus.js` — latent there (counts currently balance) but it feeds the **editor-served view** and the MT-accept comparison.
  - **Severity is bounded and should not be inflated: display-only, never persisted.** The preview does not write back, so no content is damaged. What it costs is editor trust — an editor sees their blanks visibly broken and cannot tell it is a display artefact.
  - **The fix is the same guard**, verified: `(?<!_)__([^_]+)__(?!_)` leaves the real segment untouched. ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT folded into the C16(a′) PR.** It is a different tree (`server/` is **AGPL**, not MIT), a different gate (Playwright E2E, not the vitest suite that gated the pipeline fix), and it is deploy-gated — and expanding a branch *after* its adversarial review means shipping unreviewed code, which is the failure mode that review exists to catch.
  - **⚠️ The root cause is architectural, and worth naming: the marker vocabulary is defined in at least three places** — `tools/cnxml-inject.js` (now single-sourced via `LEGACY_TERM`), the editor's own JS, and `mt-normalize.cjs`. **They drift independently**, which is why fixing the pipeline did not fix the editor. Scope this with §C16's id-anchored `[[term:text|id]]` redesign rather than as four more lookarounds. *[severity: editor-visible/display-only · blocks: nothing]*

- **C29 · 🔴 An EMPTY `[[b:]]` marker makes `orverufraedi ch05/m58805` un-injectable — its published blank-mangle therefore CANNOT be repaired** — **[CODE]** — **P1** — *found 2026-08-07 while fixing C16(a′); the one instance of the four that could not be swept.*
  - **Mechanism, measured not inferred.** EN carries `{{term}}algal bloom{{/term}}[[b:s]]` — the English plural *s* is bold-marked as a **separate** marker. Icelandic forms no plural that way, so the Málstaður API returned **`[[b:]]` with empty content**. Nothing converts an empty bracket marker, so `assertNoMarkerResidue` refuses the module and `cnxml-inject` exits with `1/1 module(s) FAILED`. **The gate is behaving correctly** — it is catching real upstream marker loss.
  - **✅ PRE-EXISTING, verified rather than assumed:** re-running the inject with the C16(a′) fix `git stash`ed reproduces the identical failure. It is not a regression from that work.
  - **⚠️ Consequence: `5-fill-in-the-blank.html` still shows `______<dfn class="term"> </dfn>______` to readers.** It is the last of C16(a′)'s four instances still live.
  - **Three options, and the cheap-looking one is a trap.** (1) **Teach inject to drop an empty bracket marker** — ⚠️ this weakens a safety gate over what is genuinely content loss, and CLAUDE.md's marker-survival rule is **per-endpoint evidence**, so it is a decision, not a tidy-up. (2) **Re-extract + re-MT the module** so the marker is regenerated — real ISK, and no guarantee the API does differently. (3) **Hand-repair the single line in `03-translated/…/m58805.cnxml`** (line 126; it is the file's **only** `<term>`) and re-render — ⚠️ **`03-translated/` is a GENERATED tree, so that edit is invisible to provenance and is silently discarded the moment the module is successfully injected again. That is precisely the `02-mt-output` hand-repair hazard A4-L3 has just finished cataloguing, one tree over** → `[[mt-output-hand-repairs]]`. **[LEAD] call — do not pick one unilaterally.**
  - **⚠️ A premise worth retiring: "a module with a `.cnxml` on disk can be regenerated."** `m58805.cnxml` exists and is committed, but no current code path reproduces it. *[severity: correctness/reader-visible · blocks: the last C16(a′) instance]*

Seven zeros alone would have been consistent with fetching something empty; a page we *know* is still broken proves the check can tell the states apart. Sanity-checked too: a live page is **30,176 bytes** against **162** for a nonsense URL, so the fetch is real content and not the SPA fallback.

The gate would refuse them today, which is exactly why they still carry residue: nothing has re-injected them since. *(⚠️ **Corrected 2026-08-07 by the review.** This line first gave a single date, `2026-03-30`, which is true of **1 of the 7**, and dated the gate to 2026-07-13 from a `git log -S` hit that merely *touched* the string rather than introducing it. - **C36 · Terminology concept model, Part A — SHIPPED, inert by construction; eight findings deferred to Part B/C, all load-bearing there** — **[CODE]** — **P1** — *logged 2026-08-08, merged to `main` as 17 commits and DEPLOYED the same day. Nothing reads the new tables and nothing was dropped. ⚠️ **The IMPORT ran against a throwaway copy — but migrations 045/046 DID apply to production's schema at that deploy**, because `migrationRunner` runs every migration's `up()` on every start. **Verified read-only on prod after the deploy:** all four tables present; `concept`, `concept_term` and `book_concept_preference` all **0 rows**; `book_domain_priority` **21 rows across all 6 registered books, none scoped to nothing**, orders matching `PRIORITIES` exactly; all four old terminology tables still present; `/api/health` `status: ok` with `migrations.total: 46`. That also closes the whole-branch review's stated gap — 046 had been measured only against a 5-book dev copy, never production's real `registered_books`. Part B cuts consumers over onto `concept`/`concept_term`; Part C drops the old tables. Evidence: [`test-results/concept-import-2026-08.md`](../../test-results/concept-import-2026-08.md). Finding 3 below cites **§C35** — same root cause, measured together.*
  1. **Re-import cascade-deletes editor term preferences.** `server/scripts/import-concepts.js:32` clears a concept's terms with `DELETE FROM concept_term WHERE concept_id = ?` then re-inserts with new autoincrement ids. `book_concept_preference.term_id REFERENCES concept_term(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` (migration 045), so re-importing a collection to pick up an Árnastofnun update **silently deletes every editor term preference for that collection's concepts** — no count, no warning in the returned stats. Twice-derived: measured directly, and found independently by a reviewer. Inert only because `book_concept_preference` is empty in Part A. **Must be resolved before Part B writes to that table.**
  2. **Migration 046 re-asserts the domain priority on every server boot, and the orphan case.** `server/services/migrationRunner.js` calls every migration's `up()` unconditionally on every start (no applied-migrations gate for the modern pattern), so `INSERT OR REPLACE` in `046-seed-domain-priority.js` is repeated *enforcement*, not a one-time seed. Two consequences, one root cause: (a) if Part B adds an admin control to reorder a book's domains, the next `systemctl restart` silently reverts it; (b) shortening a book's `PRIORITIES` list never deletes the orphaned rows, so a domain deliberately removed keeps its old `position` and keeps influencing every `ORDER BY position` consumer. Both fixed by `DELETE WHERE book_id = ?` before the insert loop, plus a decision on whether that table is user-writable.
  3. **`migration046.test.js`'s "a book scoped to nothing is the bug" test cannot detect that bug, and the bug is live on a fresh clone.** The test seeds `registered_books` with exactly the six slugs `PRIORITIES` contains, so the fixture *is* the map and the assertion is self-fulfilling. **Measured** on a database built by running all migrations against an empty file: only `lifraen-efnafraedi` and `edlisfraedi-2e` are registered, and both correctly received their priority rows (3 and 5). The other four do not exist at all — `019-register-new-books.js` registers `liffraedi-2e` and `orverufraedi` **without** the `NOT NULL` `registered_by` column that migration `003` declares, so `INSERT OR IGNORE` silently discards both (**§C35**), while `029-register-preview-books.js` registers its two *with* `registered_by: 'system'` and succeeds — the contrast is the tell; and **no migration registers `efnafraedi-2e` or `stjornufraedi` at all**, they reach `registered_books` only via the admin route. ⚠️ **Migration 046 is therefore NOT at fault and `if (!row) continue` behaves correctly** — every book that exists got its rows; on a fresh clone only 2 of the 6 books in `PRIORITIES` exist, so `resolve()` would scope four of them to nothing. Bounded by `migrationRunner`'s every-boot `up()`, which re-seeds them once they are registered through the admin route — **the same repeated execution that would silently revert an editorial reorder, so findings 2 and 3 are one coin and neither can be fixed without first deciding whether `book_domain_priority` is user-writable.** Needs a real-tree assertion, per spec §10.
  4. **Null and colliding `idordabanki_id`.** `importConcepts` only looks up an existing concept when `idordabankiId != null`, and SQLite permits multiple NULLs in a UNIQUE column, so a null-id entry re-inserts a duplicate concept on every run. Separately, two entries from different collections sharing an id silently overwrite, leaving one concept carrying both entries' terms. **Measured on the full 20-collection import: 0 null ids, and 70,187 concepts with 70,187 distinct `idordabanki_id` — zero collisions.** Neither is proof of uniqueness by construction; both are quantified as currently absent.
  5. **Domain vocabulary is triplicated with no shared constant and no test**: `COLLECTION_DOMAIN`'s values (`server/lib/conceptFromEntry.js`), `DOMAINS` (`server/scripts/verify-concept-import.js`), and `PRIORITIES`'s domains (`server/migrations/046-seed-domain-priority.js`). Measured clean today — all seven priority domains resolve to a non-empty `concept.domain` — but nothing keeps it clean, and a typo'd domain yields a fallback level that matches nothing, silently.
  6. **Neither `run-concept-import.js` nor `verify-concept-import.js` has a CLI entry point** (`require.main === module`), unlike `server/scripts/export-terminology.js:978`. The evidence file says "re-measure rather than trusting these numbers later", but the driver that produced them is not in the repo.
  7. **`homographs-separated` is one oracle pair wide** (`fruma`/biology, `rafhlað`/physics). Contamination involving no measured pair passes silently. **Do not widen the oracle by inference** — `flokkur` was excluded on reasoning and the full corpus then confirmed it appears in biology (3), which would have false-fired. Widening requires measurement, not a guess.
  8. **`model-is-non-empty` is corpus-wide**, so a single collection yielding zero still passes. Per-collection yield is computed by `runImport` and *printed*, never gated. *[severity: correctness/data-integrity (all inert today) · blocks: Part B]*

- **C36 · Part B0 — import hygiene — MERGED (PR #372 → `main` `fc2d7258`), DEPLOYED AND VERIFIED ON PROD; six of the eight findings closed** — **[CODE]** — **P1** — *2026-08-08. Plan: [`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-08-terminology-concept-model-part-b0.md`](../superpowers/plans/2026-08-08-terminology-concept-model-part-b0.md). **Touches no consumer, drops no table, and writes nothing to production** — verified: migration 047's output is byte-identical to prod's live 21 `book_domain_priority` rows, book for book and position for position, with a perturbation control confirming the diff can see a single changed row. Findings 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 8 closed; 4 and 7 deliberately out of scope, with reasons in the plan's Non-goals. **CI on the merge commit: `test`, `e2e` and `lint` all passed** (e2e is the one a local root `npm test` can never evidence — 047 runs in its bootstrap and correctly skips `__e2e-fixture__`, which has no priority row). `audit` was red, **identically on `main` and predating the branch** → §C37.*
  - **✅ DEPLOYED 2026-08-08 13:04Z, and the no-op prediction HELD — measured, with a control.** Prod HEAD `4f3fe96e`, **0 ahead / 0 behind**, working tree clean, `fc2d7258` an ancestor. **Migration 047 executed on that boot and changed nothing**: `book_domain_priority` is **21 rows, byte-identical** to the pre-deploy capture, book for book and position for position — and a single-row perturbation control confirms the comparison can still see a difference, so "identical" is a result rather than a broken check. `/api/health` (dated observation, not a status to carry): `status: ok`, `migrations.total: 47`, all seven checks ok — **including `content_backup`**, so the deploy's `git pull --rebase` restored the fast-forward and the 12-behind stranding risk is discharged. `concept` / `concept_term` / `book_concept_preference` remain **0 / 0 / 0**: nothing reads them, exactly as Part B0 intends.
    - **⚠️ Two method notes worth keeping.** The service start (`13:04:09`) and the changed files' mtime (`13:04:00`) were taken in **one SSH invocation** — §C3 ③ records a deploy whose timestamps, read across two calls, would have reported "prod is still running the old code" *as a measurement*. And the pre/post `book_domain_priority` comparison is only meaningful because the **before** snapshot was captured while the branch was still unmerged; there is no way to take it afterwards.
  - **⚠️ FINDING 1's MECHANISM WAS CHALLENGED DURING B0 AND THE CHALLENGE WAS WRONG. The register's wording stands: it IS a cascade delete.** B0 briefly asserted the opposite — that `PRAGMA foreign_keys` is off in production, so the preference row survives *dangling* rather than cascading — on a measurement taken with the **system `sqlite3` CLI** (3.46.1, stock build, reports `0`). **`better-sqlite3` is compiled with `SQLITE_DEFAULT_FOREIGN_KEYS=1`** (`server/node_modules/better-sqlite3/deps/defines.gypi:14`), so every bare `new Database()` here — production included, verified on the box — reports `1` and the cascade fires. **Right property, wrong instrument.** Now pinned as a fact by a test, so a future build dropping that flag goes red instead of silently disabling every `ON DELETE CASCADE` in the schema. **Durable: never measure this pragma with the CLI.**
  - **The before/after, measured at corpus scale rather than argued.** Pre-fix importer, identical re-import of the 20-collection corpus: `max(concept_term.id)` **192,189 → 384,378**. Fixed: **192,189 → 192,189**. The row *count* is 192,189 in both, and every verification check and the whole `VERIFY: PASS` block reads the same either way — which is exactly why the churn was invisible.
  - **The import is now runnable from the repo and reproduces the recorded yield.** `node server/scripts/run-concept-import.js --dir <raw> [--db <path>] [--allow-zero-yield]` and `verify-concept-import.js --db <path>`; exit **0** ok / **1** ran-and-refused / **2** usage-or-environment. Demonstrated on a scratch DB: 70,187 concepts / 192,189 terms in 3.8 s, per-collection yield matching [`test-results/concept-import-2026-08.md`](../../test-results/concept-import-2026-08.md) line for line. ⚠️ **The raw 20-collection fetch (72,482 entries, ~1.5 h at the mandated 1 req/s) survives at `~/idordabanki-raw-2026-08-07/` with a PROVENANCE.md** — rescued from `/tmp`, verified post-truncation-fix. It is **not** in the repo (76 MB, and it is Árnastofnun's data). Re-fetching is the fallback, not the plan.
  - **🆕 Five findings DEFERRED to Part B, from the blind-pair review. All are PRE-EXISTING (Part A or older) except the last.**
    1. **Deleting a book from `BOOK_DOMAIN_PRIORITY` does not remove its rows.** 046 keeps its own frozen `PRIORITIES`, does not export it, and re-seeds every boot; 047 iterates only the books the map names. Measured over a fresh clone: removing `edlisfraedi-2e` or `lifraen-efnafraedi` is **silent**; removing `orverufraedi` reddens `migrationsRealTree.test.js` only **incidentally** — that pin exists for §C35 and evaporates when §C35 is fixed. ⚠️ **The fix is NOT a blanket clear** — that leaves a registered book scoped to nothing, the exact bug spec §10 exists to prevent. The shape is a guard against removal-while-registered.
    2. **The concept ROW is never reconciled on re-import** (`import-concepts.js`, unchanged from `main`). `domain`, `collection`, `definition_en`, `definition_is` are written once and never updated, so editing `COLLECTION_DOMAIN` and re-running the full import moves nothing — at full reported yield and `VERIFY: PASS`, because `domains-are-known` checks membership only and a stale-but-valid domain is invisible. **Matters because spec §3 decision 5 makes `domain` the precedence key Part B's resolver reads.**
    3. **There is no entry-withdrawal path at all.** An entry that loses its Icelandic side is skipped above the prune; an entry that vanishes from the payload is never reached. Both leave stale terms and their preferences. Not covered by any of the eight original findings.
    4. **An entry with no `id` duplicates its concept on every re-import** — `findConcept` is skipped when `idordabankiId` is null and `concept.idordabanki_id UNIQUE` admits unlimited NULLs. Latent: the measured corpus has 0 nulls and 70,187 distinct ids.
    5. **`server/scripts/export-terminology.js`'s `parseArgs` swallows the next flag as a value** — the same shape B0 fixed in its own two parsers. Measured there: `--db --allow-zero-yield` consumed the flag as a path, lost the flag, and created a 0-byte SQLite file **named after the flag**. ⚠️ `export-terminology.js` is **cron-invoked on production**, so this deserves its own review rather than a drive-by. *[severity: correctness · blocks: Part B]*
  - **🆕 Part B PRECONDITIONS, established by an adversarially-verified sweep before B0 was written. Read these before designing Part B — three correct spec claims and two false ones.**
    - **Spec §7.1's "No new plumbing" is FALSE as a mechanism and accidentally true as a conclusion.** `translateChunk` calls `filterGlossaryForText` with **neither a book slug nor a chapter in scope** (`tools/api-translate.js:885`), and the glossary is a file loaded once at book level (`:634`, `:1130`) — so `resolve(book, chapter, string)` is unreachable from that call site. But if the **export** writes a resolved view, `api-translate.js` needs zero changes. **Build it that way**; it also avoids a new MIT→AGPL edge.
    - **Spec §2.1's consumer claim is REFUTED.** Three files read the old terminology tables outside `terminologyService.js`: `tools/fetch_bin_inflections.py`, `tools/fetch_idordabanki.py`, and **`scripts/verify-db-backup.sh` — a production monthly cron that `exit 1`s when the table is gone**, while its test stays green because it creates its own copy.
    - **The producer gate will NOT notice the export's reshape.** `detectProducer` short-circuits on the top-level stamp *before* reading `payload.terms`, and `exportBookGlossary` emits that stamp unconditionally. ⚠️ **And `glossaryCollisionBaseline.test.js` goes green-but-VACUOUS** under one-entry-per-English-string: `findGlossaryCollisions` needs ≥2 Icelandic values per key, so the `toEqual` passes while its subject matter disappears.
    - **`glossary-unified.json` is not an MT file.** `tools/lib/math-label-substitute.js` loads it, `buildGlossaryMap` drops any term where `status !== 'approved'`, and the result reaches published CNXML via `cnxml-inject.js`. **`concept_term` has no status column.** A resolved export that stops stamping `status: 'approved'` silently puts English into published math.
    - **C24's automaton survives unchanged** — `buildTermAutomaton` takes only `{headwordId, english}` and its cache is fingerprinted on those pairs, so the seam is the row-reconstruction block, not the matcher. ⚠️ **But `findTermsInSegments` takes no `chapter`**, and the old model's fallback tier (a headword whose translations are all foreign-subject still matches, badged `isFallback`) has **no equivalent in `resolve()` as specced** — **16 of the C24 golden's 40 matches are `isFallback: true`.** Decide that before writing `resolve()`.
  - **🆕 MEASURED, and it changes what Part B's step 5 costs.** Spec §2 records "Homographs within one subject: **0** — does not occur in the imported data", measured on the OLD 5-collection corpus. On the 20-collection corpus it is abundant, because **nine collections map onto `biology` alone**. Restricted to English strings that actually appear in a book's `01-source` (complete, per the spec's own trap warning), and counting a tie only in the **best available** domain per §6 step 4: `efnafraedi-2e` **2,001 resolve outright (82.1%) · 126 nominal ties (5.2%) · 310 real ties (12.7%)**; `liffraedi-2e` 4,742 / 604 / 965; `edlisfraedi-2e` 2,572 / 97 / 446. **"Nominal" = two concepts whose rank-1 Icelandic head forms AGREE** — omitting those buys nothing, and §7.1's flat "tied → omit" currently discards them. The real ties cluster (`cns → {miðtaugakerfi | MTK}`, `dna → {DKS | deoxýríbósakjarnsýra}` are abbreviation/expansion pairs; `north pole → {norðurpóll himins | norðurskaut | …}` is a genuine sense split), so they are not 310 independent decisions.
  - **🆕 THE ACCEPTANCE GATE FOR PART B, derived on production rather than quoted.** For `efnafraedi-2e` the export's discard rule keeps **709** chemistry-tagged translations and discards **19,057** carrying `physics` or `biology` — which the fallback rehomes — plus **9,137** carrying `mathematics`, which chemistry's priority list deliberately excludes. `pH` → biology, `bond` → biology, `carbon dioxide` → biology, `nitrogen` → physics, each verified at row level. ⚠️ **The sweep's worst-case worry is FALSIFIED: `general` / `microbiology` / `organic-chemistry` have ZERO rows on production**, and no translation is untagged — all 28,903 carry exactly one of four tags. So nothing in the discard set is left homeless by the seven-domain vocabulary. ⚠️ **Capture this baseline before Part C drops the old tables**, or the gate becomes unmeasurable. ⚠️ **This does NOT contradict §C14 ②'s "408 net / 112 correct chemistry terms" — different units.** §C14 ② diffs a proposed export against the **committed `glossary-unified.json` file**; the numbers here count **translation rows passing `exportBookGlossary`'s discard filter** in the database. Neither is derivable from the other; say which you mean.

Spec risk 3 mitigates LAEKN with "no current book lists `anatomy-physiology` first" — **position 2 still puts all of it in scope, so that mitigation does not hold.** Chemistry is unaffected at 19,749.

    - **🔴 §C38 (NEW, P1) — DOMAIN PRIORITY MAKES ONE COLLECTION AUTHORITATIVE EVEN WHEN THE CORPUS DISAGREES 5-TO-1, AND NO EDITOR PREFERENCE CAN OVERRULE IT. Found by the LEAD on 2026-08-09, from domain knowledge — not by any test, and not by either whole-branch reviewer.** — **[CODE]** — **P1** — *B4 precondition and an adoption input; not a B3 defect: the resolver did exactly what D1–D5 specify.*
      - **The case.** *Accuracy* (closeness to the true value) and *precision* (repeatability) are the pair a measurements chapter exists to distinguish. Chemistry's export resolves **both to `nákvæmni`**. Measured on the corpus, six concepts carry the English `accuracy` and **five give `hittni` as the head form** (biology ×2, mathematics ×2, plus one biology concept ranking `nákvæmni` 1 / `hittni` 2); the sixth — **physics/EDLISFR — says `nákvæmni`**. Chemistry's chain is `1.chemistry → 2.physics → 3.biology`, chemistry has no `accuracy` concept at all, so **physics@2 decides and biology's `hittni` at position 3 never gets consulted**. `precision` resolves to `nákvæmni` from the same collection, correctly. The two collapse because one domain decides both.
      - **⚠️ THREE THINGS MAKE IT INVISIBLE, and they matter more than the single term.** ① **`alternatives` is EMPTY** — it is built from the *winning concept's* other Icelandic terms, and physics concept 2500 has only `nákvæmni`, so `hittni` appears nowhere in the exported entry. ② **The losing IN-SCOPE answers are not reported at all** — `resolve()` surfaces `outOfScope` (here: the two mathematics `hittni` concepts, badged) but a lower-position in-scope concept that loses the race vanishes silently. ③ **Árnastofnun's own data conflates them**: concept 6524 carries BOTH English terms on ONE concept, `nákvæmni` rank 1 / `hittni` rank 2.
      - **⚠️ AND `book_concept_preference` CANNOT FIX IT — measured, not argued.** Seeding a book-level preference for the biology concept whose only term is `hittni` and re-resolving still returns **`nákvæmni [physics @2]`**. The preference model chooses a **term within a concept**; **position still decides which concept wins**, so a preference on a position-3 concept can never beat position 2. **The model as built gives an editor no way to say "for this book, `accuracy` means `hittni`."** That is the gap B4 has to close, and it is not a UI question.
      - **Why no check caught it.** Every layer behaved correctly: the resolver followed D1–D5, the tests pinned what they name, both blind reviewers measured the gates and the payload shape. **A wrong-but-well-formed translation is invisible to all of it** — the defect is in the relationship between two entries, and only someone who knows the domain can see it. → [[engineering-lessons]]; same shape as the census-noise finding (`at → marsnákaætt`), which is also a correctness question no gate asks.
      - **▶ Consequences to carry:** ① **B4 must make a losing in-scope concept visible** (this is *adjacent to* the deferred `integrity`-codes precondition below, and is a distinct requirement — one is about a dangling preference, this is about a concept that was never offered). ② **Adoption input:** chemistry's 22 changed terms were reviewed as words; **this one needed reviewing as a PAIR**, and the evidence document says so only because the lead raised it. Whoever takes §C14 ②'s chemistry decision should read the 22 for *relationships*, not one row at a time. ③ **Do NOT "fix" this by reordering chemistry's domain priority** — physics@2 is right for most of the corpus; the fault is that priority is the *only* mechanism.
    - **Out of scope, logged here:** `run-concept-import.js`'s `parseImportArgs` has the same `-h`-swallowing gap B3 fixed in the exporter (⚠️ it is **not** cron-invoked — prod's crontab has three entries and this is not one); `detectProducer` trusts a top-level stamp over a contradictory term shape (inherited from `PRODUCER_EXPORT`, now pinned by test rather than changed); the resolver's `integrity` codes are discarded by the builder, so an `orphan-preference` degrades silently — **a B4 precondition**; the MT-priming half of the 205-term dropout is unbounded (the render half is measured, and current published exposure is **0**).

Its preconditions, in one place:** ① **the replay source is `~/idordabanki-raw-2026-08-07/`, OUTSIDE the repo** (20 collections; a re-fetch costs ≈1.5 h at the mandated 1 req/s, so treat that directory as the asset). ② **✅ CORRECTED 2026-08-08 — THIS PRECONDITION IS ALREADY DISCHARGED, and it was discharged before it was written here.** It restated Part A's finding 1 from the finding list rather than from the tree, hours after B0 closed it. **Measured in `main`, not argued:** `server/scripts/import-concepts.js` upserts terms on the natural key (`ON CONFLICT(concept_id, lang, text) DO UPDATE`), so a term still present upstream **keeps its id** and no cascade fires; a term genuinely withdrawn upstream has its preferences deleted **explicitly, not by cascade** — deliberately, because a cascade yields no count for `preferencesDropped` and because the behaviour must not depend on `PRAGMA foreign_keys`, which is per-connection and not stored in the file. Pinned by `server/__tests__/importConcepts.test.js`'s *"re-import keeps editor preferences intact"*, parameterised over `foreign_keys` **default / ON / OFF**. Corpus-scale evidence is already in this file's B0 block: `max(concept_term.id)` **192,189 → 384,378** pre-fix, **192,189 → 192,189** post-fix. ⚠️ **What is NOT closed is a DIFFERENT failure, and it is the opposite one** — B0's deferred finding 3: an entry that vanishes from the payload, or loses its Icelandic side, is never reached by the prune, so its terms and preferences go **stale rather than deleted**. That is scoped to Part B and does **not** gate B2. *(Former text, retained because the error is the instructive part — a precondition re-derived from a finding list instead of from the code that had already fixed it, in the file that owns open-work status: "**Settle the re-import hazard FIRST, not after:** re-importing a collection cascade-deletes every editor term preference, and B2 is precisely the step that starts creating them — it is inert today only because none exist.")* ③ The import is runnable and reproducible from the repo (B0 bought that); the same run reproduces its per-collection yield exactly. ④ **B3, not B2, is what unblocks chemistry** — the export cut-over is where reader-facing behaviour changes. ⚠️ **Always write `§C36 B2`, never a bare "B2"** — `[[b2-isapitranslated-routing]]` in project memory is an unrelated, already-shipped item of the same name.
  - **The slice, and why.** Measured on disk 2026-08-08: **nine non-doc files read the old terminology tables** — `terminologyService.js`, `export-terminology.js`, `capture-c24-golden.js`, `e2e/seed-fixture.js`, migrations 032/033/044/046, **`scripts/verify-db-backup.sh` (a production monthly cron that `exit 1`s when the table is gone)**, and the two Python tools. Parent spec §9's single step ("rewrite `terminologyService` around `resolve`; consumers updated") is not one reviewable PR. **B1** `resolve()`+`buildScope()`, inert · **B2** populate prod (import replay, a data op) · **B3** export cut-over — **unblocks chemistry** · **B4** editor · **B5** stragglers · **Part C** drops the old tables.
  - **⚠️ B0's deferred finding 5 is REASSIGNED — "blocks Part B" → "B3's precondition".** `export-terminology.js`'s `parseArgs` swallowing the next flag is in the **export**, which B1 does not touch. It still deserves its own review, not a drive-by: the script is cron-invoked on production.
  - **D1 — out-of-scope concepts survive as a soft, badged tier.** Parent spec §6 step 2 drops any candidate whose domain is absent from the book's priority list. **Measured, that deletes behaviour the editor has today**: `terminologyService.js:1489-1498` badges out-of-subject terms `isFallback: true` and sorts them last, `:1625` excludes them from missing-term issues, `term-highlight.js:73` renders a `cross-subject` class and `segment-editor.js:2486` a note — surfaced but never enforced. **16 of the C24 golden's 40 matches carry the flag** (measured from `c24-golden.json`, not quoted). ⚠️ **That 16 is NOT a prediction of loss** — the golden was captured when a book had ONE subject; chemistry now has three domains in scope, so some become in-scope. What is at stake is only domains in *no* position for the book — for chemistry that includes `anatomy-physiology`, 46% of the corpus.
  - **D2 — a nominal tie resolves, and is still reported.** Parent spec §7.1's flat "tied → omit" conflates two populations. A **nominal** tie is two concepts at one position whose rank-1 Icelandic head forms are **the identical string** — nothing to guess, both answer with the same word; chemistry has **126** of them. A **real** tie is `antibiotic` → *fúkalyf*/*sýklalyf*. `resolve()` returns the agreed form AND reports the tie so an editor can merge; it merges nothing itself, so parent decision 1 is intact.
  - **D3 — an unscoped book gets an explicit state, and it NAMES WHICH FAULT.** `{ unscoped: 'unregistered' }` (no `registered_books` row — §C35; remedy is the admin route) vs `{ unscoped: 'no-priorities' }` (registered, absent from migration 046's frozen map; remedy is a migration). ⚠️ **A single `unscoped: true` was the first draft and was corrected in review** — collapsing two faults with different remedies is precisely the failure D3 exists to prevent, applied to everything except itself. On a fresh clone **4 of 6 books** are unscoped.
  - **🆕 A REAL ORDERING DEFECT IN THE PARENT SPEC, found while designing.** §6 runs *choose each candidate's term (3) → lowest position wins (4)*. A concept with an `en` term and **no** `is` term therefore **wins the position race and then resolves to nothing** — chemistry is position 1 for `efnafraedi-2e`, so a term-less chemistry concept would beat `biology`'s perfectly good word at position 3, which is never consulted. **Filtering must happen BETWEEN steps 3 and 4.** The numbered list cannot show this; it appears only when you ask what an empty term does to the ordering. **B1 gate 5 counts the triggering population rather than assuming it** (B0 finding 4 is the model) — if it is non-zero, gates 1 and 2 are not independent of it.
  - **The acceptance gate is measured on the real corpus, in a scratch DB — not on fixtures.** `run-concept-import.js` over `~/idordabanki-raw-2026-08-07/`: 70,187 concepts / 192,189 terms in 3.8 s. ⚠️ **Gate 2 must reproduce the census METHOD, not only its numbers** (2,001/126/310) — strings restricted to the book's own `01-source`, and a tie counted only in the best available domain. Quoting the numbers without the method makes the first run differ *for methodology reasons*. ⚠️ **`bench-c24.js` cannot be reused for the perf gate** — it takes `<book> <chapter> <moduleId>` and calls `findTermsInSegments`, the matcher B1 does not touch; B1 adds its own bench in that script's output shape (latency **and** RSS). *[severity: design · blocks: B2–B5 and Part C]*
    - **✅ MEASURED AND CLOSED 2026-08-08 (B1 shipped). Two corrections to the line above, both load-bearing.** *(1)* **The census source is `02-for-mt`, not `01-source`** — ruled deliberately: `02-for-mt` holds the extracted EN segments the resolver actually runs over, while `01-source` is raw CNXML (149 `.cnxml` against 249 `.md`). Recorded as an amendment in the B1 spec §8.2, because the first run "fixed" this by relabelling the script and filed a spec deviation as a correction. *(2)* **The census REPRODUCES: 1,999 outright / 120 nominal / 299 real, against the recorded 2,001/126/310 — within ~1%.** The first measurement came in 30–46% low (1,398/67/176) and was written up as a register discrepancy; the cause was **our own extractor**, whose non-overlapping two-word regex made a term's visibility depend on its byte offset. **§C36's census is reproducible after all** — the earlier note that it "is not reproducible without its method" was true only of the method-as-then-implemented. ⚠️ **The tokenisation is part of the method**: record it alongside the source directory, or the next run diverges the same way. Evidence, with both numbers and the single-variable comparison: [test-results/b1-resolve-gates-2026-08.md](../../test-results/b1-resolve-gates-2026-08.md).

## P2 — Hardening backlog · **[CODE]**

- **C37 · ✅ FIXED 2026-08-08 — `main` was RED on the `audit` gate, and no commit caused it** — **[CODE]** — **P2** — *logged 2026-08-08 while checking PR #372's checks; the failure was on `main` too and predated that branch.* **Closed by `main` `664ec099`: `nanoid` 3.3.16 → 3.3.18. `main`'s CI is green — `Security Audit`, `Lint` and `Tests` all pass on that commit.**
  - **⚠️ THE PRESCRIBED FIX BELOW WAS NOT THE ONE USED, AND THE PRESCRIPTION WAS WRONG — read this before reaching for `overrides` on the next advisory.** The entry said "the fix is an `overrides` entry … add `"nanoid": ">=3.3.17"`". **No override was needed.** `postcss` requires `nanoid@^3.3.16`, and that caret range **already permits** 3.3.17 and 3.3.18 — nothing was pinning us to the vulnerable version except the lockfile's own recorded resolution, which `npm install` will not move. **`npm update nanoid` re-resolves within the range that was always allowed**, and the whole change is three lines (version, resolved, integrity). Adding an override would have created a permanent pin to maintain — and this entry's own next bullet warns that a pin can become the vulnerability. **Check whether the existing semver range already admits the fix before writing an override; here it did.**
  - **⚠️ AND THE DIAGNOSIS WAS DELAYED BY A CHECK THAT LIED IN THE REASSURING DIRECTION.** `npm ls nanoid` reported **3.3.18** — *newer* than the advisory's `<3.3.17`, i.e. safe — while `npm audit` reported the vulnerability. Both were right about different things: `node_modules` on disk had drifted to 3.3.18, and `package-lock.json` still pinned 3.3.16. **CI runs `npm ci`, so CI sees the lockfile — the only copy that matters.** Reading `npm ls` as the answer would have concluded the red was spurious. → the standing lesson in [[engineering-lessons]] about the right property and the wrong instrument.
  - **Verified against what `security.yml` ACTUALLY runs, which is four steps over BOTH trees**, not the one the failing log named: `npm ci` (root) · `npm ci` (server) · `npm audit --audit-level=high` (root) · same (server). All four green, reproduced in scratch directories holding only the two manifests — which is the `npm ci` path exactly, without a 1.9 GB checkout. The root tree now reports **zero vulnerabilities at any severity**, so nothing is queued below `high`; `server/`'s SheetJS CDN tarball still installs.
  - *Historical record of the entry as logged (its diagnosis of HOW the gate flipped stands; its prescription does not):* `npm audit --audit-level=high` on the **root** tree reports **1 high**: `nanoid <3.3.17` (GHSA-2v37-7h3g-55p8), reached by `vitest → vite → postcss (overridden) → nanoid@3.3.16`.
  - **⚠️ The interesting part is HOW it went red.** Last green run was `c656a0e0` (2026-08-07 17:14); first failure `07dfb133` (2026-08-08 10:25). **Every commit in that window was `docs(…)`** — a new advisory was published against an already-installed version, so the gate flipped with nothing in the repo changing. **"Duration is the diagnostic" does not cover this case**: the run is real (52 s), the result is genuine, and no diff explains it. A red `audit` that nobody introduced is the easiest kind to start ignoring.
  - **No production exposure — measured, not assumed.** `npm ls nanoid` in **`server/`** (the prod, internet-facing tree) returns **empty**; it exists only in the root tree via `vitest`, a devDependency.
  - **The fix is an `overrides` entry, per CLAUDE.md — NOT `npm audit fix`.** Root `package.json` already overrides `postcss` at `">=8.5.18"`, which resolves to 8.5.23 and still carries the vulnerable `nanoid`. ⚠️ **This is the recorded hazard in its exact form: an `overrides` pin can BECOME the vulnerability** — the `postcss` pin is doing its job and the transitive dependency underneath it moved. Add `"nanoid": ">=3.3.17"` and re-derive with `npm ls nanoid` **in both trees**. ⚠️ Regenerating `package-lock.json` requires `nvm use` first (npm 10, not 11) — see the Node-24 note in CLAUDE.md. *[severity: hygiene/gate-erosion · blocks: nothing · no prod exposure]*

- **C5 · Authz batch 2** — the residual cross-book authz holes Unit-0 book-scoping never adopted; read-only leaks + one write-adjacent gap. Fails safe on writes (reads leak info, not mutation):
  - **B1-F5** + **I12-R1** — read-only cross-book leaks: `admin.js` `GET /assignments/:book` (~`:986`) exposes another book's assignments/editor-names/progress; `GET /api/pipeline/jobs` + `/jobs/:jobId` expose every book's jobs. **I12-R1 is tracked as [issue #328](https://github.com/SigurdurVilhelmsson/namsbokasafn-efni/issues/328); INVESTIGATED 2026-07-25 (findings in the issue comment) — every claim verified against `main`, no line drift. ⚠️ The fix shape changed: `GET /jobs` has ZERO callers (all four client sites use `/jobs/:jobId`), so DELETE it rather than scope it** — the `apply-all` precedent; that removes the enumerate-every-book half outright instead of leaving a maintained dead endpoint. Then scope `/jobs/:jobId` on `job.book` vs `req.user.books` (mirror `requireRole.js:135`), **keep the ADMIN bypass** (`:119-121`), return **404 not 403**. Also noted: `req.query.book` is caller-controlled (targetable, not just default-open); jobs additionally leak `userId`; jobs are **in-memory only** (`pipelineService.js:38`) so exposure dies at restart. No test covers this — `§4c` (`a4-coverage.spec.js:166`) checks UI panel visibility by role, not API book-scope. **[LEAD 2026-07-25] queued for after C1d.**
  - **B1-F2** — `status` route non-elevated transitions are un-book-scoped: an editor of book A can drive book B's section through editor-level transitions; adopt the established `requireBookAccessForSection` pattern.
  - **B4-F1** — JWT lifetime vs DB user state: `findByProviderId` ignores `is_active` (a deactivated editor's ≤24h JWT still passes) and hard-delete ≠ revocation; consult `is_active` on the auth path.
  - **🆕 `GET /api/books/:bookId/download` is `requireAuth` ONLY — no book scoping** — *logged 2026-08-06 while re-confirming §C20; pre-existing, out of scope for that fix.* Any authenticated editor can download **any** registered book's content in every track (`en-md`, `is-md`, `faithful`, and all three publication tracks). The neighbouring `GET /:book/chapters/:chapter/faithful-count` in the **same file** carries `requireBookAccess()`, so the pattern is established and the omission is local, not architectural. `VALID_BOOKS` bounds `bookId` to registered books, so this is a **cross-book read leak, not a write hole** — the same class as B1-F5/I12-R1, and it fails safe in the same direction. ⚠️ **Do not fold this into the §C20 PR** — C20's harness is about stream failure, and mixing an authz change into it muddies both reviews.
  - **B1-F1** *[decision-gated]* — keep-vs-retire the localization-suggestions family (lead product call); if kept, ship the one-line `requireHeadEditor('bookSlug')` on `POST /scan-book/:bookSlug` + the fail-open guard. Blocks I16-R1 (dead-endpoint disposition). *[severity: hardening · blocks: none (info-leak, contained)]*
- **C6 · MT-acceptance supersede edges** — item-20b acceptance-gate follow-ups, all fail safe:
  - **MTA-R8** — `returnEditToPending` ("Opna aftur") + `unapproveEdit` don't call `acceptanceService.supersedeForEdit` (unlike `saveSegmentEdit` at `segmentEditorService.js:134/:171`), so reopening a rejected-edit segment leaves a pending acceptance.
  - **MTA-R9** — the `DISCUSS_OPEN` acceptance guard matches ANY discuss row with no recency scoping, while every clearing affordance is bound to `latestEdit` → a stranded cross-editor discuss row blocks acceptance (over-refuses). Scope via `pickLatest` or add a resolve affordance.
  - **MTA-R13** — `acceptSegment` does NOT regenerate the `-review-status.json` sidecar (though `revokeAcceptance` does), and `supersedeForEdit`'s call sites (`segmentEditorService.js:134/:171`, `propagationService.js:135`) don't either → corpus `reviewStatus` staleness (both directions under-claim). *[severity: hardening · blocks: research-corpus accuracy (fails safe)]*
- **C7 · Terminology governance** — three governance gaps, two decision-gated:
  - **I19-R1** — `translate-chapter-titles.js:118/:124` primes chapter-title MT with `approvedOnly:false` — the one consumer leaking NON-approved glossary terms to Málstaður; flip to approved-only or bless deliberately.
  - **I19-R3** *[decision-gated]* — terminology queue/mining endpoints are role-gated but NOT book-scoped (an HE of book A can act on book B's terms); deliberate posture today (glossary is subject-oriented) — decide with the lead.
  - **I19-R4** *[decision-gated]* — `PUT /translations/:id` lets any EDITOR rewrite an APPROVED translation's Icelandic text/subjects with no status reset — undermines queue trust; decide reset-on-edit vs gate. *[severity: hardening/correctness · blocks: MT-priming quality]*
- **C8 · Pipeline robustness** — assorted producer/preview hardening:
  - **I21-R2** — TM auto-regen now needs a `book-licences.cjs` (→ item-17 `book-config.json`) licence row: a valid book with faithful content but no licence entry fails regen — LOUD 500 on `/api/tm/export` but SILENT warn-only stale TM on the cron. Onboard-books-licence-first is the rule; consider a loud cron guard. *[blocks: a new book's TM durability]*
  - **GATE-1** — `verify-extraction-coverage` reports `modulesMissingSource:21` (a source-matching gap in the coverage assertion; the dup-seg-id scan is sound regardless). Weakens the pre-freeze gate for those modules. *[blocks: biology-onboarding gate confidence]*
  - **osd-1** — server live preview never rebinds `BOOKS_DIR` per book → preview-time os-embed resolution is chemistry-pinned (wrong for organic); plumb an `options` param like the D4 `embedMap` fix (#192).
  - **REEQ-1** — `normalizeVisibleText` nested-bracket term false-flags `m68727`/`m68747`, blocking clean verify-reextract-equivalence runs on chemistry (waivable → fails safe). *[severity: hardening]*

## LEAD operational lane (parallel — unblock whenever convenient)

Non-coding, LEAD-executed; sequenced against P0 above.

- **✅ A2 · Off-box DB backup ACTIVATED AND RESTORE-TESTED 2026-08-04.** Bucket `openstax-backup` in **gb-lon-1 (London)**; the server is **de-fra-2 (Frankfurt)** — different region, as the item asked. rclone v1.75.0 at `/usr/local/bin` (checksum-verified download, not `curl | sudo bash`), s3 remote `linode` (`provider=Linode`, `acl=private`) wrapped by crypt remote `secret`; `BACKUP_REMOTE=secret:` on the 6-hourly cron plus a **new monthly `verify-db-backup.sh` run**. **Measured, not assumed:** objects are encrypted-named in the bucket and plaintext `sessions.*` through crypt (so the retention/verify `grep '^sessions\.'` matches) · `verify-db-backup.sh` → **`RESTORE VERIFY: PASS`** (integrity_check ok; 28,903 `terminology_translations`, 160 `segment_edits`, 83 `content_versions`) · remote prune forced with `BACKUP_REMOTE_KEEP=2` rather than waiting ~7.5 days for it to fire on its own · the cron invocation re-run under **`env -i` with only cron's environment** (exit 0). `/api/health` now reports `offbox_backup.ok: true`; **overall status is still `degraded`, solely from `glossary_export`** (C14, deliberately contained) — this item no longer contributes.
  - **⬜ ONE THING REMAINS UNCONFIRMED — the first *unattended* run.** Every verification above was triggered by hand or simulated (`env -i` with only cron's environment). The first real cron firing is **00:30 UTC 2026-08-05**. Confirm with `curl -s http://localhost:3000/api/health` → `checks.offbox_backup.age_hours` ≤ 6, or on the box `ls -l pipeline-output/backups/.last-offbox-backup`. ⚠️ **This is precisely the gap §C11(b) fell into** — that detector shipped 2026-07-27, did not actually run until 2026-08-03, and nobody noticed for 13 days. *A mechanism that has shipped is not a mechanism that has run.* **Nothing polls `/api/health`**, so if this firing fails silently the next signal is whenever someone happens to deploy.
  - ⚠️ **The runbook itself carried two live defects, both fixed here.** (a) It and `install-cron.sh` printed `BACKUP_REMOTE=secret:namsbokasafn-db`, which `backup-db.sh` rejects with `exit 5` — following the documented procedure literally produced a backup that could never succeed. (b) The crontab carried **no `PATH`**, and rclone installs to `/usr/local/bin` while a user crontab gets `/usr/bin:/bin` — so every cron run would have died at `exit 3` while an interactive test by hand passed, with no `MAILTO` and nothing polling `/api/health`. Both are now pinned by `scripts/__tests__/backup-db.test.mjs`, which feeds `install-cron.sh`'s **printed** value to the real guard rather than re-checking the rule.
  - ⚠️ **Scope of what this protects:** `sessions.db` only. `books/` content still reaches off-box storage solely through `git-backup.sh` — §C3/§C11(d) are unaffected. And the access key needs delete rights for retention, so a **compromised** box holds both the passphrase and the ability to wipe the bucket; this defends against losing the box, not against an intruder on it. Bucket versioning would close that — **not done, deliberately out of scope.**
  - **⬜ Follow-up logged 2026-08-04, deliberately NOT fixed — `scripts/deploy.sh` takes its pre-deploy DB backup without `BACKUP_REMOTE`, so that snapshot is local-only** (visible in the deploy readout as `BACKUP_REMOTE not set — skipping off-box upload`). **Defensible as it stands:** that backup exists to undo a *bad deploy*, and a local copy is what you would actually restore from — instant, no network, no passphrase, no rclone. The off-box copy answers a different threat (losing the box), which a deploy does not meaningfully make likelier. **The residual is narrow and worth stating exactly:** at the moment of a deploy the newest *off-box* copy may be up to 6 h old, so a deploy coinciding with instance loss costs up to 6 h of editorial work rather than 0. ⚠️ **Do not "just add the env var".** `BACKUP_REMOTE=${BACKUP_REMOTE:-secret:}` would put the remote name in a **second** place — today the live value exists only in the crontab. If this is ever taken up, give the remote name **one owner** that both cron and `deploy.sh` read; duplicating the default is how the `secret:namsbokasafn-db` defect this item just fixed came to survive in two files at once.
  - Runbook `docs/technical/backup-and-restore.md`. Registers L1 / PROV-2 #262.
- **A4 · Manual QA §0–§5 walk** — combined efni+vefur pass: authz boundaries, on-disk render rollback, restore round-trip, enforcement 403/503, stored-XSS rendering, page-auth redirects, CSRF posture + 3 prod-only cases. **Coordinate with P0-1** (don't deploy server-touching units mid-QA). ## Provenance & sizing

- **Inventory basis:** a 6-agent extraction sweep (2026-07-21) over the pre-semester campaign register + the status dashboard + the SDD ledger + CLAUDE.md + memory, then controller dedup/consolidation. Raw 189 open findings → consolidated here; two already-resolved false-positives (B4-D11, I18-R1) excluded.
  - **⚠️ Known defect in that basis, found 2026-07-26 — every source listed above is a *derivative*.** The `docs/audit/` reports the campaigns were created to work down are **not** in the list, so this register tracks findings discovered *during* items but never the findings the work was chartered to fix. Measured consequence: `grep -c '2026-07-11-server-code-review'` over the predecessor campaign = **0**; `grep -oE 'Batch [A-H]'` = **1 of 8**. **8 review findings were carried by nothing** until the closure audit recovered them (now **C10**). **Rule for the next inventory sweep: include the primary audit documents, and disposition their findings by id — a batch-level "shipped" is not a finding-level disposition.**
- **Critical path:** P0 is the spine — until P0-1 (deploy) + P0-2 (vefur sync) run, the 21 shipped items and all content fixes are invisible to their users. The **[CODE]** backlog (P1–P3) does not gate delivery and can proceed in parallel with the LEAD lane, subject to the mid-QA deploy caveat.
- **First codeable pickups (recommended order):** C1 appendices (biggest coherent value, unblocks appendix editorial) → C2 Playwright-red (restores E2E trust) → C3 corpus/TM durability (cheap, closes a real durability lie) → then P2 batches by decision-readiness.
